Abstract (英文): Feed-forward 4D trackers recover camera motion, depth, and point trajectories from short video clips, but chaining windows over long videos degrades their accuracy. We find that replacing inter-window alignment with ground-truth transforms leaves tracking error essentially unchanged. Static-point error instead grows with time since last observation: repeatedly predicting a static point from windows that no longer observe it corrupts previously recovered geometry. We call this failure forgetting. Merely retaining the last observed positions of unseen points reduces error by 26% on PointOdyssey without modifying the tracker. We introduce Recall4D, a training-free framework that augments a frozen tracker with a persistent world state and sparse motion anchors. Static points retain their world-space positions outside the camera view and are refined by averaging observations decoded at sparse keyframes while in view. For dynamic objects, the tracker decodes sparse representative anchors, while geometric skinning propagates their displacements to dense queries. Across two long-horizon benchmarks, Recall4D improves the accuracy of its backbone while running 7.6-9.0$\times$ faster, and its accuracy gains carry over to five further backbones and three other long-sequence tasks, demonstrating its generality.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Deep learning models for diabetic retinopathy (DR) grading often generalize poorly to unseen imaging domains, in part because predictions may exploit correlations specific to source datasets rather than retinal pathology. We address this challenge by constructing grading decisions from lesion evidence learned using only image severity labels. Clinical implications of DR grades provide multiple instance constraints that guide competitive learning of lesion and background prototypes at multiple scales. An additive ordinal scoring model then predicts severity exclusively from individual lesion prototype responses, explicitly connecting learned evidence with grading decisions without a global feature classification pathway. To improve the reliability of this evidence, we introduce teacher guided preservation under image degradations and spatial regularization against persistent coordinate preferences. Across six public DR datasets under a leave one domain out protocol, our method attains a mean quadratic weighted kappa of 0.761, outperforming the strongest competing method by 0.036. Localization and prototype removal analyses show that the learned responses align with retinal lesions and contribute to grading decisions. Experiments across five other prototype architectures and under image degradations further demonstrate the generality and robustness of the approach.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Synthetic aperture radar (SAR) image registration under varying acquisition geometries is challenging because orbit direction, look side, and incidence angle affect backscatter, terrain-induced distortion, and feature visibility. However, large-scale geometry-aware benchmarks remain scarce. This paper presents MOVR-SAR, a multi-resolution dataset of 450,845 registered and quality-screened tile pairs derived from 2774 scene pairs from Capella, Umbra, Sentinel-1, and NISAR. It covers X, C, and L bands, ground sampling distances of 0.25-20 m, and diverse regions and land-cover types. A unified framework defines four imaging-geometry pair categories from orbit direction and look side, plus viewing-angle-separation labels for Capella and Umbra, enabling stratified evaluation under same- and opposite-direction illumination. Systematic evaluation of representative matching methods shows that official and cross-modal pretrained weights perform poorly on high-resolution X-band imagery. Fine-tuning on MOVR-SAR improves accuracy and reduces disparities across platforms, imaging-geometry pair categories, and viewing-angle separations. For example, fine-tuning increases FlexGlue AUC@5 from 3.06% to 89.79% on Capella and from 1.79% to 89.03% on Umbra, reaching 93.41% and 94.77% on Sentinel-1 and NISAR, respectively. Fine-tuning substantially reduces the official-weight same-orbit, same-look-side advantage and reverses the three-method average category ranking on Capella, suggesting that this bias reflects training-data coverage rather than an invariant limitation of cross-geometry matching. Land-cover effects remain platform- and method-dependent. MOVR-SAR provides a training resource and geometry-aware benchmark for robust SAR-SAR registration. The dataset and model weights will be released at https://github.com/fdhzhz5870/MOVR-SAR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): UAV detection and tracking remain constrained by the scarcity of high-quality training data. Existing game-engine-based synthetic UAV data suffer from a substantial synthetic-to-real gap, while the high cost of manually collected real-world UAV data limits dataset scale. Therefore, we introduce WorldUAV, a high-quality UAV dataset constructed through an efficient framework powered by world models. Specifically, WorldUAV directly integrates UAVs into readily available real-world videos, naturally preserving realistic backgrounds, illumination, and scene dynamics. Leveraging the spatiotemporal priors of world models, it generates UAV videos with plausible motion, pose variations, and cross-frame consistency, effectively narrowing the gap between synthetic and real data. We further develop an agentic pipeline orchestrated by a multimodal large language model that acquires sources, plans UAV trajectories, invokes synthesis, and verifies generated videos, enabling fully automated dataset construction. WorldUAV comprises 6,878 UAV videos and 205,778 UAV images. Experiments show that WorldUAV significantly improves the performance of UAV detectors. Moreover, it substantially reduces dataset construction costs, requiring only 15 seconds and USD 0.0005 API cost per high-quality UAV image. Our work offers both a high-quality dataset and a scalable data generation paradigm for UAV detection and tracking.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal memory enables long-horizon vision-language agents to preserve and retrieve past experiences. While prior research has focused on memory construction and retrieval, effectively using the provided memory remains a challenge for vision-language models (VLMs). We find that VLMs often fail to answer downstream questions even when sufficient multimodal evidence is provided. To investigate why VLMs struggle, we replace memory images with reference captions and observe substantial performance gains, suggesting difficulties in reading visual evidence directly. However, model-generated captions may omit critical information, limiting this approach in practice. These limitations motivate a closer examination of memory utilization, revealing failures in both evidence reading and downstream question answering. Building on this distinction, we propose \textbf{MemRU}, a two-stage training framework that improves evidence reading through supervised fine-tuning (Read SFT) and downstream evidence utilization through Group Relative Policy Optimization (Use GRPO). Experimental results demonstrate that our 8B model generalizes across three unseen multimodal memory systems, outperforming Qwen3-VL-32B and approaching GPT-5-mini.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 4D occupancy forecasting models 3D scene evolution to support future prediction and scenario generation in autonomous-driving world models. Semantic occupancy abstracts image color and texture into discrete labels, leaving 3D voxel arrangements as the primary cues for distinguishing local shapes within each class. Existing methods borrow image-encoding frameworks to obtain compact latent representations through height channelization or plane factorization, but do not sufficiently model within-column height patterns and cross-column relations jointly before compression, weakening these geometric cues. Forecasting also underutilizes complementary history and struggles to retain reliable observation evidence. We therefore propose GeoTex, an occupancy world model combining geometry-as-texture encoding with history-grounded forecasting. GeoTex Representation jointly encodes within-column height patterns and cross-column relations into compact continuous latent representations and restores 3D structure through content-dependent height unfolding and geometric interaction. GeoTex Prediction retains initial evidence throughout rollout in fixed cross-observation relational memory and incorporates complementary recent history through target-referenced residual updates. After prediction training, decoder-only adaptation improves semantic decoding of predicted states. GeoTex improves average forecasting mIoU and IoU by 9.79% and 11.91% relative to GenieDrive while reducing parameters from 3.47M to 2.33M, with gains increasing at longer horizons. Code is included in the supplementary material.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) optimization updates the Gaussian parameters one training view at a time, tens of thousands of times. Unlike such single-view updates, multi-view batches reduce dependence on individual training views, and second-order optimization reduces the number of updates with larger, structured parameter changes. Because fewer updates leave fewer chances to correct a change that fits only the views used in one update, scene coverage becomes more important for each second-order update. Multi-view second-order updates, however, are expensive. Pixel sampling reduces this cost, but two issues remain. First, selecting views without considering overlap leaves some views redundant. To address this, we propose a view selection strategy that reduces this redundancy. Second, existing work processes the sampled pixels of each view in a separate pass and recomputes alpha compositing for these pixels from scratch. We introduce two implementation strategies that reduce both overheads by processing the sampled pixels of multiple views in joint passes and reusing the transmittance and accumulated color cached during rendering. On three datasets, our method reaches higher PSNR than the sampled second-order baseline in less than half its optimization time, with competitive SSIM and LPIPS.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Automated currency denomination recognition is a computer vision task with applications in cash handling, financial technology, and assistive systems. This study classifies seven Nepali currency denominations (Rs. 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 500, and 1,000) using three configurations: a conventional convolutional neural network (CNN), MobileNetV2 with transfer learning, and MobileNetV2 with hyperparameter tuning. A compiled dataset of 16,249 images was used, comprising 10,500 training, 3,500 validation, and 2,249 independent test images. Images were resized to 128 x 128 pixels, normalized, and augmented to vary orientation, scale, illumination, and appearance. Models were evaluated using accuracy, precision, recall, F1-score, and precision-recall and ROC analysis. The hyperparameter-tuned MobileNetV2 achieved the highest validation accuracy (95\%), with precision, recall, and F1-score of 0.93, 0.91, and 0.92, but reached only 84\% accuracy on the held-out test set. MobileNetV2 with transfer learning but no additional tuning achieved 89\% test accuracy, and the conventional CNN achieved 85\%. Transfer learning therefore provided a competitive approach under the evaluated conditions, while higher validation accuracy from tuning did not yield higher test accuracy. To demonstrate practical deployment, a Flask-based web prototype using TensorFlow Lite was developed, supporting image upload and live webcam recognition with both visual and voice output. The study shows the feasibility of combining lightweight deep learning with a voice-assisted web interface for Nepali currency recognition, and highlights the need for more diverse datasets, greater robustness under uncontrolled imaging conditions, and broader real-world evaluation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visual language models (VLMs) have demonstrated exceptional capabilities across various multimodal tasks, but they remain susceptible to hallucinations, undermining their reliability. Existing hallucination benchmarks primarily focus on identifying hallucinations and describing their manifestations, often neglecting their underlying causes. Specifically, multimodal hallucinations can stem from two distinct failure modes: text modality bias (TMB), reflecting an over-reliance on misleading text input; and language prior (LP), reflecting a reliance on previously learned knowledge. To differentiate these failure modes, we propose VTK-Bench, a benchmark that explicitly separates three information sources: visual information (V), textual information (T), and prior knowledge (K). By establishing mutually exclusive answers corresponding to V, T, and K, VTK-Bench enables fine-grained attribution analysis of model responses. Furthermore, we introduce discrete attribution and soft attribution metrics to characterize information source preferences. Extensive experiments on 11 representative open-source VLMs show that LP is more prevalent than TMB in most evaluated models. Notably, TMB decreases substantially with model scaling, whereas LP exhibits relatively limited improvement and remains the dominant failure mode. These findings highlight the importance of different information source preferences for a deeper understanding of multimodal hallucinations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent advances in world models for game interaction and control have increased the demand for large-scale gameplay videos paired with camera trajectories. However, capturing synchronized camera trajectories often requires game-specific pipelines, limiting scalability. We therefore propose GameWeaver, a unified diffusion framework that automatically produces paired video--camera data through three complementary tasks: camera pose estimation from observed video, video generation along a prescribed camera trajectory, and joint video--camera generation from a reference image and text. First, each tipxel stores the normalized 3D coordinates of a pixel ray's endpoint in a reference coordinate frame. The resulting three-channel tipxel fields and RGB frames share the pretrained video model's VAE. Next, a fully trainable dual-stream Transformer exchanges appearance and geometry features through bidirectional gated attention and conditions both streams on shared text features. Finally, deterministic fitting recovers camera intrinsics and normalized trajectories from generated tipxel fields. In our evaluation tasks, GameWeaver achieves a normalized translation MSE of 0.0029 for camera control; for joint generation, it scores 85.48 out of 100 on instruction adherence versus 64.71 for the best cascade on that metric, validating the feasibility of the proposed approach.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Fine-grained few-shot class-incremental learning (FG-FSCIL) aims to incrementally learn visually similar new classes from limited images without catastrophic forgetting. Prior work typically learns strong representations on base classes or leverages vision foundation models (VFMs) directly as backbones or via knowledge distillation. However, VFM backbones incur high computational costs and do not guarantee fine-grained recognition. Although knowledge distillation reduces inference cost, our preliminary experiments reveal that distilled students still lag substantially behind their VFM teachers, particularly on new classes. To address this limitation, we propose STAND, a systematic VFM-to-student distillation design tailored for FG-FSCIL. Specifically, STAND connects the teacher and student by equipping the student with a fixed copy of the teacher's classifier, aligning their outputs via projectors, and feeding both models composite images that combine different training images. On these composite images, relation distillation matches how the teacher and the student relate each image to other images, while difference distillation matches how this relation changes between two composite images. Finally, we freeze the student and classify new classes using length-calibrated prototypes, which restore the reduced similarity of few-shot prototypes. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that STAND achieves state-of-the-art performance across FG-FSCIL datasets at low computational cost, while generalizing across student backbones and broader datasets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Spectroscopic thermometry in explosion fields has attracted considerable attention in related engineering domains, primarily because it can mitigate the interference of traditional contact methods with temperature distributions and address the limitations of high-temperature measurement scenarios. However, existing physics-model-driven methods typically rely on regressing a single radiance-ratio pair, which limits their adaptability to wide-range temperature estimation. This limitation arises from notable variations in the mapping between grayscale ratios and radiance ratios at different temperatures. In contrast, existing data-driven methods often suffer from limited performance due to the scarcity of training data, a challenge exacerbated by the sensitivity of explosion fields. In this work, we propose a gated mixture of experts (GMoE) framework that integrates a divide-and-conquer strategy in deep learning with physical prior knowledge for temperature estimation, thereby decomposing the complex task into multiple expert-specific subtasks. Specifically, each expert is developed using physically calibrated polynomial functions to model the relationship between grayscale ratios and radiance ratios for a given pair of spectral bands, with temperature then estimated using a physical model incorporating the corresponding parameters. Additionally, a gating network is designed to adaptively weight the outputs of different experts and generate the final temperature estimate. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed framework achieves the best performance when implemented with the Transformer-based gating network, yielding a mean RMSE of $12.70 \pm 1.32$ K and a mean MAE of $7.96 \pm 0.86$ K. Compared with conventional spectral colorimetry, the proposed framework reduces the mean RMSE and MAE by 63.4\% and 70.8\%, respectively.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have made substantial progress in visual reasoning, but extended generation over large visual contexts remains computationally expensive. During autoregressive decoding, visual attention is recomputed for every generated token, even when consecutive tokens rely on similar visual evidence. We introduce VAPOR (Visual Attention Persistence with On-demand Refresh), a training-free framework that reduces this redundancy by reusing visual attention outputs across decoding steps and refreshing them when needed. VAPOR caches the visual attention output while continuing to update textual attention at every step, helping reduce visual grounding drift during extended reasoning. To decide when to reuse cached outputs, VAPOR estimates visual attention mass using a low-rank approximation. It also uses prefill-based attention consistency and cache age to make independent Persist and Refresh decisions for each attention head. VAPOR retains the full visual key-value (KV) cache, so all visual tokens remain available when new evidence is needed. Experiments on eight image and four video reasoning benchmarks across two MLLM families show that VAPOR improves accuracy over dense attention on nearly all benchmarks while achieving 1.11-1.21x decoding speedups. Compared with the strongest state-of-the-art acceleration baseline in each setting, VAPOR further improves accuracy by 0.35-2.15 percentage points while achieving comparable or lower decoding latency. Our code is available at https://github.com/Su-my/VAPOR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Change detection identifies where images differ, but in practice users often seek only a particular kind of change. Existing referring change detection methods rely on predefined categories, textual descriptions, or annotated masks, limiting flexibility or requiring additional annotation. In this paper, we propose a new way to perform change detection, where an unannotated before-and-after image pair serves as a visual example to guide change localization in another pair. Our framework, Visual Example-Guided Change Detection (VECD), learns comparable attribute states from single images and introduces an example-conditioned readout to match changes across different objects and scenes. The example itself defines the request, without text, example masks, or test-time fine-tuning. We evaluate VECD on four datasets spanning rendered scenes and natural image edits: EditCLEVR, MagicBrush, HumanEdit, and MaskEdit. Experiments demonstrate cross-dataset generalization without fine-tuning: a single fixed example pair guides change detection across multiple scenes, even when examples and queries originate from different datasets. VECD selects matching changes in scenes with multiple simultaneous changes and returns an empty mask when the requested change is absent. It further generalizes to attribute families withheld from paired training, achieving 74.54\% to 93.38\% mIoU across four holdout settings. Code is available at https://github.com/qianyuancs/VECD

</details>

Abstract (英文): Wearable assistants must localize interface elements from camera observations when direct screenshot access is unavailable, yet existing GUI grounding benchmarks primarily evaluate native screenshots. We introduce GlassGround, a new benchmark that pairs native screenshots with wearable camera observations while holding GUI states, instructions, and targets fixed. GlassGround contains 3,250 tasks across Android and iOS, captured using four wearable devices under four viewing conditions. Across ten open-source models, mean grounding accuracy drops from 94.8% on screenshots to 48.5% on camera images. Further analysis reveals that changes in target scale, screen geometry, and background cannot fully explain this performance degradation. We then study two ways to narrow this gap. At inference time, local zooming and screen rectification help but leave most of the gap. For training-time adaptation, we build on GlassGround's paired-view design and introduce PairDistill, a baseline that aligns target representations across views alongside coordinate supervision. Trained on independently collected data and using no screenshots at inference, it improves camera-image accuracy by 9.4 and 12.2 points on two backbones and transfers to unseen devices and operating systems. Combined with local zooming, it reaches 69.6% and 64.4%, but does not close the gap. The GlassGround dataset and code are available at https://github.com/yunnw/GlassGround.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Unified multimodal autoregressive (AR) models represent text and images in one token sequence and generate all modalities with a shared next-token prediction head. Their image generation, however, decodes an $H\times W$ visual grid token by token in raster order, which takes $HW$ sequential steps and dominates inference time. Existing acceleration methods for visual AR generation either change the tokenizer, the prediction head, or the model interface, or keep the model but cannot reach high parallelism without losing quality, so they do not carry over to unified AR models. We propose ScanAR, a row-scan parallel decoding method that only redefines the predecessors of visual tokens. The first visual row is generated from left to right as usual, and each later row is generated in a single parallel step, with every token predicted from the vertically aligned token of the previous row. Nonvisual tokens keep the standard autoregressive rule. ScanAR reduces the visual decoding depth from $HW$ to $H+W$ without changing the backbone, vocabulary, or output head and without adding parameters, and it supports both training from scratch and lightweight post-training. On Emu3.5-Image-34B, ScanAR post-training reaches a GenEval score of 82.36, the best among post-training acceleration methods trained on the same data, and generates $512\times512$ images $13.8\times$ faster than raster decoding under the same inference setting. On ImageNet, ScanAR trained from scratch has the highest throughput at every model size with FID comparable to raster LlamaGen. On the 34B Xiaomi-Robotics-U0 model, ScanAR preserves the overall quality of embodied scene generation, interleaved image-text generation, and video generation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Medical visual question answering (VQA) requires acquiring useful evidence and deciding where to look next. Yet final-answer rewards do not reveal which observations support answering or guide subsequent inspection. We introduce MedPACT, an agentic reinforcement learning framework that learns selective observation policies through immediate answer support and prospective guidance. Our content-controlled estimator compares next actions induced by real and matched alternative observations under a shared real-observation history, isolating how observed content improves subsequent decisions. We combine both forms of credit within GRPO, assigning observation credit to the corresponding tool-call tokens alongside trajectory-level outcome supervision. MedPACT uses a single vision-language model and image-question-answer supervision, with credit estimation confined to training. Experiments with 4B and 8B backbones on six medical VQA benchmarks demonstrate improved accuracy, strong out-of-distribution generalization, and substantially lower inference costs. Case studies further illustrate how MedPACT avoids repeated observations and uses acquired evidence to guide subsequent inspection, demonstrating real-world practice potential.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Action-conditioned video world models generate future observations from an initial frame and a camera trajectory, but often fail to align camera commands with visual dynamics or represent full 6-DoF scene transformations. We present DuoWM, a first-person drone video world model with two complementary visual conditions. Action flow projects future camera positions and orientations into each target frame, while Object flow forward-warps the initial observation and overlays local 6-DoF transformations to ground the induced scene motion. We further introduce transform-aware pretraining, which pairs object appearance with explicit transformation axes before standard RGB video training. On Sekai-drone dataset, DuoWM achieves the best results across video quality, 3D consistency, and trajectory following. Compared with Captain Safari, the state-of-the-art method, our approach reduces real-domain FVD and LPIPS by 17.2\% and 21.6\%, respectively, while improving game-domain AUC@30 from 0.5003 to 0.5719 and AUC@15 from 0.3088 to 0.3747.

Abstract (英文): Mapping the damage to buildings following natural and human-caused disasters is essential for disaster response and humanitarian assistance. However, differences in hazard types, geographic environments, building characteristics, and imaging conditions cause cross-event distribution shifts that limit the generalization of deep learning models to new disaster events, even for geospatial foundation models (GFMs) pretrained on extensive Earth observation data. Unsupervised domain adaptation (UDA) can mitigate these shifts, but adaptation remains challenging when damage-proportion and damage-appearance shifts coexist and interact. We identify enhancing target-domain discriminability as key to addressing this challenge. To this end, inspired by positive-unlabeled (PU) learning, we introduce positive-unlabeled domain adaptation, a paradigm that incorporates PU weak supervision to enhance target-domain discriminability, requiring only a few labeled damaged buildings in the target domain. Building upon this straightforward intuition, we develop Positive-ANchored Domain Adaptation (PANDA), comprising two complementary and mutually reinforcing components: Pairwise Alignment with Intra-class Robustness (PAIR) for discriminative representation learning and Refine Gate and Verifier (RAV) for supervision refinement. On a global-scale multi-disaster benchmark, PANDA generally outperforms representative domain adaptation and PU learning methods. Results on the 2025 Los Angeles wildfires show its effectiveness in new disaster scenarios with few labeled damaged buildings. Further experiments demonstrate the potential of integrating PANDA with GFMs for cross-event adaptation. These findings highlight PANDA's practical potential for rapid, reliable, and cross-event building damage mapping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Spatial transcriptomics (ST) profiles gene expression with spatial context but remains costly, motivating models that infer expression from H&E images. Existing approaches face three key limitations: (i) a single patch embedding is often insufficient: morphologically similar regions can exhibit distinct expression due to spatial context, while naive neighborhood aggregation can over-smooth boundaries; (ii) conventional fixed, gene-indexed output heads treat genes independently and cannot directly support strict zero-shot prediction for unseen genes; and (iii) common metrics under-measure spatial pattern fidelity and boundary resolution. To address these challenges, we propose TIGEM, a topology-informed, gene-conditioned framework for histology-to-ST inference. TIGEM learns topology-aware spot representations by regularizing graph attention with tissue-level topology and morphology cues to avoid cross-compartment smoothing, and uses gene semantic modulation to replace gene-indexed heads, enabling zero-shot prediction for unseen genes. We also introduce Graph Spectral Correlation (GSC) to evaluate spatial pattern fidelity and boundary resolution complement to PCC/MSE. Across three large-scale ST datasets, TIGEM achieves overall state-of-the-art performance and significantly improved boundary prediction resolution, and further demonstrates the ability to predict expression patterns for genes unseen during training, extending prior zero-shot histology-to-ST efforts with topology-aware spatial propagation and PLM-based gene conditioning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) renders photorealistic novel views in real time, but its per-scene training (optimization) remains slow. Recent fast training methods such as FastGS shorten training to a few minutes by reducing the number of Gaussians. Profiling FastGS, we find two costs that remain even in its compact model. First, rasterization is still the largest cost, and redundant work remains in it. Second, the loss computation does not shrink with the number of Gaussians, so its share of the training time grows as the model becomes compact. We present SprintGS, a training framework built on FastGS that keeps the model compact and reduces both costs. For rasterization, scheduled tile culling limits each Gaussian to the tiles where it matters, using a coarse footprint that is annealed to the exact one before training ends; convergence-aware pruning removes converged, nearly transparent Gaussians; and a shuffle-efficient backward pass reduces the data exchanged between GPU threads. For the loss, a fused loss computes the loss and its image gradient in the forward pass without a loss map, removing most of its cost. SprintGS trains 2.15x faster than FastGS at comparable quality, and 1.81x faster in its high-quality setting. It also accelerates other fast training methods and dynamic, sparse-view, and surface reconstruction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World-action models (WAMs) unify future video prediction and action generation for robotic control, but their reliance on iterative denoising incurs substantial inference costs. Distilling these models into few-step generators offers a promising route to efficient deployment, yet maintaining task performance under aggressive step reduction remains challenging. Existing methods rely on point-wise trajectory consistency distillation, which pursues high visual fidelity while leaving downstream action information unexploited in optimizing video generation, and also lacks explicit distribution-level alignment. To address this, we propose DriftWAM, a few-step WAM distillation framework that incorporates the actions induced by predicted videos into video learning. Specifically, DriftWAM integrates action-aware video drifting, which performs video distribution matching through a joint visual--action similarity kernel to steer sample evolution under the guidance of these induced actions, with action-guided video regularization, which leverages the compatible teacher--student matches identified by the joint kernel to backpropagate action-level gradients directly into the video generator. Evaluations on the RoboTwin 2.0 and LIBERO benchmarks, as well as in real-world robotic manipulation tasks, show that DriftWAM consistently outperforms prior consistency distillation methods across few-step settings at the same inference speed.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World action models (WAMs) support robot control through visual prediction, but dense future modeling repeatedly encodes static context already available in the current observation, while action-relevant changes are often spatially sparse. We study sparse dynamic representation learning, retaining the current observation as a contextual anchor and using a small set of compact dynamic tokens to represent anticipated changes. We develop CompactWAM, a sparse-dense joint predictive framework that enables sparse representation learning within a video-supervised shared backbone, without requiring a reconstruction objective for the sparse pathway. Since a limited token budget alone does not specify which information to retain, we introduce Dynamic Forcing, which pairs direct action supervision with privileged dense future guidance to promote action relevance and dynamic fidelity. While action prediction gradients directly drive compact tokens to capture control-relevant cues, dense future guidance further shapes them via action functional guidance that matches sparse and dense contributions to the action expert, and dynamics-aware distribution matching, which aligns temporal relational changes relative to a static reference. At inference, a single visual forward pass encodes current observations and compact dynamic tokens for action generation, eliminating dense future modeling. CompactWAM achieves 98.4\% success on LIBERO and 77.9\% on LIBERO-Plus, retaining respective success rates of 98.3\% and 76.6\% with only 9.2\% of the dense future token budget. Evaluations on RoboTwin~2.0 and real-world manipulation further demonstrate the effectiveness of the learned sparse predictive representations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Video anomaly understanding (VAU) requires reasoning about event progression, interactions, and the contextual conditions that distinguish abnormal behavior from common activity across diverse scenes. Although video agents enable multi-round analysis, the predefined analytical operations may not adequately satisfy these diverse reasoning demands. In this paper, we present EvoVAU, a framework that evolves a reusable tool library for a frozen video solver. Guided by task outcomes and execution trajectories, an external proposer identifies potential limitations in the library and refines it, retaining task experience as analytical operations that can be reused across questions. Specifically, Cross-Version Contrastive Feedback guides tool refinement by combining current errors with paired execution trajectories from historical corrections and regressions. Adoption-Aware Selection accepts a candidate library only when overall development-set accuracy improves and corrections outnumber regressions among questions on which the solver invokes new or modified tools. Using a small development set, EvoVAU alternates tool refinement with candidate evaluation and selection. At test time, the evolved library remains fixed, with no further involvement from the proposer. Experiments on several VAU benchmarks demonstrate strong performance and the flexibility of the evolved library across anomaly domains.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reconstructing 3D joint kinematics from a single video is ill-posed due to depth and stature ambiguity, limb occlusion, and missing uncertainty estimates. We introduce the Biomechanical Flow Model (BFM), a continuous-time flow-matching prior over subject anthropometry, anatomical joint-angle trajectories, and virtual markers, trained on multi-camera clinical recordings from 565 participants and steered toward monocular 2D keypoints at test time without paired video training. In a head-to-head comparison on the same clinical trials against a state-of-the-art deterministic trajectory optimizer fitting the identical skeleton, BFM achieves comparable joint-angle accuracy across walking and timed-up-and-go (TUG). Across held-out control, prosthetic, and neurologic participants, BFM reaches 3.91 deg and 4.97 deg mean absolute joint-angle error on walking and TUG while providing credible intervals that bridge temporal and whole-limb occlusions. Where keypoints are hidden, the intervals widen with the error and keep their coverage near nominal. Supplying individual anthropometric calibration removes most monocular pelvis depth error, whereas testing on out-of-distribution pediatric crouch gait produces biased, overconfident extension predictions, highlighting the need for pathologically diverse movement priors.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language-action (VLA) models generate robot actions from visual observations and language instructions in real-world environments, making malicious model tampering a potential threat to physical safety. We study whether object-triggered backdoors can be implanted through a small, fixed set of parameter bit flips without retraining. The central challenge is to make persistent weight modifications disrupt trigger-present behavior while preserving performance on non-trigger inputs. We propose FlipVLA, a two-stage selector-amplifier method that exploits existing visual features within an FC-GELU-FC structure. The selector constructs a trigger-dependent response in a selected channel, using GELU to attenuate background activity. The amplifier modifies a connected downstream weight to convert this response into an action-relevant perturbation. Channel screening and discrete bit search construct both components under a limited modification budget. Experiments on OpenVLA-OFT and GR00T N1.7 across four LIBERO suites achieve attack success rates of 90.22% to 100%, with task success decreasing by at most 1.5 percentage points on clean scenes.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Text-to-motion generation aims to synthesize realistic human motions from natural-language descriptions, enabling applications in animation, virtual avatars, and embodied agents. Despite recent progress, existing methods either retrieve motion exemplars or retain long-range context, but rarely exploit memory to jointly reason about semantics, dynamics, and generation history. In this paper, we propose MMotion, a collaborative multi-scale memory framework that unifies scale-conditioned semantic retrieval, latent dynamics modeling, and causal working memory. Specifically, semantic memory provides motion priors at different generation scales, dynamics memory captures latent state transitions, and working memory preserves causal context from previously generated motions. A collaborative fusion mechanism integrates these complementary memories to determine what motion to generate, how it should evolve, and how it should remain consistent with its history. MMotion is architecture-agnostic and can be readily integrated into both diffusion-based and next scale autoregressive models. Extensive experiments across standard benchmarks and diverse backbones demonstrate consistent improvements over strong baselines.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In-Hand Object Scanning (IHOS) from monocular video captured by a commodity smartphone holds immense potential to democratize 3D digitization for non-expert users, facilitating applications in digital archiving, augmented reality, and custom fabrication. Yet, IHOS remains largely understudied due to a lack of realistic datasets and systematic benchmarks. To bridge this gap, we introduce ToysRUs, a high-quality dataset featuring diverse figurines captured by 4K Ultra High-Definition (UHD) monocular videos. Studying IHOS from first principles, we examine structure-from-motion (SfM) for camera pose estimation, which is key to IHOS. We identify two key challenges: small baselines between consecutive frames, and sparse feature matches across large-view, long-range frames, further exacerbated by dynamic hand occlusions. Guided by these insights, we propose performing SfM on video sub-sequences sampled at sufficient angular intervals, and iteratively stitches the corresponding sub-trajectories into a unified global trajectory. Furthermore, for high-fidelity surface reconstruction, we introduce occlusion-aware 2D Gaussian Splatting, which incorporates opacity rendering and omits the opacity loss on hand regions. Quantitative and qualitative evaluations demonstrate that our framework significantly outperforms existing approaches, including neural implicit representations and 3D foundation models, in both geometric accuracy and photometric fidelity.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Autoregressive text-to-motion generation requires local motion predictions to reconcile cross-temporal textual requirements with the available motion context. While existing methods primarily improve motion representations and generation order, the internal formation of conditioned motion representations remains less explored. We introduce MotionODE, a continuous-depth post-training framework that refines hidden-state evolution in pretrained autoregressive generators. MotionODE reinterprets selected Transformer residual branches as local vector fields and replaces their original updates with midpoint Runge--Kutta (RK2) integration, while preserving the motion representation and outer generation mechanism. To address the mismatch between pretrained update paths and newly introduced intermediate states, we propose mixed one-step/two-step RK2 post-training under motion-token supervision, with optional distillation and update-map constraints to preserve pretrained behavior. This minimally invasive framework supports both next-token and next-scale generation without training the generator from scratch. Extensive experiments on HumanML3D demonstrate consistent improvements in text--motion alignment across MoScale, BAMM and LMR, highlighting internal representation updates as a complementary direction for improving autoregressive motion generation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Understanding large-scale 3D urban scenes requires representations that capture both multi-scale geometric structures and complex spatial relationships. However, existing 3D vision-language methods primarily emphasize global semantic alignment or object-level representations, leaving structured relational modeling underexplored within unified contrastive and generative frameworks. In this paper, we present 3D CoCa v3, a scene-graph-enhanced contrastive and generative framework for urban 3D scene understanding. Our framework features a Pyramid Vision Encoder for multi-scale geometric representation learning and a language-model-based decoder with question-guided caption retrieval and visual cross-attention. We further introduce MSG-based relational descriptions to enrich contrastive 3D-language alignment with structured spatial relationships. To support semantic and relational supervision, we construct City3D-9.6K, a multi-view urban vision-language dataset with aligned visual-geometric observations, scene captions, and scene graphs. Extensive experiments on City-3DQA demonstrate that 3D CoCa v3 achieves 88.35% and 64.79% overall top-1 accuracy on the sentence-wise and city-wise splits, surpassing 3D CoCa v2 by 20.88 and 3.57 percentage points, respectively. Furthermore, 3D CoCa v3 achieves the best performance on GeoEval3D, demonstrating its effectiveness in urban spatial reasoning and geometric understanding. Code is available at https://github.com/AIGeeksGroup/3DCoCav3.

Abstract (英文): This paper proposes a new paradigm and method for giving large language models spatial understanding. Standard language models operate exclusively over discrete words (secondary representations of things rather than continuous space itself), forcing them to guess geometry verbally. Whereas most multimodal approaches equate spatial grounding with camera images or video frames, spatial structure itself is not a picture: it consists of coordinate frames, solid boundaries, and how objects move and relate to each other in continuous space. Motivated by this distinction, we place continuous object tokens combining pose (position, orientation, and scale) and signed-distance shape embeddings alongside word tokens inside a single uninterrupted transformer sequence, allowing language and geometry to attend to each other as object scenes are built and updated step by step. Control tokens switch output automatically between natural-language words and continuous object state updates to a persistent object workspace, while an attention mask blocks earlier prose when generating the answer, so responses must be read directly from settled geometry. To prove this method works, we attach a narrow parallel spatial stream (+4.4% weights) alongside unfrozen Qwen3-1.7B layers. In our two-dimensional tests, the model spawns and transforms multi-object scenes from English descriptions, transfers shapes to held-out nouns, and relies causally on the workspace to answer spatial questions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are widely used for image segmentation. However, existing methods either use a decoder or use patch-based approach which trades boundary precision against the number of decisions as coarse grid cells mix foreground and background pixels, while finer grids require many more predictions, and autoregressive mask generation adds sequential decoding overhead. We propose SPiXSeg, which shifts the output from per-patch to per-superpixel label assignment, using superpixel tokens formed by aggregating patch-level visual features. An off-the-shelf model partitions each image into task-agnostic superpixels, so the partition is computed once and reused across tasks. A Spix-to-Mask (S2M) module scores each superpixel token in a single MLLM forward pass by directly comparing the MLLM's hidden representations, without autoregressive generation or a separate scoring network, and the selected superpixels are mapped directly to pixels without refinement. Without any trainable decoder head or post-hoc refinement, SPiXSeg achieves an average cIoU of 80.5 with LLaVA-1.5-7B and 81.9 with InternVL2.5-8B on RefCOCO, RefCOCO+ and RefCOCOg and, when trained only on COCO-Stuff, extends to open-vocabulary semantic segmentation with mIoU of 29.0, 59.5 and 95.4 on ADE20K, PASCAL Context and PASCAL VOC, outperforming several SoTA MLLM baselines. Our results suggest that a fixed, query-agnostic superpixel partition is enough for accurate language-guided segmentation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Breast ultrasound segmentation models often generalize poorly across scanners, institutions, and patient populations. We investigate continuous foundation-guided pseudo-label weighting for unsupervised domain adaptation (UDA). An exponential-moving-average (EMA) teacher generates target-domain pseudo-labels, while a frozen ultrasound segmentation model (AutoSAMUS) modulates their contribution using a bounded weight that combines the prior's certainty with its probabilistic agreement with the teacher. In contrast to our earlier selective BiRP rule, the proposed formulation supervises every target pixel with a weight in [0.5, 1] and requires no foundation-model inference at test time. We evaluate the method on five public breast ultrasound datasets, spanning three source domains and eleven source-target directions. Under the TransUNet protocol, continuous weighting outperforms both all-pixel EMA and original BiRP on all four BUSI-source targets, and achieves the highest mean Dice and lowest HD95 among the compared methods on all four BUS-BRA-source targets. Results with BrEaST as the source are mixed: continuous weighting performs best on BUSI and comparably to original BiRP on OASBUD, whereas none of the adaptation methods surpasses source-only training on BUS-UCLM. In a separate U-Net pipeline, agreement-only weighting performs comparably to the full formulation, leaving the incremental value of prior certainty unresolved. Because the weighting rule was developed after some target-test results had been examined, we treat the study as exploratory and report adverse as well as favorable outcomes.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Infrared and visible image fusion (IVIF) is essential for robust perception, yet existing methods remain limited under complex degradations. While diffusion models (DMs) offer powerful modeling capabilities to combat such degradations, their iterative process incurs high computational cost. One-step distillation effectively accelerates inference but typically leads to over-smoothed results. To resolve these limitations, we propose a one-step fusion framework with hybrid reinforcement learning, termed OneHRL. We treat degraded inputs as intermediate noisy states along a generative trajectory, enabling a distilled one-step generator to rectify them directly to the clean fusion results. Concurrently, to mitigate the over-smoothed results in distillation, we incorporate the Flow-GRPO paradigm to introduce stochasticity into the deterministic one-step generation. This restores the stochastic exploration necessary for sampling-based reinforcement learning. Furthermore, we design a coarse-to-fine reward that integrates a global semantic reward with an object-level reward. This hybrid reward ensures that the generator captures both naturalness and fine-grained texture. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves superior fusion quality and efficiency.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Echocardiographic images are frequently degraded by spatially structured haze artifacts that obscure anatomical boundaries and hinder clinical analysis. Conventional diffusion models typically employ pixel-independent Gaussian noise, limiting their ability to represent localized ultrasound haze. In this study, we propose a structure-informed diffusion framework that explicitly models ultrasound haze as an accumulation of localized Gaussian components constrained by the ultrasound fan geometry. The forward process generates spatially correlated corruption by sampling component locations and scales, providing a formulation that better reflects the spatial characteristics of real echo artifacts. During reverse restoration, a dual-head network predicts an anchor probability map and Gaussian scale parameters to progressively remove structured artifacts while reducing the center bias associated with direct coordinate regression. We evaluated the framework on the public DehazingEcho2025 benchmark and two private clinical datasets, without retraining or fine-tuning on either private cohort. Our method achieved the highest mean generalized contrast-to-noise ratio across all three datasets, reaching $0.715 \pm 0.090$ on the public benchmark while preserving tissue edges.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Conventional embodied UAV tracking couples target observability with heading control because targets may leave the field of view of forward-facing cameras. We investigate a complementary paradigm that uses full-spherical perception to follow designated targets without active heading adjustment. To support this setting, we introduce 360UAV-ETrack, a simulation benchmark comprising 2,652 sequences, over 1.73 million synchronized observations, eight tracking tasks, and 150 held-out closed-loop scenarios. We propose 360UAV-TVLA, a panoramic vision--language--action (VLA) framework integrating persistent visual referring (PVR), compressed temporal memory (CTM), and geometry-aligned trajectory supervision (GTS) for instance identification, motion reasoning, and three-dimensional control. Experiments across two VLA backbones show consistent gains over standard fine-tuning, with normalized average tracking frames increasing from 22.56\% to 35.28\% for $π_0$ and from 25.68\% to 33.64\% for $π_{0.5}$. Our benchmark further reveals a substantial gap between offline imitation accuracy and closed-loop tracking reliability, establishing a challenging testbed for future research https://libingyu01.github.io/360UAV-ETrack_360UAV-TVLA/

Abstract (英文): Audio-visual understanding requires perceptual grounding, temporal understanding, and semantic inference. Multi-teacher distillation can consolidate these capabilities, but direct probability matching does not explicitly capture teacher prediction changes across audio-visual conditions. We introduce OmniMeld, a multi-teacher on-policy distillation method that integrates domain expertise through modality-response-guided distribution matching. We train four teachers covering grounding and correspondence, temporal dynamics and event structure, causal and inferential reasoning, and contextual and holistic understanding. Each sample is routed to its corresponding teacher for supervision conditioned on student-generated answer prefixes. We derive transport costs from teacher prediction changes over shared candidate tokens across audio-visual conditions, using optimal transport to match student predictions to those of the teacher under complete audio-visual input. We also construct OmniMeld-20K for domain-specialized teacher training. The dataset spans four capability domains, with responses structured into evidence, reasoning, and answer sections and verified through consistency checks and media-based review. Experiments on audio-visual and general video understanding benchmarks demonstrate competitive teacher performance among similarly sized open-source models. The student improves over its base model across all evaluated benchmarks and surpasses the teachers' base model on several benchmarks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Online forward matching aligns flow-based generative models by repeatedly generating, rewarding, and fitting model-produced endpoints. However, this behavior creates an on-policy feedback loop: as high-reward modes become increasingly favored, alternative modes become less likely to appear in subsequent training pools, progressively reducing visual diversity. We introduce RefMix, a reference-assisted endpoint proposal strategy that addresses this limitation by injecting alternative candidates into the training pool while retaining the original task reward and forward matching objective. In particular, RefMix uses a reference model during early high-noise steps to expose alternative visual modes, followed by the current reward-tuned model for endpoint completion. Across state-of-the-art online forward matching methods, RefMix consistently improves visual diversity while preserving task reward performance. Controlled inference experiments further demonstrate that training-time endpoint exposure is critical to improve reward--diversity trade-offs, beyond the impact of reference-assisted inference. By rethinking how training endpoints are proposed, RefMix achieves up to 44% higher diversity over forward matching baselines at comparable reward levels.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Raindrops and reflections frequently co-occur on windshields and jointly obscure scene content, making their unified removal, referred to as UR$^3$, a highly ill-posed problem. Existing approaches are mainly based on diffusion models and thus can produce visually plausible restorations. However, they may generate scene-inconsistent structures in severely corrupted regions. To address this limitation, we propose MAFNet-UR$^3$, which adopts a three-stage framework to exploit multiple observations for scene-consistent unified raindrop and reflection removal. Specifically, Stage I suppresses raindrops while preserving the reflection layer, and Stage II performs prior-guided reflection removal followed by image-space refinement. Stage III estimates degradation masks from intermediate restoration results to provide degradation cues and performs mask-aware multi-scale fusion across observations to preserve scene structure and fine details. As a result, our proposed MAFNet-UR$^3$-L achieves state-of-the-art performance on the RDRF-testing benchmark, outperforming DiffUR$^3$ by 2.33 dB in PSNR and 2.7634 in the challenge score. Our method ranked third in the LoViF 2026 UR$^3$ Challenge, achieving a final score of 41.1416.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long sequence reconstruction must correct accumulated drift while preserving locally consistent geometry. In robotics and autonomous driving, embodied acquisition provides recurring platform- environment geometry and repeated observations of scene structure and platform motion. We present Embodied-VGGT, which exploits these cues to establish reliable local relationships and selectively preserve them during global refinement. Memory-Augmented Grounded Chunks (MAGC) builds on Scal3R's global context memory and uses relative scale references derived from predicted ground geometry to regularize test-time memory updates, encouraging cross-chunk consistency while limiting changes to reliable local geometry. Superpoint Alignment (SA) and Cross-Loop Optimization (CL) jointly address the robust establishment and preservation of geometric relationships. SA balances ground and non-ground evidence across spatial regions to estimate inter-chunk translation. CL uses geometric and motion consistency across interleaved windows to select coherent chunk groups, each receiving a shared Sim(3) correction that preserves its internal relative transformations during global optimization. Experiments on five public driving benchmarks demonstrate consistent and substantial improvements in long sequence reconstruction. On KITTI Odometry, Embodied-VGGT achieves a mean absolute trajectory error of 7.30 m and a Chamfer distance of 2.67 m, reducing these errors by 46.8% and 23.3% compared with the state-of-the-art baseline under same evaluation protocol. To facilitate reproducibility, we released our code: https://github.com/Ang-Sheep/Embodied-VGGT.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visual brain decoders are typically trained and evaluated on stimuli drawn from similar semantic distributions, leaving their ability to reconstruct unseen concepts poorly understood. We study this concept generalization problem by withholding semantic concepts from a target subject's training data. We find that three recent fMRI-to-image decoders degrade substantially for the held-out concepts, even when the concept remains available for multi-subject models. In contrast, brain encoders that map a pretrained visual representation directly to voxels are markedly more robust to the same semantic holdout. Motivated by this asymmetry, we use such an encoder to generate synthetic fMRI responses for new images and add the resulting image-fMRI pairs to decoder training. Across three decoders, four subjects, and twelve semantic concepts, synthetic data recovers a substantial fraction of the performance lost on unseen concepts without requiring additional, time-consuming fMRI acquisition. We further show that the same approach improves general decoding performance across a wide range of data budgets. Analyses of encoder training conditions, encoder designs, and synthetic-data controls show that the gains depend on both encoder generalization and the semantic content of the synthetic supervision, rather than merely increasing the amount of training data. Together, our results show that encoder-generated fMRI can transfer semantic generalization to decoders.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Due to the Deep Learning requirement for a large training dataset, Transfer Learning has become a central method in the field of Computer Vision, which heavily used Deep Learning. Since the adoption of transfer learning in the field, the performance of the models in computer vision is significantly upgraded. The common transfer learning practice in computer vision research is to use state-of-the-art architectures in the ImageNet dataset as the backbone network. The best performing architecture in ImageNet is currently held by EfficientNet, which is a set of architectures that were progressively grown from a baseline architecture.
  By observing the EfficientNet development approach, it is natural to hypothesize that the smaller EfficientNet architecture is capable to contain the knowledge of the larger architecture. This hypothesis comes from the fact that the larger EfficientNet architecture was grown from the smaller architecture. Therefore, in this study, we experimented if it is beneficial to transfer the knowledge from the larger architecture to the smaller architecture of the EfficientNet. To achieve this goal, we proposed a transfer learning method that uses Knowledge Distillation as the knowledge transfer mechanism from the larger to smaller architecture. We found that the proposed method is able to upgrade the performance of each of the EfficientNet architecture. Several smaller architectures are even able to outperform the larger architecture that was trained using the standard transfer learning method.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Pre-trained generative models offer rich visual priors for image matting, but their adaptation often relies on variational autoencoders (VAEs), whose spatial compression can degrade fine alpha details and introduce additional inference overhead. To address these limitations, we propose X-Matte, a VAE-free pixel-space flow matching framework for single-step image matting. By eliminating latent compression, X-Matte formulates the RGB-to-alpha transition as a continuous flow directly in pixel space. We adopt trajectory-wide x-prediction with transition-aware alpha supervision, enabling the model to learn from intermediate states while directly predicting the target alpha matte in a single forward pass. This design avoids both VAE encoding and decoding and iterative sampling. Extensive experiments demonstrate performance competitive with state-of-the-art trimap-free matting methods on P3M benchmarks, together with promising zero-shot generalization to RWP-636 and AIM-500. Moreover, X-Matte achieves up to a 9.4x inference speedup over a constructed single-step latent counterpart at high resolution, demonstrating the effectiveness of VAE-free pixel-space modeling for accurate and efficient image matting.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Deep-learning-generated media are increasingly passed off as real, driving an arms race between image generators and deepfake detectors. Trust in the latter is limited by their often black-box nature; and, while region-based explanations can sometimes be extracted, these become less useful as generation quality improves. Counterfactual examples, which show the minimal change that flips a detector's decision, reveal what evidence drives a verdict, helping analysts decide whether to trust it. This work introduces Counter-DECEIVR (DEtector-guided reCtifiEd flow InVeRsion for counterfactuals), a novel training-free framework that generates counterfactual examples. Counter-DECEIVR steers latents in the velocity field of a rectified flow model using gradients from off-the-shelf deepfake detectors, without training a generator or adapting the detector; unlike prior methods, it requires no per-instance or per-detector fitting and no diffusion training on the target data. It also leverages the detector's explanation maps to concentrate editing to salient regions, turning low-level feature importance into localised counterfactual edits. A single parameter controls the strength of the edits. Across two deepfake detectors and two widely used forensics datasets, Counter-DECEIVR outperforms prior counterfactual generation methods, including training-based ones. Code will be made available upon acceptance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): End-to-end driving policies increasingly operate without explicit HD maps at inference, raising the question of what road knowledge they actually internalize. To probe this knowledge through model outputs, we introduce Road-KSU, a framework that automatically converts HD maps and actor annotations into a road-centric scene graph. From this, we derive road-focused question answering and reasoning targets, complemented by map-based trajectory compliance metrics. Pairing Road-KSU with geographically disjoint splits yields a $2 \times 2$ evaluation crossing output modality (language vs. action) with place familiarity (seen vs. unseen roads). On NAVSIM, 95% of test samples lie within 10m of a training sample, and scores of 13 published planners on turns rise steeply with the number of training drives through the test location, pointing to memorized road geometry. Retraining on geographically disjoint splits and probing with Road-KSU confirm that action-space performance degrades most on unfamiliar turns, with failures dominated by geometric compliance. Language accuracy, however, drops on road-centric VQA on straight and curved roads alike, and remains stable on dynamic scene content. Scaling the training data further reveals a double dissociation: more drives through the same places improve driving on unseen roads but leave road question answering unchanged, whereas spreading a fixed data budget over more places increases road question answering with little change in driving performance. These findings show that improved driving scores need not imply broader road understanding on unseen roads, and identify geographic diversity as key to transferable road knowledge.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Online services that deploy Image-to-3D models enable users to generate high-quality 3D assets from a single image. However, the generated 3D geometry can violate content policies and cause broader security risks. To prevent such content, these services typically use policy-restricted inspectors to examine uploaded images before generation. Since the inspector examines only the visible content of the input, a visually benign image can still induce the generation of policy-restricted 3D geometry. However, turning this vulnerability into an effective attack is non-trivial in the black-box setting: global backpropagation through the long DiT generation trajectory is difficult to fit, while different image preprocessing pipelines across services can substantially reduce transferability. In this paper, we present PTA3D, a targeted adversarial attack against Image-to-3D models that addresses these challenges through Prefix Trajectory Alignment (PTA) and Preprocessing Aware Optimization (PAO). PTA progressively aligns the prefix DiT outputs of the adversarial trajectory with those of the target trajectory, where the core geometric structure is formed, allowing the final geometry to be controlled without optimizing through the complete generation trajectory. PAO exposes adversarial optimization to diverse image transformations rather than a single preprocessing pipeline, reducing sensitivity to the mismatch between surrogate and target services. We establish a prefix-to-output bound and a preprocessing-aware transfer bound to theoretically characterize the effectiveness of the two designs. Experiments on three open-source Image-to-3D models and three online services show that PTA3D achieves 41.5%-88.5% ASR on online services and 90.8%-94.6% ASR in white box settings, substantially outperforming existing attacks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D layout-controlled image editing gives users explicit control over object placement and camera viewpoint. In a sequence of edits, however, the latest image can lose the appearance of an object when it is occluded or leaves view. The target layout specifies where the object should reappear, while its appearance must be recovered from earlier observations. We introduce LayoutRecall, a multi-round editor that stores and reuses appearance in the local coordinate systems of 3D boxes. Box-Surface Latent Memory retains image latents on box faces and retrieves them along rays in the target view. Geometry-Aligned Memory Conditioning supplies the retrieved tokens to a FLUX.2 editor using target-view positions, shared box-local embeddings, and object-wise attention. A correspondence loss encourages the editor to attend to matching observations during denoising. We also introduce LayoutLoop, a multi-round 3D layout-controlled image editing benchmark with calibrated layouts and designed revisits. We conduct extensive experiments on LayoutLoop and real-scene data to verify the ability of our method. The project page is at https://ykchizs.github.io/layoutrecall.github.io/.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generating coherent and explorable 3D worlds from scattered images with little or no visual overlap is a fundamental and long-standing challenge in computer vision, which existing geometry-aware methods fail to address. Meanwhile, existing image- and video-based methods may generate unseen regions but produce inconsistent geometry across views. To bridge this gap, we present WorldBridge, a framework for coherent and controllable 3D world generation under scattered images with little or no visual overlap. WorldBridge encodes scattered references into a global scene context to guide geometry and appearance generation across views. Our temporal and spatial reference attention mechanism further enhances the fidelity of temporal references along the target trajectory and supports flexible spatial references outside it. A plug-and-play latent-reference consistency strategy improves reference fidelity through reprojection-guided test-time optimization. We also introduce the WorldBridge benchmark suite to evaluate 3D world generation from scattered, low-overlap references, which is rarely explored by current benchmarks. Both qualitative and quantitative results demonstrate that our method enables controllable and coherent 3D world generation, surpassing the existing state-of-the-art solutions. Our project page is available at https://davidyan2001.github.io/WorldBridge/.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Latent video generators are trained on clips of a few seconds, and much of this limit lies in their tokenizers, which spend as many tokens on a static frame as on a moving one and need future frames that do not exist during generation. We introduce DeltaRAE, a causal representation autoencoder for video. Its derivative tokenization encodes periodic anchor frames in full and all remaining frames with a few tokens each, and its causal encoder and decoder read a frozen pretrained image encoder. On UCF-101 and Kinetics-600, a standard diffusion transformer trained on DeltaRAE latents is competitive with state-of-the-art video tokenizers. DeltaRAE halves the tokenizer GFLOPs on 16-frame clips relative to keeping the same number of patch tokens for every frame, and it represents a minute of video with 18.8x fewer tokens. The tokenizer trains end to end on one-minute videos, and in video prediction a generator on its latents produces a full minute of video. The same generator collapses within 15 seconds in most videos when the tokenizer has the same token budget but no pretrained encoder.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In real-world applications like egocentric recording, visual accessibility, and surveillance review, visual evidence for Video Large Language Models (Video LLMs) is often only partially captured. A reliable model should match its response to the evidence: answering when sufficient, guessing from visible clues when partial, and abstaining when absent. Yet existing benchmarks enforce a binary answerable-or-unanswerable split, leaving partial evidence unmeasured and obscuring whether failures arise from missing evidence or misjudging sufficiency. We introduce ClueProbe, a diagnostic benchmark to (1) separate perceiving evidence from judging sufficiency, and (2) validate our diagnosis via targeted fine-tuning. ClueProbe comprises 1,721 human-annotated questions across 598 natural videos, labeled Answerable, Guessable, or Unanswerable, alongside controlled perturbations altering wording or visual input. Evaluating 10 open-source and proprietary Video LLMs reveals that failures stem from judgment rather than perception: models perceive the evidence yet lack graded sufficiency awareness, answering or refusing outright while rarely guessing from partial clues. Targeted fine-tuning shows that this reflects missing supervision rather than insufficient capacity: supervising the guess level lifts appropriate guessing from 1.6% to 40.4% without extra false refusals, whereas conventional refusal tuning rejects 92% of guessable questions. Data and code will be released at https://github.com/suat-llm/ClueProbe.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Image analogy transfers a demonstrated transformation from a before/after pair (A, A') to a new query image B. Existing methods assume all changes in (A, A') should be fully transferred, limiting flexibility when users want only a subset of the demonstrated edits. We introduce Selective Image Analogy (SIA), a new setting where a single demonstration pair encodes multiple edits, and the user selects at inference time which edits to apply to B. To support this, we curate a multi-edit analogy dataset with full-edit and partial-edit targets. We then train a model that first learns to transfer the complete demonstrated transformation, and then gains selective control through a mask-aligned suppression mechanism: a token-gated LoRA that restricts adapter updates to suppressed edit tokens during denoising, paired with a concept suppression vector that weakens the selected tokens in the text embeddings before denoising. Together, these components allow SIA to suppress unwanted edits that remain visible in A', while preserving the user-selected ones, all without requiring a new demonstration pair for each edit combination. We perform extensive quantitative and qualitative evaluations along with user studies to demonstrate the efficacy of our approach.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate 3D surface reconstruction from unposed images remains a fundamental challenge in computer vision. Recent feed-forward pointmap methods eliminate the need for camera poses but inherently produce discrete, sparse representations. In this paper, we present a generalizable pipeline that directly recovers continuous, high-fidelity 3D surfaces from unposed images by seamlessly integrating pointmap- and ray-based representations. Our approach first extracts explicit geometric priors using a pre-trained pointmap model. A query-view-centric selection module then identifies geometrically relevant reference views via a robust surfel voting mechanism. Next, a cross-attention regressor bridges target query rays with reference features to estimate a continuous coarse surface. Lastly, a raylet-based module aggregates local 3D features to carve out high-frequency details and analytical normals. Trained as a single foundation model on 9 diverse datasets, our method demonstrates exceptional zero-shot generalization on 6 unseen datasets, clearly surpassing state-of-the-art baselines in geometric accuracy and detail preservation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Timestep-distilled diffusion models generate high-quality images in one or a few steps, but often suffer from reduced mode coverage and limited diversity. Recent methods improve diversity by optimizing the initial noise at inference time, requiring costly backpropagation through the denoiser. Moreover, their diversity objectives typically repel generated samples without accounting for how the corresponding teacher model distributes its outputs. We introduce Teacher-Weighted Initial-noise Spherical Transform (TWIST), a lightweight network that transforms the low-frequency components of initial noise to improve diversity without inference-time optimization. TWIST uses a teacher-guided determinantal point process (DPP) objective to align the feature-space diversity of student samples with that of the teacher. By amortizing noise optimization into a single network, TWIST eliminates inference-time backpropagation while preserving the efficiency of few-step generation. On SDXL-Turbo, TWIST matches the diversity of MoNO at less than 25% of its inference cost, while achieving higher recall and better PickScore, ImageReward, and CLIPScore under matched compute budgets. On SDXL-Lightning, it achieves comparable recall at lower cost without the ImageReward degradation observed with MoNO.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) excel at static visual understanding and are increasingly applied to spatial planning, where a model interprets a visual layout and outputs an action sequence to reach a goal. Grid games are a common testbed for this ability because their rules are explicit and a solver can verify every move. Yet existing benchmarks mostly report end-to-end success rates, which hide whether a failure stems from visual perception, rule application, or multi-step planning. We introduce GridProbe, a diagnostic benchmark that 1) locates the failing stage between visual perception and planning and 2) verifies the diagnosis by targeted fine-tuning. On Sokoban, GridProbe pairs end-to-end solving with four probes (recognition, localization, single-step prediction, and state tracking) and gives every board both as an image and as an equivalent text grid. Evaluating seven MLLMs on Sokoban and a maze game reveals distinct bottlenecks behind similar success rates: proprietary models are limited by multi-step planning, whereas open-source models are limited by rule application or by visual grid indexing, i.e., counting rows and columns. Planning failures also hide perception failures: when planning is easier, the visual cost of the same model grows from 7.6 to 37.7 points. The diagnosis identifies what to train: fine-tuning only the vision module of the perception-limited model on board reading raises its interactive Sokoban success 3.5-fold (5.8% to 20.4%) and transfers zero-shot to the unseen maze game (single-turn success 13.8% to 67.1%), whereas the same data does not help a rule-limited model and training on solver solutions brings no gain. We will release the benchmark and code at https://github.com/suat-llm/GridProbe.

</details>
