Abstract (英文): Recent 3D generative models can produce high-quality meshes with complex structures and fine geometric details, with broad applications in games, film, and digital design. However, high-quality generation typically relies on long multi-step sampling processes that repeatedly evaluate large networks, leading to long inference times. At the same time, reference-driven tasks such as game-asset reconstruction and product digitization demand close correspondence to the input image: generated geometry must faithfully reconstruct the specific reference object. Acceleration must therefore reduce sampling computation while preserving the model's ability to reconstruct that object. To enable fast and faithful 3D generation, we introduce Spe3DUp, a coarse-to-fine framework based on Faithful Trajectory Distillation (FTD). FTD directly supervises few-step students with the teacher's input-conditioned denoising trajectories to preserve its generation behavior. We apply FTD separately to coarse geometry generation and geometric refinement, compressing both sampling processes while maintaining correspondence to the reference input. We further accelerate the complete mesh-generation pipeline with efficient geometry decoding and parallel remeshing. Experiments show that Spe3DUp maintains geometric quality and input correspondence close to those of the teacher, accelerates the coarse and refinement DiTs by 10.0x and 19.9x, respectively, and achieves a 7.7x end-to-end speedup over the original teacher pipeline.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Two-frame video frame interpolation commonly resamples endpoint optical flow by the query time, which selects a constant-velocity straight-line path even though the same endpoint displacement is compatible with many intermediate trajectories. We present HermiteVFI, which replaces independent query-time motion prediction with one explicit continuous trajectory per pixel. The measured endpoint displacement fixes the two trajectory positions, while two latent endpoint derivatives learned from the frame pair control the interior path. A cubic Hermite curve is the minimum-degree polynomial that can independently satisfy these two position and two derivative boundary quantities. Its residual form contains linear motion exactly, structurally preserves the measured displacement endpoints for any network output, and has coefficients that are predicted once per frame pair and evaluated in closed form at arbitrary t. We further show that one interior query time gives a rank-deficient constraint on the two free trajectory coefficients, whereas any two distinct interior times are full rank at the representation level, motivating multi-time supervision. Appearance-derived directional cues help infer the latent derivatives that endpoint displacement alone cannot identify. On X4K1000FPS, SNU-FILM, and Vimeo90K-Septuplet, the largest gains occur for difficult non-uniform motion and high interpolation ratios. The amortization applies to trajectory-coefficient prediction; downstream reversal, refinement, and synthesis remain query dependent.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Cross-subject EEG-to-image retrieval suffers from substantial performance degradation on new subjects due to subject shift. Test-time adaptation (TTA) can mitigate this shift by adapting pretrained models with unlabeled target-subject EEG. However, most existing TTA methods struggle to match supervision granularity to each query's prediction confidence, leaving many queries without reliable pseudo-labels. To address this, we propose a novel framework named Hierarchical Selective Test-Time Adaptation (HSTTA). HSTTA builds a hierarchy over gallery image embeddings, uses hierarchical selective prediction to assign each EEG query a supervision granularity matching its current prediction confidence, and constructs adaptation objectives accordingly. We further propose Cross-Session Consistency Calibration (CSCC), an unsupervised method that calibrates the global confidence threshold to target a predefined accuracy level for hierarchical predictions. Moreover, we introduce Reciprocal Hierarchical Backoff (RHB), which moves queries lacking reverse-matching support to coarser nodes to mitigate error reinforcement. Experiments on two public benchmarks and ablation studies validate the effectiveness of HSTTA and its key components. On THINGS-EEG2, HSTTA outperforms the strongest EEG-specific TTA baseline by 17.9 (13.4) and 8.9 (3.7) percentage points in Top-1/Top-5 accuracy, respectively, under cosine (CSLS) ranking. Further analyses show that HSTTA yields more accurate pseudo-labels during adaptation, supporting more reliable model updates.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent advances in Gaussian splatting have enabled efficient underwater reconstruction, but sparse observations, light attenuation, and backscatter still leave scene regions underconstrained and cause novel-view artifacts. We present OceanFix, a reference-guided framework for sparse-view underwater 3D reconstruction that combines diffusion-based view repair with Gaussian scene refinement. Our repair model follows a two-stage training strategy that separates underwater appearance adaptation from geometric refinement. The first stage learns underwater appearance from reference observations and estimated medium features. The second uses camera poses and reference depth to align reference features, with relative-depth supervision guiding structural repair without requiring target-view depth at inference. We further propose medium-aware distillation that transfers repaired views into the 3D scene according to geometric repair demand and channel-specific optical observability. Through iterative rendering, repair, and distillation, OceanFix refines underconstrained regions while retaining supervision from the observed views. Experiments on underwater datasets demonstrate that OceanFix improves rendering quality in sparse-view 3D reconstruction, reducing visible artifacts and recovering finer scene details.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Audio-visual large language models (AV-LLMs) are designed to represent the temporal structure of audio and video, yet it remains unclear how effectively they use this temporal information to relate sounds to visual events. To examine this, we probe an AV-LLM on an audio-visual synchronization task by shifting the audio so that the queried sound overlaps a different visual event. We observe that the model still favors the originally aligned event over the newly aligned one, indicating limited sensitivity to changes in audio-visual temporal alignment. To address this, we propose Modality-Shift Contrastive Decoding (MSCD), a training-free decoding method that emphasizes audio-visual temporal alignment in model predictions. MSCD contrasts logits under preserved and disrupted temporal alignment to favor predictions supported by aligned audio and video. We disrupt this alignment by temporally shifting either the audio or the video, while keeping the other modality fixed. Extensive evaluations with two AV-LLMs across three benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of MSCD for temporally grounded audio-visual understanding. In particular, MSCD improves accuracy over the base models on Daily-Omni by 2.2 and 2.8 percentage points for OmniVinci and video-SALMONN 2+, respectively, suggesting that emphasizing temporal alignment can also benefit broader audio-visual understanding.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Infrared and visible images offer complementary cues for all-weather object detection, yet real-world sensor dropouts can leave one stream unavailable and degrade paired-input detectors. Meanwhile, existing modality-adaptive methods apply heavy cross-modal attention uniformly across scales, incurring prohibitive token overhead on high-resolution maps. We propose Admoab-DETR, a unified real-time detector supporting infrared-only, visible-only, and paired inputs with a single set of weights. Specifically, \emph{Resolution-Tiered Fusion} (RTF) allocates fusion capacity by spatial token density and feature granularity: a lightweight Spatial Modality Gate governs fine local fusion at the highest-resolution scale with zero-cost single-view pass-through, while channel-compressed deformable Admoab necks capture cross-modal correspondence at coarser semantic scales. Furthermore, \emph{Mode-Matched Feature Distillation} (MMFD) aligns multi-scale encoder features with an availability-synchronized full-fusion teacher, avoiding ill-posed cross-modal hallucination while cutting parameters by 18.1\% and paired latency by 39.6\% at zero inference overhead. We also construct Admoab, a strictly registered 20,000-pair infrared--visible benchmark across diverse scenes. Experiments show Admoab-DETR achieves state-of-the-art accuracy--efficiency trade-offs across missing-modality and paired benchmarks. Our code will be available at https://github.com/liwenchao0615/Admoab-DETR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Predicting the outcomes of robotic manipulation requires modeling both visual appearance and evolving 3D geometry. This poses two challenges: video compression can obscure fine-grained robot motion, while jointly predicting RGB and geometry demands sufficient generative capacity. We introduce Loop4D, a framework that addresses these challenges through motion-preserving 4D representations and recurrent generative refinement. Our 4D VAE encodes RGB and pointmap sequences into compact spatiotemporal latents while preserving motion details and geometric structure. Our RGB VAE combines geometry-guided supervision with predictive reconstruction to reduce compression-induced motion artifacts. Our geometry VAE uses lightweight adapters to adapt pretrained RGB representations while preserving their priors. Building on these representations, our Dual-Stream Looped Diffusion Transformer maintains separate appearance and geometry streams with shared Transformer weights and cross-modal connections. By reusing Transformer blocks across loops, we increase effective depth without replicating backbone parameters, enabling models with fewer parameters to improve joint RGB-geometry prediction through additional computation. Experiments demonstrate high-quality 4D generation and validate Loop4D in simulated robotic manipulation tasks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Self-supervised video representation learning has achieved remarkable progress by leveraging large-scale unlabeled video data. Recent methods commonly extend Masked Image Modeling (MIM) to Masked Video Modeling (MVM), where video representations are learned by capturing semantic information from temporal contexts during masked content recovery. Unlike static images, videos exhibit inherent temporal variation, as visual objects continuously change their positions, appearances, and motion states across frames. Therefore, MVM needs to preserve semantic consistency while capturing temporal changes of corresponding regions, which raises two central challenges: 1) how to establish reliable temporal correspondence across frames, and 2) how to formulate an appropriate representation learning objective for modeling both semantic consistency and temporal variation. To address these challenges, we propose Variation-aware Temporal Correspondence for video Representation learning (VT-CoRe) framework. For challenge 1), VT-CoRe introduces a sandwich sampling strategy to construct two-sided temporal references and a Patch Registration Module (PRM) to establish reliable patch-level correspondence. For challenge 2), VT-CoRe imposes latent-space semantic alignment on registered representations through a self-distillation architecture and further proposes temporal variation modeling to constrain representation trajectories over time. Experiments on 8 downstream tasks across 16 benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness and applicability of VT-CoRe across pixel-level, frame-level, video-level, and vision-based robotic learning scenarios. Complementary representation analysis shows that VT-CoRe learns a more structured latent space for variation-aware temporal correspondence learning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Real cameras are not pinholes, yet most dynamic Gaussian splatting assumes they are. Footage is therefore undistorted first, losing field of view or detail, especially with wide-angle and fisheye cameras. Static Gaussian splatting has largely solved this with ray-based rendering, which suits any camera. Native 4D Gaussians, which model motion in space-time, cannot use it directly: each Gaussian's orientation and size cannot be recovered reliably from its covariance. We present AnyCam4D, which trains native 4D Gaussians on original footage by ray-based rasterization, preserving field of view and resolution at about the per-step cost of standard projection. Our key contribution is a numerically stable way to obtain orientation and size: we condition a square root of the 4D covariance. Trained models render through other cameras, with rolling shutter, depth of field or chromatic aberration. With a synthetic 170-degree fisheye camera, training on original images improves peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) by 3.0-8.1 dB. On real footage, AnyCam4D has lower perceptual error than the standard projection adapted to each camera, at comparable PSNR and without camera-specific derivations. With the synthetic camera rotated 60-80 degrees away from the capture rig's view, it leads by 6.2 dB against ground truth.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Physical hand interaction depends on configuration, contact, and force, yet nearly identical appearances can conceal different contact states and exerted forces. We therefore formulate physical hand state estimation from egocentric video and wrist surface electromyography (sEMG), combining visual geometry with neuromuscular evidence to jointly recover these coupled components. To support this task, we introduce EgoMyo, a benchmark spanning 5.75M video frames, 50 participants, 34 contact configurations, and repeated donnings, synchronizing these inputs with 3D hand pose and with contact and normal force registered onto the hand surface from bare-hand pressure. Controlled analysis reveals that vision resolves spatial structure while sEMG better estimates force magnitude, that sEMG's gains concentrate in regions where vision is least reliable, and that contact localization limits force accuracy. Guided by these findings, we propose MyoBridge, which makes the hand region the unit of vision-sEMG fusion, lets each region gate its own share of sEMG, and distributes regional force magnitudes only over predicted contact vertices at inference. On held-out trials, MyoBridge reduces pose and force error by 39.8% and 18.5% over the strongest baseline and achieves the lowest pose error on unseen sessions and subjects.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Relighting reconstructed 3D scenes remains challenging due to the complex nature of light transport: slow or approximate visual feedback restricts the editor's ability to fine-tune the results to satisfaction. We present a hybrid relighting approach that enables interactive, high-fidelity updates of direct and indirect indoor illumination without per-edit optimization. Our approach permits the editor to make a wide range of relighting edits at room-scale, with near-instant feedback. Our key insight is to precompute geometric light exchange rather than illumination responses, retaining an inexpensive transport solve that accommodates changes in emission and surface reflectance. We evaluate direct illumination on reconstructed 2D Gaussians using visibility-aware sampling of one-sided emitters, while propagating diffuse interreflection through sparse radiosity on a triangle proxy. A sparse projection fitted through Gaussian alpha compositing transfers the proxy solution to the reconstruction. Our formulation further supports fast fixture transformations and emitter insertion by recomputing light source injection. Across eight relighting conditions of our synthetic test suite, our sub-second edits improve relighting fidelity over prior path-traced editing by an average of 5\,dB PSNR, while reducing editing latency by up to three orders of magnitude. Qualitative results further demonstrate realistic, interactive relighting of real-world captures.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Computer-using agents are increasingly expected to complete realistic user goals rather than isolated GUI actions. However, existing benchmarks typically isolate web, mobile, or desktop environments, leaving it unclear whether a single generalist agent can execute long-horizon workflows that span multiple operating systems and persistent application states. We introduce OS-Omni, a live cross-platform benchmark and execution environment for evaluating computer-using agents across Windows, Ubuntu, macOS, iOS, Android, and Web environments. OS-Omni is released in versioned waves of 300 tasks each; this paper focuses on the first release, v1, which contains 48 tasks per platform and 12 cross-platform workflows that pair a desktop with an Android phone. OS-Omni provides reproducible initialization, platform-specific adapters, trajectory logging, and executable final-state evaluators, and includes controlled service-backed applications for workflows involving account, cart, budget, calendar, message, and other persistent state. Human studies show that OS-Omni tasks require 67.54 interaction steps, substantially exceeding OSWorld references of 15.0 steps. On v1, the strongest evaluated agent, Claude Opus 5.5, reaches 68.5% average success, trailing the 77.3% human reference by 8.8 points; the gap is uneven across platforms, with frontier models exceeding humans on Linux but trailing them by more than 25 points on iOS and macOS. These results indicate that current agents remain far from platform-robust, long-horizon computer use, especially when success requires sustained state tracking and durable final-state changes.

Abstract (英文): Code-driven video generation asks a coding agent to write and run rendering programs. This gives exact control over typography, data visualizations, synchronized audio, and multi-shot scenes, and it leaves an editable project behind. A program that exits cleanly can still render overlapping labels, a camera aimed away from its subject, broken object interactions, or narration that drifts out of sync, and none of these raise a runtime error. Feeding rendered frames and audio back into the coding session bloats its long-horizon context, while critiquing only the finished full-resolution render turns early design mistakes into costly rewrites. We introduce VibeVideoAgent, a training-free framework that lets coding agents inspect their drafts throughout production. Multimodal perception runs in short-lived contexts kept apart from the coder and returns compact, grounded Findings. Seven primitives operate on a unified multi-clock representation of assets and drafts: they explore media from coarse to fine, verify quoted requirements with evidence from every required modality, compare a draft with a reference or an earlier revision on separate clocks, and measure stream signals locally. A lightweight staged protocol has the agent test its riskiest decision on a minimal preview first. On all 239 VibeVideoBench tasks, with matched agent runtimes and a fixed perception model, VibeVideoAgent improves holistic audiovisual quality (L3) for Claude Opus 5.5 (4.03 -> 4.43, while L2 rubric compliance rises from 98.87% to 99.35%), GPT-6 Astra (4.03 -> 4.27), and Claude Sonnet 5.5 (3.97 -> 4.10), with the largest gains in audio-visual synchronization (up to +0.56). Controlled ablations show that each component contributes, and trajectory audits show that the granularity of code edits governs the trade-off between rubric adherence and perceptual quality.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite time series capture how land surfaces change over time. Pixel-wise temporal foundation models read a year of satellite observations per pixel, yet public benchmarks lack a temporal axis or subsample it to a few dates. We introduce Analemma, a temporal benchmark of six datasets that carry every 10 and 20 m Sentinel-2 band and a time series spanning a growing season, completed where their releases omit geometry, dates, or cloud masks. One frozen-encoder protocol evaluates all models across label ratios and three seeds, with two baselines without pretraining. The TESSERA model ranks first overall. A from-scratch model on monthly composites outranks every foundation model except OlmoEarth v1.3-Base, suggesting that temporal input explains most of the performance. Abstract (英文): A detector scores candidate detections, but the recall and false alarms at a given confidence threshold depend on the input distribution. We introduce Detector Operating Behavior Inference (DOBI), a second-level inference task: estimating a fixed detector's threshold-dependent, distribution-level operating response from the internal evidence of a single unlabeled image. DOBI treats the detector's threshold response, i.e. recall, false positives, and precision across thresholds, as a latent behavioral state under the current data condition. Controlled data-space sampling probes this state space offline, and a lightweight monitor learns the inverse map from one image to this state, whose single functional output supports multiple operating policies. In a controlled proof of concept with a fixed YOLOv10-Nano person detector and COCO-derived degradations, predicted PR behavior reduces mean absolute deviation from 0.128 for an image-independent mean-behavior prior to 0.054, and predicted functional distances track empirical regime distances (Spearman 0.96). From one unlabeled image, the inferred behavior selects operating points as well as a non-deployable lookup that knows the true regime (macro-regime $F_1$ 0.6604 for both, versus 0.6407 under pristine calibration; oracle 0.6620). On this discrete benchmark, regime-supervised curve lookups are strong controls. Pre-specified continuous and compositional probing shows where functional inference matters: on unseen combinations, the functional decoder has a lower mean curve error than a template lookup, increasingly with distance from the training conditions, whereas the lookup is more accurate for interpolation. These results establish the feasibility of single-image inference of distribution-level detector behavior over the probed conditions. Source code is available at https://github.com/beckerio/dobi

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diffusion models excel at image and video generation, but iterative denoising incurs substantial inference latency. Recent token-adaptive acceleration methods select Taylor prediction orders for the full-model residual using shallow-layer proxy scores. However, candidate orders are evaluated on these proxy features but used to predict the full-model residual, creating a proxy--target mismatch: the proxy-preferred order may not minimize residual prediction error. To address this mismatch, we propose \textbf{TORE} (\textbf{T}aylor \textbf{O}rder selection via \textbf{R}esidual \textbf{E}rror estimation), which selects token-wise Taylor orders based on estimated prediction errors for the full-model residual. Yet direct error evaluation requires future ground-truth residuals, which are unavailable during order selection. TORE therefore estimates prediction error by analyzing how errors arise in Taylor extrapolation. Specifically, it decomposes prediction error into truncation error and finite-difference derivative error, then estimates both contributions from cached residuals to preselect token-wise orders for upcoming skip steps. Extensive experiments show that TORE achieves $4.31\times$ speedup on FLUX.1-dev and $4.18\times$ on Wan2.1 while maintaining high generation quality and fidelity.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Pretrained visual representations improve diffusion-transformer training, yet exploiting both global context and local spatial structure requires deciding which semantic states to model and how to access them. We introduce Semantic State Reading (SSR), which jointly denoises image latents, a global CLS token encoding image-level context, and a compact patch state preserving spatial correspondence. SSR couples this global-local modeling with distinct reading paths: local states are fused with corresponding image tokens at the input, while independent projections inject the current global state into every transformer block. Both semantic scopes thus remain accessible throughout denoising. Ablations show complementary benefits from local-state modeling and repeated global reading, and semantic-trajectory interventions alter image layout under a fixed class label. On ImageNet $256 \times 256$, SiT-XL/2 with SSR reaches or surpasses the reference FIDs of SiT, REPA, and Representation Entanglement for Generation (REG) with $100\times$, $40\times$, and $2\times$ fewer training iterations, respectively. SSR achieves unguided FID 1.78 at 2M iterations and FID 1.34 with classifier-free guidance alone. Combining classifier-free guidance with autoguidance yields FID 1.31 at 1M iterations, achieving the best FID in our main comparison table.

Abstract (英文): Physically based rendering (PBR) material estimation facilitates relighting, material editing, and 3D content creation. As the reflectance of a surface heavily depends on whether it is a metal or a dielectric, producing any map that determines albedo involves a decision on metalness, which we call the "intended metalness" of the map. Rendering maps with mismatched intended metalness yields physically implausible or misinterpreted materials. The metallic--roughness workflow makes metalness explicit and thus encourages base color to match it, but does not enforce this match, especially for automated methods directly predicting each map. Therefore, we propose ReMeDi, which enforces the match through two components. First, we reparameterize base color as a metalness-weighted blend of its two variants, estimated under the all-metal and all-dielectric hypothesis, respectively. Second, as available supervision cannot teach a single network both hypotheses at every pixel, we restrict each of two separate networks to one hypothesis, so that each variant is ensured to follow its hypothesis. Besides enforcing the match, this also improves each estimated material map. Experiments show that ReMeDi outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both material accuracy and relighting fidelity.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Artistic mesh generators turn a dense 3D object into an artist mesh: a compact, quad-dominant mesh that is easy to edit. Most of current models are auto-regressive methods which decode a mesh token by token, take tens to hundreds of seconds per mesh. Diffusion- and flow-based generators are much faster, but their meshes still fall short of artist quality. To our knowledge, PostMesh is the first post-training method for a diffusion- or flow-based artist mesh generator, and it needs no expensive artist-made ground truth: the generator is trained on its own samples, scored by a reward computed from the dense input and the output. We propose an artistic mesh reward that keeps the output consistent with the dense input while making the mesh structure into artistic. We also propose a multi-negative flow preference optimisation that transfers the reward to the generator. After a short LoRA post-training, PostMesh is comparable to or better than slow autoregressive generators on most artist-mesh quality metrics and in a blind VLM preference test, at a fraction of their time.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Robust image-text retrieval aims to maintain accurate cross-modal matching when image or text queries are incomplete, supporting reliable information access in real-world applications. However, existing methods typically focus on adapting to distribution shifts, without adequately addressing candidate discrimination for incomplete queries and ranking instability. To this end, we propose semantic projection and alignment with relational constraints (SPARC), which elicits latent multimodal knowledge from frozen encoders to improve candidate discrimination and ranking stability. Specifically, we first devise a semantic calibration module on a product of spheres that refines incomplete visual and textual features through tangent-space updates, maintaining the unit-sphere structure used for cosine similarity. Second, we introduce a robust semantic guidance module that combines latent multimodal knowledge encoded in complete image-text features with cross-modal semantic anchors to promote semantic alignment of the calibrated features. Finally, we introduce a matching relation constraint module that fuses original query features with their calibrated counterparts and applies a soft ranking constraint to penalize reductions in initially positive matching margins, thereby limiting ranking perturbations. Extensive experiments on Flickr30K and COCO demonstrate that SPARC outperforms competing methods under various image occlusion and word deletion settings, while achieving consistent gains across different pretrained backbones.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Embodied agents operating around people must not only recognize social cues, but also decide when the available evidence is sufficient, whether to intervene, and how to respond without violating social norms. Existing benchmarks typically evaluate these abilities in isolation or with access to completed videos, obscuring where an online social decision fails. We introduce AIRIS, a causal streaming benchmark that formulates social embodied intelligence as a time-anchored chain of Attention, Intention, Reaction, and Interaction (A-I-R-I). AIRIS contains 8,000 expert-curated questions grounded in 357 egocentric clips across 11 context categories and 36 capability dimensions. At each query, models receive only the observation prefix available at that time; decision-aware metrics reward calibrated waiting and complete reactions while penalizing premature commitment. Evaluating 45 proprietary and open-source general-purpose, embodied, streaming-video, and omni-modal models reveals fragmented and temporally miscalibrated competence. General-purpose VLMs excel at passive cue grounding and intent recognition, whereas embodied specialists lead on isolated reaction sub-decisions; yet across all families, models frequently guess intents before sufficient evidence emerges and fail to jointly align intervention timing, action domain, and concrete execution within the same episode. AIRIS thus provides a diagnostic foundation for bridging the gap between passive social perception and reliable embodied interaction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-Language-Action (VLA) and World Action Models (WAMs) are vulnerable to adversarial visual interference, motivating the study of universal and transferable physical patch attacks. We propose Object-Associated Patch Attack (OAPA), a universal and transferable physical patch framework that enables an attacker to select which task object's manipulation to disrupt through patch placement, without re-optimizing the patch. Our framework combines object-associated placement, differentiable physical compositing, and final action-expert representation divergence. Physical compositing accounts for perspective and occlusion across camera views, while representation divergence guides patch optimization using either relative RMS or cosine distance. Experimental results demonstrate strong attack transferability across task suites and policies, together with the ability to selectively disrupt manipulation of an attacker-chosen task object through patch placement.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monocular 3D bounding box tracking must account for both object motion and changes in camera viewpoint. Framewise box lifting with temporal filtering leaves these motion components implicit, while static-scene fusion assumes stationary objects. We introduce MoCA4D, a framework for 3D box lifting and tracking from monocular video, associated 2D box prompts, and camera intrinsics. An observation branch estimates per-frame geometry, while a dynamic branch predicts a camera transformation shared across objects and a separate rigid transformation for each object. We propose Star Optimization to refine current depth and orientation using the observation and historical boxes propagated through these transformations. The refined box then guides subsequent propagation. Across all evaluated objects in Argoverse 2 and ADT, Star Optimization reduces center motion error by 38.1% and 38.7%, respectively, relative to Kalman filtering of the same observations. On MOVi-F, the dynamic branch achieves the lowest corner and rotation errors and the highest IoU among the evaluated methods over eight-transition motion windows. Real-data camera-only controls further link predicted object motion to improved localization on dynamic-object tracks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Failure analyses of monocular depth models often name a cause for each error, such as an occluding boundary, a thin object or a reflective surface, but such labels are rarely tested. We present the Vision Error Observatory (VEO), an audit that finds regions failing the $δ_1$ criterion on sensor-measured pixels, labels each with a likely cause or abstains, and then subjects the labels to four tests: agreement with object annotations on data the label model never saw, agreement with blinded human raters, response to targeted image edits, and value as a training signal. We apply it to eight models on NYU Depth V2, DIODE and iBims-1, with labels also checked on Hypersim, and fix most decision rules before their results. The occluding-boundary label agrees with annotations (AUROC 0.85 on NYU, 0.81 on Hypersim, 0.69 on iBims-1), but a single depth-edge cue does as well, and most boundary failures of the strongest model vanish under a 2-pixel tolerance. The reflection label does not transfer to synthetic data, the textureless label is near chance against annotations, and only one of four edit tests, an exploratory one, supports its label. Evaluated against five blinded raters, VEO's labels matched the human consensus only at chance level (Cohen's $κ$ = 0.05). In addition, an increase in the weight on the diagnosed regions during fine-tuning led to an increase in the error on targeted pixels at every weight tested. The architecture-specific failure fingerprint claimed by an earlier version of this project also failed to replicate across the datasets. Plausible failure explanations, in short, need to be tested before they are trusted; we release the protocol, code, ratings and every result file.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In one-step super-resolution (SR), vision-only models extract semantic cues directly from the low-resolution (LR) image, keeping those cues spatially aligned with the structures they help restore. Recent models achieve high restoration quality with generators of up to 1.4 billion parameters. Given how much structure the LR image already provides, could a much smaller generator achieve comparable quality? We introduce SPGSR, a vision-only framework that adds a learned RGB correction to the LR image. A frozen DINOv2 guides prediction, and a lightweight block refines the correction locally. SPGSR achieves higher PSNR/SSIM and lower LPIPS/DISTS than VOSR, a leading vision-only SR method, across three benchmarks. Its $31.8\mathrm{M}$-parameter generator is just $6.5\%$ the size of VOSR-0.5B's. SPGSR also achieves competitive quality against T2I-based SR methods. SPGSR's generator uses only $0.60\%$ of the HR pixels used to train and distill VOSR's generator. For $512\times512$ outputs, SPGSR achieves $2.80\times$ and $3.35\times$ GPU-pipeline speedups over VOSR-0.5B and VOSR-1.4B, respectively.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multi-page document vision-language models encode each page independently, creating a feature-level discontinuity at page boundaries: visual tokens are formed without access to content on adjacent pages, and the language model must reason over these page representations formed in isolation. We introduce DocSEAM (Document Selective Enrichment via Anchored Masking), a lightweight module inserted between a pretrained vision encoder and the language model that injects the inductive bias of local cross-page context into visual representations. For each page, DocSEAM forms a local triplet with its two neighbors, applies center-anchored masked self-attention with directional salience gates to fuse information from neighbor pages, and returns only the enriched center-page tokens, leaving the visual token count unchanged. After a supervised cross-page warm-up and multi-page fine-tuning with DocSEAM frozen, our 9B-parameter model achieves state-of-the-art results among the evaluated open-weight models of up to 10B parameters on five multi-page document VQA benchmarks. We further show that under controlled re-pagination, DocSEAM reduces sensitivity to boundary placement and improves cross-page span localization

</details>

Abstract (英文): World models and vision-language-action policy models are increasingly proposed as foundations for robot manipulation. They predict how scenes evolve and which actions to take, yet successful manipulation depends not solely on following observable trajectories but also on the hidden variable of interaction force. Neurophysiology has shown that humans possess a visual prior for this hidden variable, motivating us to ask the question: Do the representations of visual foundation models also capture the forces involved in an interaction? We define force awareness as the ability of representations to (1) indicate when contact force is applied, (2) track how it changes during an interaction, and (3) reflect how large it is. We probe frozen image, video, world- and policy model representations with a lightweight readout trained on synchronized video and force measurements from two manipulation datasets. Our experiments show that most models capture when contact occurs but not how much force is being applied. Surprisingly, robot post-training does not improve this either, highlighting an important gap in current models capabilities.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Instruction-guided image editing has a wide range of applications, yet existing models may inadvertently alter content that should remain unchanged. Reliable consistency evaluation is therefore essential for assessing content preservation and providing reward signals for Reinforcement Learning (RL). However, existing reward models may overlook subtle visual changes or fail to distinguish necessary modifications from unintended alterations, limiting the reliability of consistency assessment. To address this challenge, we propose PreserveReward, a consistency reward model for image editing that integrates visual On-Policy Self-Distillation (OPSD) to enhance fine-grained visual perception and consistency assessment. PreserveReward learns from reference preservation scores through a regression-based scoring loss and employs two complementary teacher views for self-distillation. The local teacher uses matched crops to identify subtle visual changes, while the joint teacher combines local details with global context to determine whether these changes align with the editing intent. To improve the reliability of distillation supervision, we calibrate teacher score distributions via minimum-KL projection under reference-score error constraints and adaptively select teacher process supervision based on gradient alignment with the scoring objective, mitigating scoring bias and optimization conflicts. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that PreserveReward achieves strong alignment with human preference on non-edited content preservation and generalizes well across diverse editing scenarios.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Precise 9-DoF pose control is essential for visual content generation, enabling users to specify object location, size, and orientation in 3D space. However, existing methods typically focus on either text-to-image (T2I) or text-to-video (T2V) generation, with limitations in pose controllability and temporal coherence. We present SceneDesigner++, a unified framework for 9-DoF pose control across both T2I and T2V generation. We construct ObjectPose9D++, a dataset containing high-quality images and videos with accurate pose annotations. Trained on ObjectPose9D++, SceneDesigner++ combines absolute and relative pose encoding strategies to decouple frame-wise spatial grounding from inter-frame motion modeling. Specifically, absolute pose encoding leverages CNOCS map sequences to provide explicit, frame-wise 9-DoF pose guidance. Meanwhile, our relative pose encoding, termed VPRoPE, incorporates relative pose information into rotary position embeddings to explicitly model inter-frame pose transitions, thereby promoting temporally coherent object motion. A learnable orthogonal transformation is further introduced for each attention head, allowing different heads to capture complementary geometric relationships while preserving input feature norms to promote training stability. We further adopt a two-stage training strategy to learn the absolute and relative pose encoding mechanisms separately. Benefiting from this lightweight and decoupled design, SceneDesigner++ also supports integration with an independently trained camera-control module, enabling separate or joint control over object and camera motion. Finally, we introduce T2IVPoseEval, a benchmark for evaluating diverse object poses and motion patterns in both T2I and T2V tasks. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SceneDesigner++ consistently outperforms same-backbone baselines in pose fidelity and motion quality.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A medical video language model should answer from the visual evidence its question asks about. We find three places where this breaks down and address each with an approach we call MedEvidU. First, benchmarks reward answers that ignore the video: on one dataset, the position of a clip within its recording predicts the surgical step in 37% of questions, and a constant answer beats every trained model on a standard safety-assessment metric. We build MedVidBench-v2, 34 tasks from 18 surgical and clinical video datasets, in which prompts and metrics are audited with answers given without video, and which tests unseen hospitals and windows cut blindly from whole recordings. Second, common video encoders merge consecutive frames into shared tokens, so a question about one frame is answered from a blend of two. Query-slot inputs give each queried frame its own tokens at almost no extra cost. Third, a fine-tuned model has memorized its training questions, which leaves little to learn from. Label-matched question resampling (LQR) adds unseen questions about the same recordings while keeping the answer distribution fixed, in a final supervised epoch and in reinforcement learning. Together, these raise the grounding IoU of a 4B model from 30.4 to 44.4, above a supervised 27B model, and instrument grounding at an unseen hospital from 33.9 to 72.5. On blindly cut windows, event-presence accuracy improves by 5.5 points and localization F1 by 8.1 points. Project page: https://gaozhongpai.github.io/MedEvidU/

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large vision-language models (LVLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities in handling multi-modal downstream tasks, gaining increasing popularity. However, recent studies show that LVLMs are susceptible to both intentional and inadvertent attacks. Existing attackers ideally optimize adversarial perturbations with backpropagated gradients from LVLMs, thus limiting their scalability in practical scenarios as real-world LVLM applications will not provide any LVLM's gradient or details. Motivated by this research gap and counter-practical phenomenon, we propose a novel hard-label attack method for LVLMs, named HardPatch, to generate visual adversarial patches by solely querying the model. Our method provides deeper insights into how to investigate the vulnerability of LVLMs in local visual regions and generate corresponding adversarial substitution under the practical yet challenging hard-label setting. Specifically, we first split each image into uniform patches and mask each of them to individually assess their sensitivity to the LVLM model. Then, according to the descending order of sensitive scores, we iteratively select the most vulnerable patch to initialize noise and estimate gradients with further additive random noises for optimization. In this manner, multiple patches are perturbed until the altered image satisfies the adversarial condition. Extensive LVLM models and datasets are evaluated to demonstrate the adversarial nature of HardPatch. Our empirical observations suggest that with appropriate patch substitution and optimization, HardPatch can craft effective adversarial images to attack hard-label LVLMs.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent feed-forward approaches mark a paradigm shift in novel view synthesis by eliminating the reliance on per-scene optimization, directly inferring 3D representations from input views. However, their reliance on global scene-token context incurs substantial memory and latency overhead, constraining their ability to handle increasingly dense source-view contexts. Compressing source representations offers an intuitive way to limit this token budget. Prior general-purpose compression methods are not tailored for novel view synthesis, as their fixed, target-agnostic nature fails to adapt evidence allocation to individual target views. To overcome this, we introduce TACC, a target-aware compression module tailored for feed-forward latent NVS. Specifically, TACC organizes each source view into a progressive carrier hierarchy. Driven by source-target geometric relevance, it adaptively retrieves tokens to dynamically allocate exact computational quotas. This selective retrieval directly yields a compact, target-conditioned representation. Extensive experiments on the RE10K demonstrate that under dense input settings, TACC achieves $9.23\times$ and $64\times$ higher Effective FPS than Full Context on LagerNVS and LVSM, respectively. Effective FPS excludes one-time source encoding and Carrier construction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Path-traced rendering shows CT and MR volumes with realistic lighting, but it restarts after every change of view, clipping plane or transfer function (TF), the map from voxel intensity to colour and opacity, which limits the frame rate. Feed-forward Gaussian splatting models such as RenderFM predict the Gaussians directly from the volume instead of fitting them to each scan, and are fast. RenderFM, however, handles only CT, runs its network again after every TF edit, has no clipping operator, and places its Gaussians on a uniform 3-mm grid that blurs organ boundaries and cut faces. We present RenderFM-Next, a feed-forward model that addresses these limits. One network handles both CT and MR. Its Gaussians are predicted once per volume: a new TF needs no pass of this network, and a clipping plane cuts them exactly, using operators from prior work. The model predicts coarse 3-mm Gaussians for the whole volume and refines them to 1.5 mm only where refinement improves the image. A small score head ranks the coarse Gaussians by how much refining them would reduce the rendering error; at render time the user chooses how many are refined, and those next to a clipping plane are always refined, which sharpens cut faces. We build a benchmark of about 2,000 CT and MR scans with path-traced references under clinical presets, TF edits and clipping. Our RenderFM-Next improves over RenderFM retrained on this benchmark by 2.8 dB on CT and 2.4 dB on MR and renders a 1600x1600 view in 4-6 ms. Refinement adds 0.30 / 0.69 dB over the same model trained without it (0.51 / 1.17 dB on cut faces), and refining a learned 10% of the coarse Gaussians matches a random 50% with 2.4x fewer Gaussians. Project page: https://gaozhongpai.github.io/renderfmnext/

</details>

Abstract (英文): Upgrading feed-forward geometry to metric scale with visual-inertial odometry (VIO) raises the question of which correction should align dense feed-forward prediction with sparse metric VIO anchors. Existing methods use either a similarity transformation, which cannot represent depth-dependent distortion, or a projective transformation, which alters pre-calibrated intrinsics. We prove that the transformations compatible with pre-calibrated intrinsics form a ten-dimensional manifold. Beyond Sim(3), this manifold contains three degrees of freedom (DoF) that locate the plane at infinity and leave residuals growing quadratically with depth range after any similarity alignment. These DoF are invisible in the image and can be resolved only by metric depth along viewing rays or by multi-view constraints. To estimate them, we propose ProManO, a probabilistic manifold optimization framework in which every anchor is weighted by its along-ray uncertainty and inlier probability within a single expectation-maximization (EM) procedure. ProManO certifies the similarity synchronization and refines the plane at infinity only where it is observable, which guarantees an objective value no worse than that of the optimal similarity alignment. Experiments on EuRoC, CID-SIMS, Oxford Spires, and KITTI-360 demonstrate that ProManO achieves the highest mean F1 and outperforms Sim(3) alignment on all four datasets. The project page is available at https://h0xg.github.io/ProManO/.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A first-person (ego) image reveals what a camera sees, but not who or what carries it. Given a synchronized third-person (exo) image, camera wearer identification asks who is ego: which candidate carries the ego camera? We extend this task beyond humans to robots, vehicles, animals, and drones, and broaden its spatial scope (where is ego) from selecting a provided candidate mask to wearer segmentation with absence prediction and mutual camera localization across ego-exo views. We introduce FindEgo-1M, comprising 1.16M real and simulated ego-exo pairs across 12 wearer types, 17 domains, and 111 test settings. Our approach exploits a geometric relation: the ego camera's projection into the exo image, i.e., the epipole, lies on or near its wearer. We introduce EgoRay-ZS, the first method to turn a frozen feed-forward 3D reconstruction model, VGGT-$Ω$, into a training-free solution for wearer identification and segmentation. Its geometric readout ranks candidate masks or segmentation proposals by angular offset and distance to the ego camera, while the relative pose provides mutual camera directions. We then develop the full EgoRay model through pose-supervised training on FindEgo-1M and label-free test-time adaptation constrained by candidate masks. Without task-specific training, EgoRay-ZS achieves 78.0% identification accuracy on TF2023's original labels, versus 52.9% reported for the dataset-trained PEN. On FindEgo-1M, EgoRay achieves state-of-the-art chance-corrected accuracy of 87.1 and 77.7 on seen and unseen settings. On matched queries, EgoRay also outperforms GPT-6 Astra by 31.6 and 21.3 points on seen and unseen settings, respectively, while running $18\times$ faster. Data, code, and models will be released.

</details>

Abstract (英文): It is commonly accepted that high-level concepts are represented linearly in the representation space of large neural networks. For Large Vision Language Models (LVLMs), finding those interpretable directions in visual representations is important for interpretability. To find them, the classical approach is to train a linear classifier on the representations, but it needs a large amount of labels and only recovers what can be read from the representations, not what the model actually uses. In this paper we start from the following insight: The LLM Decoder of an LVLM is also a classifier whose classes are the possible next tokens. So we use its first-order Taylor expansion as a linear classifier. Hence, we introduce Decoder-as-a-Probe (DaP), which significantly outperforms classical probing and steering baselines especially when training data is sparse. (1) It verbalizes what an LVLM perceives in a given image. (2) It can surgically change the perception of the LVLM about specific attributes (e.g. change only the color, the clothes worn or the action done). Due to its simplicity and data efficiency, we believe DaP can serve as a plug-and-play tool for LVLM research, such as interpretability, hallucination mitigation or instruction tuning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Classifying a whole-slide image requires encoding thousands of high-resolution tiles with a pathology foundation model, which accounts for most of the inference cost. Existing efficient methods reduce this cost by selecting fewer tiles, but typically train a new model around their selection strategy. A largely unexplored alternative is to keep an existing full-bag model frozen and run it on selected tiles at the same encoder cost. We introduce this comparison, alongside random tile sampling at matched cost. To select tiles, we fit a lightweight selector with 3.3K parameters to the attention of a frozen model, without additional slide labels. The selector ranks regions from a slide thumbnail, encodes the top K regions at 5x magnification, and selects b for processing at 20x. We evaluate seven frozen MIL aggregators on six tasks and compare them with nine efficient methods trained on the same features and splits. At a budget of (K,b)=(1000,10), encoding costs about 6.6% of the full bag and is 10-14x faster, excluding tiling. The AUROC drop is no more than 0.005 in 33 of 36 aggregator-task pairs. At (500,10), the cost falls to about 4% and inference is 13-22x faster, but AUROC drops by up to 0.035 on CAMELYON17, where small metastases are easily missed. Selection improves over random sampling by about 0.10 AUROC on tasks with focal lesions, while random sampling performs well on diffuse tasks. In our comparisons, the frozen model at (500,10) is ahead of nine efficient methods in 55 of 60 paired comparisons. Giving an existing WSI model fewer tiles is thus a baseline that a new efficient model should be compared with at matched encoder cost.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Collaborative reconstruction with consumer phones promises faster and more complete indoor maps. However, heterogeneous devices introduce degraded observations and unreliable cross-device associations, so a single poor contributor or incorrect match can corrupt the shared Gaussian map. To address this, we propose \textbf{CADENCE}, a collaborative Gaussian simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM) system that separates finding cross-device matches from deciding whether and how peer observations update the map. To find matches, a sensor-grounded regional memory summarizes committed observations and ranks candidate peer regions using appearance, relative pose, gravity, barometric altitude, and observation quality. Deterministic geometric verification and optional physical checks then decide which of the proposed matches are accepted. Once a match is accepted, separate cue-based confidences control how its observations contribute to pose constraints, geometry, appearance, and memory. As a result, useful depth can still contribute even when color is unreliable. Throughout this process, local tracking remains isolated from mapping, verification, and networking. We evaluate CADENCE on eight Matterport3D houses that were not used to train the memory. On these houses, regional memory achieves an area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) of $0.95$ to $0.97$ and thus outperforms appearance retrieval, while verification keeps false-link rates below $0.5\,\%$. Furthermore, soft confidence weighting reduces the mean receiver peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) loss from $2.66$ to $1.05$\,dB across eight degradations while retaining useful peer depth. Finally, using inertial and platform motion, CADENCE tracks all twelve held-out iPhone scans with a mean absolute trajectory error of $6.98$\,cm, and its refined maps achieve better average rendering quality than the baselines.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generative models enable unrestricted adversarial attacks that aim to fool classifiers while preserving the natural appearance and semantics of the original image. However, existing diffusion-based black-box attacks largely rely on transferability from surrogates, making their effectiveness sensitive to differences between surrogate and victim. Improving transferability can also distort semantic content and degrade image quality. Moreover, retaining the original prompt to preserve fidelity leaves the diffusion model's conditional prior underused for adversarial guidance. We address these limitations by adapting score-based black-box attacks to reverse diffusion, using the victim's output probabilities to guide latent updates without accessing its gradients. We further introduce adaptive-target guidance, which uses the victim's current runner-up class as an adversarial condition to steer denoising towards the decision boundary. Because such guidance can introduce unwanted semantic changes, we also propose target-search guidance, which searches for a small perturbation of the original condition embedding using a score-based attack. This achieves comparable attack effectiveness with better image quality than runner-up guidance. Experiments on the ImageNet-Compatible dataset show that our framework outperforms transfer-based baselines in both attack effectiveness and image quality.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Validating industrial oxy-combustion burner designs traditionally requires costly experimental campaigns in which each operating condition is physically reproduced on an instrumented rig and imaged via OH* chemiluminescence, rendering iterative design exploration prohibitively slow. This work develops deep generative surrogate models that map a 15-dimensional vector of continuous operating parameters (thermal power, swirl numbers, momentum ratios, mixture fractions, etc.) directly to a $512\times64$ heat-release flame image, trained on 1,609 experimental samples from the OXYTEC dual-swirl non-premixed oxy-combustion database. Two complementary generative paradigms are investigated, as they offer opposite strengths for surrogate modelling: a custom spatially-anchored conditional least-squares GAN (cLSGAN-L1-CBN), whose single-pass generation enables near-instantaneous inference, and a custom physics-guided denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM), whose iterative sampling favours the smooth, continuous heat-release gradients of fluid flows. Both are validated through ablation on a stratified held-out test set. The optimized cLSGAN reaches RMSE 0.022 and FID 20.68 and generates an image in about 20 ms on a mobile workstation GPU, though extended training leaves its texture realism unchanged; the DDPM improves steadily over long training horizons, reaching RMSE 0.019 and FID 20.29 at 40,000 epochs, at the cost of roughly thirty seconds per image on the same hardware --- three orders of magnitude slower than the adversarial surrogate. A nearest-neighbour analysis traces the persistent worst-case errors to sparsely sampled regions of the parameter space, and a parameter-sensitivity study confirms smooth, physically consistent responses to perturbed inputs. These results demonstrate that carefully guided generative models can act as reliable visual surrogates for combustion experiments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Industrial anomaly detection (IAD), the task of identifying and localizing deviations from normal product conditions, is essential to automated quality inspection in manufacturing. Many vision-language models for IAD rely on large domain-specific datasets for fine-tuning and make predictions in a single pass, leaving no opportunity to re-examine ambiguous regions. Although tool-using agents can revisit these regions, predefined tool interfaces limit how they can combine intermediate results in a follow-up check. When such checks are insufficient, models may miss subtle defects or misidentify the defect type. To address this limitation, we introduce ADClaw, an inspection agent that uses executable code to test anomaly hypotheses without task-specific fine-tuning. For each hypothesis, a Structured Normality Prior identifies the applicable rules and retrieves normal visual references for the inspection target. Guided by this prior, the agent writes and executes code in a persistent Inspection Workbench to combine masks, anomaly maps, and measurements from earlier steps into a new test of the suspected defect. After each check, Hypothesis-Guided Evidence Reasoning assesses whether the evidence supports the suspected defect and its location, using gaps or inconsistencies in that evidence to guide the next code action. ADClaw achieves competitive results in detection and localization across 2D and 3D industrial anomaly detection benchmarks and in anomaly understanding on MMAD.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The severe susceptibility of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to hallucinations poses a critical threat to their reliability, making a rigorous evaluation framework indispensable. However, existing evaluation pipelines largely rely on predefined test items, suffering from high construction costs, rapid obsolescence as MLLMs evolve, and ineffectiveness in probing model-specific weaknesses. To address these limitations, we propose SEAM, a self-evolving and adaptive probing framework for automatic MLLM hallucination evaluation. Specifically, SEAM introduces a composable Hallucination Probe Construction Library comprising ten hallucination-probing skills, three elicitation wrappers, and five question formats. Building on this library, SEAM establishes a dual-loop framework. In the inner loop (adaptive probing), skill-guided visual analysis, question composition, and hierarchical quality verification are coordinated with verifier and target-response feedback to refine test items and uncover model-specific hallucinations. In the outer loop (skill evolution), a skill updater distills historical probing trajectories into localized, reusable rule updates, which undergo held-out validation and are frozen before testing. Driven by these innovative structural designs, SEAM establishes an automated and self-evolving evaluation paradigm. Experiments across four target MLLMs and ten hallucination types demonstrate that SEAM substantially outperforms representative static benchmarks, achieving an 89.2\% relative improvement in mean valid hallucination discovery rate across 12 matched comparisons.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We introduce a new model for real-time streaming captioning on a single GPU. YoloMT is a 449.8M-parameter streaming captioner that provides Pareto-frontier performance among generative vision models in its class. Distilled from a 27B teacher by SFT and GRPO, it leads every model under 1B parameters on OVO-Bench, MVBench, StreamingBench and Video-MME, answered from its captions, and beats LFM2.5-VL-1.6B and SmolVLM2-2.2B. YoloMT is designed to read more frames cheaply and make them informative, encoding each frame once and caching it as 16 tokens. This allows the model to incorporate and infer from past frames without additional vision passes, making it efficient and effective. We also provide a detailed recipe for training YoloMT, with its stages and data, as well as ablations that demonstrate the contribution of each component.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Dense global attention is crucial for capturing cross-view geometry in visual geometry foundation models, yet its quadratic complexity limits efficient inference on long image sequences. Existing sparse attention methods primarily select entries of the global attention matrix based on attention probabilities and construct masks independently across layers, overlooking value redundancy and cross-layer information reuse. By analyzing attention distributions and clustering value representations across layers, we reveal two forms of \emph{cross-layer consistency}, characterized by highly similar attention probability distributions and localized changes in value redundancy structures. Building on these observations, we propose \textbf{DuCo}, a training-free sparse attention framework that exploits historical attention information and value redundancy for efficient geometric inference. Specifically, DuCo leverages historical attention information to estimate current-layer dense attention probabilities and introduces value clustering to exploit redundancy, with clusters efficiently refined through local updates across layers. Both factors are integrated through probability-weighted value distortion to jointly optimize four cluster representation states under an attention budget for sparse mask construction. Experiments across three visual geometry foundation models and five benchmarks demonstrate favorable accuracy--efficiency trade-offs.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models introduce language reasoning into end-to-end autonomous driving. They output a language decision together with a trajectory, which makes the planner more interpretable. However, in existing methods the language decision is either an independent output or an index that selects among similar candidate trajectories, and its annotations are not verified against the trajectory. As a result, the language decision and the trajectory are often inconsistent in closed-loop driving. To address this, we propose Commit2Drive, a framework that imposes the language decision as a consistency constraint on trajectory planning. The decision is a commitment, a maneuver class with a continuous parameter that one deterministic rule reads from any trajectory, and it is grounded in facts reported by the perception of the model itself. A Maneuver Bank maps the commitment to a code with a kinematic meaning, and consistency losses train the planner to execute every admissible decision of a scene, so that the trajectory follows the decision. An automatic data pipeline supplies the supervision. Decisions are labeled from the future motion of the expert, counterfactual executions train the decision and the trajectory together, and rule-based functions with a Vision-Language Model (VLM) generate, review and refine the language annotations. The code is a lookup that involves no hidden state of the language model, so Commit2Drive is extensible to rule-based decisions, which the same planner executes without retraining. Commit2Drive uses a 0.5B language model and runs closed-loop inference online on a single consumer graphics card. On Bench2Drive, Commit2Drive reaches a Driving Score (DS) of 78.50 and a Success Rate (SR) of 55.91% with a lightweight model, which is competitive with the state of the art, and greatly reduces the language-action inconsistency. Abstract (英文): Camera-controlled video generation anticipates how a scene appears from new viewpoints by relying on a prior learned implicitly from large-scale data. Ultrasound poses the same problem, with the probe pose as the viewpoint, but under opposite conditions: anatomy offers an explicit prior that every sonographer relies on, whereas tracked scans are too scarce to learn it implicitly. As a result, pose control alone is not enough, and pose-conditioned ultrasound generators produce implausible anatomy in unscanned regions and inconsistent anatomy on revisits. We go beyond pose control and give the generator the two kinds of knowledge that make up a sonographer's mental model. An anatomical prior, built from a population of subjects, guides generation where nothing has been scanned, and is refined online by every generated chunk towards the scanned subject. A 3D latent memory, designed around the cross-sectional physics of ultrasound, compounds the generated slices into a volume of the patient and reslices it at new poses, keeping revisited anatomy consistent. The fine-grained memory enters the generator at the input, while the coarse anatomy prior guides the layout across transformer blocks. Experiments on thyroid, kidney, and lumbar spine show improved visual fidelity, pose following, and anatomical consistency over pose-conditioned and memory-augmented baselines.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Emergent cross-modal classification and retrieval enable transfer between modalities without direct paired supervision. However, existing methods often prioritize sample-level alignment, overlooking local discriminative information and partwise statistical relations. To this end, we propose partwise reasoning across modalities (PRAM), which activates latent multimodal knowledge in frozen encoders by composing partwise statistical relations through a shared pivot modality. Specifically, we first present a multi-granularity covariance regularization module that estimates covariances from global and local vectors in two collections of modality-pivot pairs, stabilizing local estimates through adaptive blending with their cross-part means. Second, we devise a pivot-based spectral relational reasoning module that infers global and partwise statistical relations between target modalities through regularized covariance composition and extracts paired projection directions and their correlation strengths via whitening and spectral decomposition. Finally, we introduce a multi-granularity score fusion module that standardizes and fuses matching scores from global and local relations, incorporating task-specific information on pivot associations and class semantics for class prediction and candidate ranking. Extensive experiments across ten datasets demonstrate that PRAM achieves state-of-the-art performance in 16 classification settings and 19 retrieval settings, while exhibiting highly competitive performance in three sensing scenarios.

</details>

Abstract (英文): An image classifier names the object in an image, and its accuracy is the share of images named correctly. A classifier that names a car on a road may miss the same car pasted onto clouds. Composite benchmarks measure such failures by pasting objects cut from photographs onto backgrounds from other photographs. A pairing puts every object label on a different background label, such as truck on sea. The pairing on which some classifiers do worst, their hardest pairing, makes a strict test, but may be hard only for them or only on the photographs used to choose it. We show that the hardest pairing of some classifiers stays hard for others and on new photographs. We split 40 ImageNet classifiers into halves balanced by reported ImageNet accuracy. One half picks its hardest pairing of 8 object and 8 background labels from Common Objects in Context (COCO). The other half, held out of the choice, compares that pairing with random pairings of the same objects and backgrounds. For the 14 held-out classifiers with little net accuracy change after pasting objects back onto their own backgrounds, mean accuracy on composites from new photographs falls from 50.3% over random pairings to 43.7% on the hardest pairing, 6.6 percentage points lower. A benchmark using these composites can compare this pool's held-out classifiers on the fixed and random pairings.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This paper introduces Atunda, the first systematically collected video dataset of Afrogenic dance moves, designed for training and evaluating deep learning models for movement classification. The first phase of the dataset includes nearly 400 videos covering 13 distinct dance moves from African and African Diaspora performative culture, making it the first AI-ready resource of its kind. Using this dataset, we train and evaluate various configurations of temporal convolutional neural networks, achieving a classification accuracy exceeding 95%. The baseline results provide a benchmark for future studies in movement classification, especially in the understudied area of Afrogenic performative culture and tradition. This work lays the foundation for developing in the future digital metrics of similarity, automated attribution systems, and tools that preserve and promote the cultural heritage of Afrogenic dances.

Abstract (英文): Counting how many times something happens is among the simplest questions about a video and among the hardest for video-language models, autoregressive and diffusion alike. The difficulty lies between the frames: a model samples only a few of them, while each repetition unfolds as movement in between. Video codecs already record this movement densely as block displacements, and codec-based video models use it to shape their visual input. We are the first to show that this motion provides direct evidence for the count: a candidate count is a testable motion hypothesis, and unwinding the cycles it implies measures how strongly the observed directions support it. We present CoDiCount, a unified training-free framework that turns this test into a count potential and applies it while a frozen diffusion vision-language model (dVLM) denoises its answer. At every denoising step, structured count messages map each option letter, or each grammar-valid edit of an open integer, to the count it expresses and reweight the model's own probabilities within that set. Consistent gains over the official decoders on MotionBench, TOMATO, and SVCBench demonstrate the broad generalization of CoDiCount across diffusion models, datasets, and diverse counting tasks. With CoDiCount, a 7B dVLM outperforms GPT-5.1 and Gemini-2.5-Pro on MotionBench, surpasses GPT-4o and much larger open models on TOMATO, and matches GPT-5.4 on SVCBench.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generative video models are becoming powerful renderers, yet lack the predictable control interfaces of conventional systems such as HTML/CSS engines or Blender. Their capabilities emerge implicitly from training, making it difficult to determine what they can reliably execute. We present Neural Rendering Compiler (NeurCo), which automatically probes frozen video models to construct model-specific capability manuals describing which semantic requirements they can reliably satisfy. NeurCo represents user intent in a model-agnostic intermediate representation of objects, constraints, events, and cameras, then compiles it into model-specific execution strategies. Reliable requirements remain unchanged, while unreliable ones are selectively rewritten or compiled into alternative execution strategies. We introduce VComBench, a benchmark of 380 compositional, multi-requirement tasks spanning text-to-video, image-to-video, and reference-to-video generation. NeurCo improves requirement satisfaction from 46.8% to 52.3% on Wan and from 66.5% to 69.3% on Minimax H3, and achieves further gains when combined with prompt enhancers, including on Seedance 2.5. Our analysis reveals model-specific differences in control capabilities and trade-offs between controllability and photorealism, highlighting capability-guided compilation as a promising approach to programmable generative video rendering.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Interactive world models must generate video continuously while responding promptly to user actions. Real-time throughput alone leaves input latency unresolved: coarse generation blocks delay action updates, and generation after input adds work to the response path. We introduce ShowNow, a fine-grained speculative streaming framework that uses computation available during playback to prepare action-conditioned futures before input arrives. Single-latent commitment enables frequent decisions, while configurable preparation covers one to eight actions. We construct a matched multi-future dataset to train alternative continuations from a common state; shared-history tree execution and fused commitment make their preparation efficient. On a single H100, our 5B model sustains 480p generation at 16 fps with eight prepared futures. In ViZDoom, the complete ShowNow deployment reduces median input-to-display latency from 620 to 208 ms relative to the native-block SolarWM-FT deployment. Matched-policy experiments reduce restart empty ticks from 1.56% to 0.09% (94%) and improve on-time responses to action changes. These results establish complementary roles for fine-grained commitment and advance preparation, with measured trade-offs in visual fidelity and long-horizon consistency. ShowNow turns playback-time computation into responsive visual interaction. Project: https://github.com/showlab/ShowNow.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing scene understanding requires multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to interpret queried targets in relation to nearby objects and surrounding structures, which serve as spatial references or define the scope of an answer. However, standard answers and bounding boxes often leave these contextual relationships implicit. Thus, we construct GeoContext-5K to make them explicit in 5,394 training pairs for visual question answering (VQA), with spatial cues, visual facts, and supporting regions. GeOPD uses these annotations for on-policy distillation, guiding a frozen teacher with region marks on the full image and a visual fact while preserving surrounding spatial references. It prioritizes teacher corrections that agree with the shift in the student's next token distribution when evidence is restored. A complementary objective weights the contrast between the student's scores on the two views by how much masking lowers the teacher's log probability for the same sampled token. Experiments on five remote sensing VQA benchmarks show that GeOPD outperforms the evaluated open-source MLLMs and matched post-training baselines, improving over the 4B and 9B base models by 5.81 and 2.97 percentage points on average. The dataset and code will be publicly released at https://github.com/YeeCharm/GeOPD.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generalization to unseen image generators has driven efforts to adapt pretrained visual representations for detection. Yet recent findings show that vision foundation models already encode transferable detection knowledge, while further adaptation can impair generalization. This motivates a shift from backbone adaptation to knowledge readout. Conventional linear probing offers a direct way to read this knowledge but is limited to final-layer RGB features. Multi-cue detectors broaden the evidence base, but their designs introduce other tradeoffs: adaptation may compromise pretrained transferability, heterogeneous encoders complicate feature integration, and late fusion precludes intermediate cross-view interaction. To overcome these limitations, we propose ReMoT, a Mixture-of-Transformers-inspired framework that reads complementary knowledge from spatially aligned RGB and wavelet views within a fully frozen visual foundation. ReMoT employs staged joint attention to enable controlled local and global cross-view interaction, while learnable fusion queries progressively read complementary evidence across Transformer depth without writeback. ReMoT achieves 91.2% average accuracy across eight benchmark groups with 3,074 trainable parameters, attaining the highest overall average among the evaluated methods. These results support structured knowledge readout as a promising alternative to backbone adaptation. Code is available at https://github.com/yunncheng/ReMoT.

</details>
