Abstract (英文): We consider the problem of constructing knowledge bases for applications based on large language models, in which the structure of a knowledge base determines how an application can retrieve and connect the information needed to answer a question. We study Self-Structuring Learning, a setting in which questions and expected answers guide the construction of a persistent knowledge base whose editable structure can be a graph schema or the organization of linked knowledge documents. We propose Knowledge-Base Gradient Descent, which diagnoses discrepancies between expected answers and responses obtained through the current knowledge base. At each round, the method proposes a structural edit, rebuilds the knowledge base, and adopts the edit when the learning loss decreases. The retained structure is evaluated on other questions about the same sources. By randomizing over candidate edits, we express the loss change of an edit as a derivative with respect to the probability of selecting it, and we evaluate joint edits as single candidates because their changes interact. Schema Descent instantiates the method for graph schemas. In experiments on large documents, each a bundle of research papers, the updated knowledge cards and graph schemas obtain higher mean Answer F1 on held-out generated questions than their initial structures, with schema gains in every document. On human questions, mean F1 rises for schemas and falls for cards: the updated structures serve the kind of question they were learned from.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate satellite data-based field boundary delineation is essential for land management and agricultural statistics, but integrating tile-level predictions into continuous large-area vector products introduces topological errors and computational challenges. We present Delineate Anything Flow (DelAnyFlow), a framework for scalable field instance mapping that integrates the YOLOv11-based DelAny model with structured post-processing, cross-tile instance unification, vectorization, and topology-consistent simplification. DelAny is trained on the Field Boundary Instance Segmentation-22 M (FBIS-22M) dataset, containing 672,909 multi-resolution patches from 0.25 to 10 m and 22.9 million field instances. It achieves the mean average precision with the intersection over union (IoU) threshold set at 0.5 (mAP@0.5) of 0.720 and mAP@0.5:0.95 of 0.477, while providing substantially faster inference than the zero-shot segment anything model (SAM2). Component-level evaluation shows that cross-tile unification reduces seam-induced field fragmentation by a factor of 4–6, while anchor-guided simplification reduces separated neighbouring fields from about 20% to 0.2% at comparable geometric compression. Demonstrating operational scalability, DelAnyFlow mapped Ukraine’s entire 603,000 km2 territory in 5.4 h on a single workstation. Quantitative comparisons with independent operational products show larger IoU values and small-object recall (producer accuracy), particularly for fragmented agricultural landscapes; DelAnyFlow reaches an IoU of 0.971 and a small-object recall of 0.878, compared with 0.926 and 0.405 for the best operational product. The framework therefore provides a reproducible pathway from tile-level GeoAI predictions to spatially continuous, topologically consistent field-boundary products at regional and national scales. Code, models, the dataset, and national vectors are available at https://lavreniuk.github.io/Delineate-Anything/ .

</details>

Abstract (英文): The cover and management factor ( C -factor) is a crucial parameter influencing the accuracy of soil erosion model estimates. However, conventional approaches often overlook the influence of vegetation vertical structure, particularly understory vegetation, which may lead to biased estimation of erosion severity. To address this issue, a synergistic C -factor estimation framework was developed that integrates multi-angular remote sensing (RS) observations and directional fractional vegetation cover ( DFVC ) to enhance the characterization of vegetation structure and improve C -factor estimation accuracy. The PROSAIL model was used to simulate canopy reflectance under different viewing angles, from which a dataset of multi-angular vegetation indices (VIs) was constructed. Among these VIs, the index most strongly correlated with leaf area index ( LAI ) was selected to develop a progressive “VIs– LAI – DFVC – C -factor” estimation model. The applicability of the method was further evaluated using field measurements collected across different vegetation types and structural conditions. The perpendicular vegetation index ( PVI ) was found to be significantly correlated with LAI and was identified as the optimal feature for LAI retrieval. Among the multi-angular combinations, the three-angle combination of 0°, 36°, and 55° was retained for LAI retrieval and achieved an RMSE of 0.233 and a MAPE of 0.059. Based on the overall evaluation, the 0° + 36° configuration was adopted for final DFVC -based C -factor mapping. The proposed estimation framework demonstrated high reliability ( R 2 = 0.754), with a mean C -factor estimate value of 0.019 in the study area. This study highlights the importance of incorporating vegetation vertical structure information in C -factor estimation and demonstrates that multi-angular RS can offer effective complementary information for characterizing structural features such as canopy gaps and directional occlusion. The proposed framework presents a new approach for quantitative soil erosion monitoring and environmental management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Tenuous atmospheric layers play an important part in applications ranging from modeling atmospheric heat to transportation safety, disaster and hazard response to wildfires and volcanic eruptions. The height and optical properties of atmospheric layers are usually provided by satellite lidar observations. A known challenge in the identification of atmospheric layers is the detection of tenuous clouds and aerosols, which often escape existing layer detection schemes. The problem lies in the discrimination of faint signals from background, especially in situations where stronger reflectors exist within the same regions, during nighttime and daytime conditions. The Density-Dimension Algorithm (DDA) is an auto-adaptive algorithm which has had wide application from the detection of crevasses on ice sheets in ICESat-2 Advanced Topographic Laser Altimeter System (ATLAS) data, to the detection of atmospheric layers in ICESat-2 and CALIPSO lidar data. This paper focuses on the implementation of the DDA for atmospheric lidar data collected by the Cloud-Aerosol Transport System (CATS) aboard the International Space Station (ISS). We aim to demonstrate the versatility of the DDA in detecting a variety of atmospheric layers, with an emphasis on tenuous clouds and aerosols. Layer detection facilitated by the new CATS-DDA is evaluated using airborne data collected with the Cloud Physics Lidar (CPL). Comparisons of CATS-DDA results with detections in the CATS Level 2 Feature Mask product indicate an increased detection capability for tenuous layers and a refined, more realistic representation of atmospheric layers afforded by the high-resolution capabilities of the DDA, which retains the native resolution of the recorded data. Beyond the functionality of the DDA for layer detection in CATS lidar data, results from this paper serve to demonstrate the ability of the DDA to overcome some of the limitations of the CATS instrument. These findings may aid in developing instruments and algorithms for future atmospheric satellite missions, such as NASA FALCON (Fleet for the Atmosphere Linking Commercial Observations with NASA).

Abstract (英文): The determination of physical height differences using highly stable atomic clocks has emerged as a novel approach in relativistic geodesy, exploiting gravitational redshift as a direct observable of geopotential differences. In this study, we investigate the feasibility of satellite-based clock comparisons using the Atomic Clock Ensemble in Space (ACES) onboard the International Space Station, which enables time transfer via microwave (MWL) and optical (ELT) links. Since operational optical data are not yet available, a comprehensive full-scale simulation of realistic ACES observation scenarios is performed, including detailed noise models of clocks and links. A slope-based estimation method is applied to time series of clock comparisons in order to extract the relativistic redshift signal and derive height differences between the ground stations. The performance of the approach is evaluated for quasi-common view, non-common view, and split non-common view configurations, where the latter divides the observation period into shorter intervals. The results show that, under the considered simulation assumptions, optical links enable faster convergence and can achieve height accuracies at the decimeter level within a few days and at the centimeter level over longer periods, while microwave links are more strongly affected by noise and bias contributions. Non-common view processing significantly increases observation availability with only minor loss in accuracy, and the split approach provides robust solutions for larger networks. These findings demonstrate the potential of satellite-based clock comparisons for determining physical height differences between remote locations under the considered simulation assumptions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Onboard processing for remote sensing satellites has long been of interest in ocean monitoring because it can provide timely information on rapidly changing ocean environments. Recently, the integration of artificial intelligence (AI) into onboard processing has emerged as an active research topic. However, application scenarios and on-orbit demonstrations of AI-based onboard processing remain limited. In this study, we achieved, to our knowledge, the first on-orbit detection of ocean eddies using AI on the Haishao-1 (HS-1) spaceborne synthetic aperture radar (SAR) satellite. Specifically, an onboard application module was developed for HS-1, integrating image preprocessing and deep-learning-based detection based on our previously developed Eddy Detection based on YOLO (EOLO) model. On-orbit experiments verified successful execution of the application module. Ground reproduction of the tasks using hardware identical to the onboard computing units yielded an average processing time of 3.4–6.1 s per image. Evaluation against expert annotations of 288 HS-1 scenes showed that EOLO, originally developed using Sentinel-1 imagery, achieved an F1 score of 78% after threshold adjustment. The application module has also been deployed on the Haishao-2 platform for Arctic sea ice extraction, further demonstrating its extensibility. This study presents the implementation and on-orbit validation of the onboard application module, providing a practical reference for future applications.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing image–text retrieval requires diverse textual supervision and fine-grained visual representations. Redundant captions limit linguistic diversity, while rewriting can introduce semantic drift. A single global image descriptor may also underrepresent small objects and local spatial arrangements, limiting fine-grained discrimination. To address these challenges, we propose RS-DeCLIP, a two-stage retrieval framework. Stage 1 adapts a RemoteCLIP ViT-B/32 dual encoder with DeCLIP-style multi-view supervision using official captions and captions polished by a large language model. A semantic consistency gate downweights text self-supervision (TSS) between less consistent original and rewritten captions. Similarity-aware negative downweighting and text-to-image weighting address ambiguous negatives and directional imbalance, while training-only fine-grained alignment strengthens detail sensitivity. Stage 2 targets the fine-grained visual information underrepresented by the global descriptor. Five learnable visual query vectors, conditioned on the global image embedding and guided by soft spatial preferences, attend to a dense P2 feature map to extract local evidence. Consistency-gated orthogonal residual fusion integrates this evidence into the frozen global image embedding. These visual queries are distinct from the user’s text query: image feature extraction and fusion do not use the retrieval text, so fused image descriptors can be precomputed and compared with independently encoded text vectors. On the Remote Sensing Image Captioning Dataset (RSICD), Stage 1 improves mean recall (MR) from 35.26% to 38.54%, and Stage 2 further increases MR to 39.27%. On the Remote Sensing Image–Text Match Dataset (RSITMD), Stage 2 improves MR from 50.90% to 52.58%. The final model exceeds the reported GeoRSCLIP-FT (RET-2) results by 0.40 and 0.77 percentage points on RSICD and RSITMD, respectively, and the Representation Discrepancy Bridging (RDB) method by 1.18 and 0.54 percentage points.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Regional winter rapeseed yield prediction requires models that remain reliable across highly variable climate conditions. We developed an interpretable climate-responsive ensemble for rapeseed yield prediction using satellite and environmental predictors. The framework was evaluated on 542 city–year observations from 73 cities in the middle and lower Yangtze River from 2014 to 2022. Monthly Landsat vegetation indices and TerraClimate variables were screened using Group Lasso. We evaluated three base learners using leave-one-year-out cross-validation: a temporal convolutional network (TCN), a bidirectional long short-term memory network, and a random forest. We combined their predictions using regime-specific weights. The ensemble achieved a coefficient of determination (R2) of 0.848, a root mean square error (RMSE) of 189 kg ha−1, a mean absolute error (MAE) of 151 kg ha−1 and a relative RMSE of 8.9%. Compared with the TCN, the strongest base learner overall, it reduced RMSE by 25.57 kg ha−1 under waterlogging and 8.98 kg ha−1 under combined freeze and waterlogging. SHapley Additive exPlanations of the random-forest component identified spring vegetation indices and soil moisture as the strongest predictors, with soil moisture more influential under waterlogging and drought–waterlogging. Climate-anomaly context was associated with changes in model skill and predictor attribution, supporting regional rapeseed yield monitoring under contrasting meteorological conditions.

Abstract (英文): Mining-induced subsidence is a common geological hazard in coal mining areas, and reliable deformation monitoring is important for risk reduction. Cumulative InSAR deformation fields contain deformation accumulated during preceding periods, whereas newly occurring deformation over a short forecast horizon generally represents only a small fraction of the cumulative magnitude. Direct prediction of future cumulative deformation may therefore exploit temporal persistence to achieve relatively low mean errors while underestimating locally strong subsidence. To investigate this issue, we used 276 ascending Sentinel-1 SAR images acquired from 10 January 2020 to 11 July 2024 to perform DS-InSAR monitoring at the Wutongzhuang Mine, Hebei Province, China, and constructed 138 continuous two-dimensional cumulative vertical deformation fields. We defined three prediction tasks—cumulative-input–cumulative-output (C2C), cumulative-input–increment-output (C2D), and increment-input–increment-output (D2D)—and applied each task to CNN-PhLSTM, ConvLSTM, and VMRNN. Monitoring identified two major subsidence zones in the north-central and southwestern parts of the study area, with a maximum cumulative vertical deformation of approximately −467.96mm by 11 July 2024. The Pearson correlation of colocated PS-InSAR and DS-InSAR deformation rates was 0.88; comparison between DS-InSAR and leveling at P1 and P2 yielded a correlation of 0.9742, a mean absolute error (MAE) of 8.23 mm, and a root mean square error (RMSE) of 10.29 mm. Keeping historical cumulative deformation inputs unchanged and converting the future supervision target to deformation increments reduced the RMSEs of CNN-PhLSTM, ConvLSTM, and VMRNN by 56.48%, 12.17%, and 17.16%, respectively, with consistent improvement directions across three random seeds. Further converting historical inputs to increments produced smaller, model-dependent changes. Under the unified D2D task, VMRNN achieved the lowest mean RMSE and MAE and the highest mean R2, showing relatively favorable overall predictive performance among the compared models. Spatial predictions also show that VMRNN-D2D generally preserves the spatial structures of the major subsidence zones and local troughs, while errors are concentrated mainly in actively developing strong-subsidence areas, where subsidence magnitudes tend to be underestimated. These findings indicate that representing the future supervision target as deformation increments provides an effective strategy for improving short-term prediction of cumulative InSAR deformation across different networks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): An X5.8-class solar flare began at 01:10 UT on 11 May 2024, during the main phase of the super geomagnetic storm, providing an opportunity to investigate solar flare effects under highly disturbed space weather conditions. Using multi-instrument observations from the Sanya incoherent scatter radar (ISR), ionosondes, Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) receivers, and ground magnetometers, this study investigates the ionospheric and geomagnetic responses over East Asia. The Sanya ISR observations revealed pronounced electron density enhancements near 110 km and at 300–400 km, with electron density at 300–400 km reaching 1.92–4.08 times the background values. The F2-layer peak electron density (NmF2) increased rapidly to 2.02 × 1012 el/m3, approximately three times its preflare value, whereas the F2-layer peak height (hmF2) initially decreased and subsequently increased. The ISR-derived vertical total electron content (TEC) increased by approximately 20 TECU relative to the reference day, with the fractional contribution from around 350 km increasing by approximately 5 percentage points. Ionosonde observations revealed pronounced latitudinal differences. F-region echoes at Mohe and Beijing nearly disappeared against the background of a strong negative ionospheric storm, whereas the flare-associated response was more prominent at the low-latitude station Sanya, where the minimum reflection frequency (fmin) increased to 8.85–9.95 MHz. GNSS-TEC observations revealed distinct latitudinal differences in the ionospheric response, with maximum TEC enhancements of 36.76 and 35.40 TECU at the low-latitude stations SYBG and QION, respectively. At middle latitudes, the negative ionospheric storm remained dominant despite short-term positive flare-associated enhancements, while some stations in the northeastern high-latitude region exhibited small positive perturbations superimposed on the negative background. The geomagnetic response exhibited a pronounced east–west asymmetry within the 40–50°N latitude band, with positive disturbances in the horizontal component of the geomagnetic field over 75–95°E and negative disturbances over 96–135°E. These findings suggest that flare-induced photoionization and conductivity enhancement interacted with the pre-existing storm-time electrodynamic environment, producing altitude-dependent ionospheric responses and pronounced spatial variations in TEC and geomagnetic disturbances.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Arid ecosystems face severe climate threats, yet stochastic climate volatility often masks true ecological transitions. In this study, we developed an integrated spatiotemporal framework to decouple mean ecological trends from climate noise in Gansu Province, China. We combined Remote Sensing Ecological Index (RSEI) time series, the multi-scalar Standardized Precipitation Evapotranspiration Index (SPEI), and a probabilistic Dust Hazard Index (DHI) to evaluate historical dynamics (2001–2024). Subsequently, eco-environmental sustainability (2025–2099) was projected under CMIP6 scenarios using a Random Forest algorithm optimized via spatial block cross-validation. Historical evaluations revealed a significant regional ecological amelioration (RSEI trend: +0.0279/decade, p < 0.01), with the degraded area decreasing from 68.60% in 2001 to 52.68% in 2024. However, spatial polarization persisted, with severe degradation covering 38.84% of the hyper-arid northwest. Feature importance and dual correlation analyses identified short-term moisture deficits (SPEI-3) and chronic dust exposure as primary biophysical controls; notably, dust hazards strongly suppressed vegetation in degraded zones (r = −0.43). Future projections indicate that warming-induced aridity will intensify ecological stress. Under the intermediate SSP2-4.5 scenario, the area of Serious Decline (SSD) expands from 35.09% in the near term to 47.15% by the end of the century. Conversely, the high-emission SSP5-8.5 scenario, characterized by thermal extremes up to 3.58 °C, triggers an abrupt structural transition during 2061–2099; the SSD area more than triples (from 12.13% to 43.95%), overwhelming historical restoration gains. Safeguarding these fragile drylands necessitates proactive management integrating targeted dust mitigation and water rights allocation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visible–infrared fusion is a main route to all-weather object extraction in near-ground remote sensing, but single-band RGB remains unstable under occlusion, clutter, and low light and illumination variation. This study presents LightMaskMamba, a lightweight visible–infrared object detection network for near-ground remote sensing. Public benchmarks test fusion quality across scenes, and the self-built near-ground wampee field dataset is for deployment-oriented field validation. The core of the network is a lightweight mask-guided Mamba fusion architecture. Its key module, the Lightweight Mask Mamba Fusion Block (LMMFB), uses an object-related mask to drive shallow-to-deep VIS–NIR feature fusion, focuses computation on object regions, and reduces scan redundancy. Dual-band detection therefore stays light enough to run at the edge. On the self-built wampee dataset, LightMaskMamba-v11s reaches 97.4% mAP50 and 52.0% mAP50:95. Relative to single-band YOLOv11s, mAP50 is 2.3 and 4.6 percentage points higher on IR and RGB. Parameters rise by 3.7M, from 8.8M to 12.5M. The same model is likewise effective in detection accuracy, and lighter, on public multimodal object-detection datasets. On a Jetson Orin NX Super, TensorRT INT8 mixed-precision inference reaches about 17.3 FPS, or 58 ms per image.

Abstract (英文): Seafloor sediment classification is critical for benthic habitat mapping and marine environmental protection. Existing classification methods mostly adopt seafloor surface observation parameters such as backscatter and bathymetry. These parameters only characterize surface seabed properties and lack physical prior information related to sediment formation mechanisms, which limits classification accuracy and physical interpretability. Considering that bottom-current kinetic energy (BCKE) governs sediment erosion, transport and deposition and further shapes the spatial distribution of sediments, this paper proposes a bottom-current-fused two-stage Bayesian MIC-augmented mRMR (BCTBM) architecture for seafloor sediment classification. BCTBM combines BCKE with acoustic and topographic features to construct a 77-dimensional multi-source feature set. Samples with high class separability are selected using the Jeffries–Matusita (J-M) distance, and a two-stage feature selection algorithm is further developed. ReliefF is first applied for coarse-grained feature screening to generate a candidate feature set; subsequently, the Bayesian MIC-augmented Minimum Redundancy Maximum Relevance (BM-mRMR) model employs Bayesian optimization (BO) to adaptively solve the optimal relevance–redundancy trade-off weights for seven target dimensions, and mRMR finally derives the optimal feature set based on the obtained optimal weights. The proposed method is validated in the shallow-water sediment classification scenario of the eastern English Channel. Eighty-four comparative experiments demonstrate that introducing BCKE improves the overall accuracy by 3.48% on average, with a maximum increment of 9.4%. The proposed two-stage BM-mRMR method achieves average accuracy improvements of 4.84% and 6.08% over ReliefF and ReliefF-mRMR, respectively. The classification results are consistent with the Hjulström–Sundborg sedimentary-dynamic framework, offering an effective pathway for refined benthic habitat mapping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-definition (HD) maps for autonomous driving store each lane marking as a separate polyline. Satellite imagery covers wide areas at low cost, but current methods cannot turn it directly into such polylines. The reason is that no existing output representation suits polyline detection. Segmentation masks do not tell which line a pixel belongs to, and they merge lines that meet or cross. Existing cell-wise connectivity representations tie their output slots to fixed angular sectors. Their training target therefore changes discontinuously at sector boundaries. We propose STELLA, an output representation suited to polylines, and a method to train it. At each grid cell, STELLA predicts one self-slot and two unordered connection slots. During training, these slots are matched to the ground-truth (GT) connections at minimum cost. One line thus becomes one chain of cells, and post-processing only cleans up overlapping lines. We also propose AI-CCQ, an asymmetric instance-level metric for thin, long lines. It gives credit to a partly drawn line but rejects a line that switches to a neighboring marking. On the SEED-MAP test set, STELLA reaches an AI-CCQ F1 score of 0.4483, against 0.3005 for a segmentation-based method and 0.3551 for the Sat2Graph representation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Domain shift remains a challenge for RGB-based surface-landmine detection, particularly when labeled imagery from a new environment is unavailable. This study examines unsupervised domain adaptation on SULAND_v2 using YOLOv8-S and a teacher–student framework. Complete acquisition sequences are separated between adaptation and evaluation, and seven adaptation configurations are compared across three source-training seeds. On the primary assignment, mean average precision at an intersection-over-union threshold of 0.50 (mAP50) increases from 53.70% for the source-only model to 63.56% for Mean Teacher and 64.22% for the CMT-style configuration. Controls with the same optimization budget indicate that additional training alone does not account for the Mean Teacher improvement. The pooled gain is concentrated in PMA-2, whereas PFM-1 performance decreases. Reversing the adaptation and evaluation sequences yields improvements for both classes, suggesting that the class response depends on the sequence assignment. Individual-sequence results and class-specific recall and false positives further reveal variation within the pooled scores. Additional alignment objectives provide small, inconsistent gains over Mean Teacher on the primary assignment, while source-domain accuracy decreases modestly. These findings suggest that unlabeled target imagery can improve detection within the evaluated dataset, although its benefits vary across classes and acquisitions. The intended application is candidate generation for visible surface targets and analyst review; operational reliability requires further validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate estimation of gross primary productivity (GPP) at fine spatial and temporal resolutions is essential for characterizing carbon uptake across heterogeneous landscapes. Coarse-resolution GPP products smooth differences among neighboring vegetation patches, while many fine-resolution products report only total GPP and do not distinguish the contributions of sunlit and shaded leaves. In this study, we developed SS-GPP30, a spatially continuous dataset that provides 8-day estimates of total GPP and its sunlit- and shaded-leaf components at 30 m resolution across the Beijing–Tianjin–Hebei (BTH) region from 2017 to 2025. The dataset was generated using a two-leaf light-use-efficiency framework driven by Harmonized Landsat and Sentinel-2 (HLS)-derived leaf area index, regional-scale ERA5 meteorological forcing (0.25°), annual 30 m land cover, and seasonal, vegetation-type-specific dynamic representative clumping index. The methodological advance over existing high-resolution GPP products lies in integrating 30 m HLS-based canopy dynamics with sunlit–shaded TL-LUE modeling and seasonal vegetation-type-specific dynamic clumping index, thereby enabling finer temporal representation of canopy photosynthesis and spatial characterization of heterogeneous landscapes. Evaluation against flux-derived GPP at eight eddy covariance sites showed that the seasonal index reduced the root-mean-square error in four of five vegetation and crop groups, with the largest reductions in forest (5.3%) and shrubland (4.6%). Cross-product comparisons across vegetation types yielded R2 values ranging from 0.70 to 0.89, while SS-GPP30 better preserved fine-scale spatial heterogeneity than coarser-resolution products. During 2017–2025, mean annual GPP was 1431.7 g C m−2 yr−1, with sunlit- and shaded-leaf GPP contributing 742.0 and 689.7 g C m−2 yr−1, respectively. Shaded leaves contributed 48.2% of total GPP and showed marked spatiotemporal variation. Both components increased significantly, with a stronger trend in shaded-leaf GPP. Therefore, SS-GPP30 fills an important gap in fine-resolution GPP mapping by providing 30 m estimates of total, sunlit-leaf, and shaded-leaf GPP, thereby resolving both regional vegetation productivity and within-canopy photosynthetic partitioning across the BTH region. The framework also has potential for application to other regions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This paper proposes a Noise-Decoupled Multi-scale Evidence-aware (NDME) algorithm to address the critical challenges of feature sparsity and noise interference in detecting tiny targets (occupying <0.1% of image area) in deep-space images. By innovatively integrating physical principles with deep learning, NDME constructs a Multi-scale Quantized Feature Pyramid Network (MS-QFPN) and introduces a Non-local Sub-pixel Attention (NSA) mechanism to effectively enhance the sparse feature representation of tiny targets. Simultaneously, an adaptive noise-signal covariance matrix based on the International Celestial Reference Frame 3 (ICRF-3)-inspired deep-space noise physical model is designed to suppress cosmic-ray noise while preserving weak target features (signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) > 2). Compared with state-of-the-art methods such as YOLOX series and ALCNet, NDME demonstrates significant advantages in small-target detection on the IST-S and IRDST datasets, exhibiting robust adaptability under complex noise environments. Experimental results show that NDME outperforms existing methods in recall, precision, and other key metrics, providing an effective solution for tiny-target detection under deep-space-like sparse-feature and noise conditions, while validation on real deep-space observations remains future work. It should be noted that the public benchmarks used in this work are deep-space-like infrared proxy datasets; they are used to evaluate the sparse-feature and noise-robustness aspects of the method rather than to claim direct deep-space validation.

Abstract (英文): Terrestrial Laser Scanning (TLS) is pivotal for creating high-precision 3D geospatial data in urban digital twins and Scan-to-BIM applications. However, TLS point clouds frequently suffer from severe glass-induced reflection artifacts, which fundamentally compromise geometric fidelity and downstream automated modeling. Existing methods struggle with real-world physical complexities because they rely heavily on idealized reflection symmetry assumptions and fail to address the critical “measurement void” problem caused by transparent surfaces. To address these limitations, we propose a unified cross-modal framework RADAR for robust reflection artifact removal in complex urban environments. First, our approach leverages a multimodal vision foundation model—integrating panoramic imagery and LiDAR-derived spatial maps—to accurately detect glass regions. These initial masks are then geometrically refined and completed via ray-casting to recover missing surfaces. Second, to handle imperfect reflections, we introduce the Reflection-aware Local–Global Geometric Similarity (RE-LGGS) descriptor. By jointly encoding multi-scale PCA-derived structures and spatial orientation consistency, RE-LGGS robustly identifies virtual points even under severe geometric distortion and data sparsity. Extensive evaluations on multiple public TLS datasets demonstrate that the proposed framework achieves competitive performance across diverse glass-scene conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Rapid and accurate assessment of maize straw volume and spatial distribution can help to improve straw utilization efficiency. This study estimated the theoretical maize straw resources of West-Central Jilin Province, China, from 2019 to 2022. Net primary productivity (NPP) was simulated with the Carnegie–Ames–Stanford Approach (CASA) model on the Google Earth Engine (GEE) platform using Sentinel-2 imagery and converted to theoretical straw resources using an NPP–yield coefficient and the straw-to-grain ratio. The estimated maize straw resources were evaluated against field measurements collected in 2022, yielding an R2 of 0.63 and a root mean square error (RMSE) of 849.26 kg/ha. At the county level, comparisons with statistical reference straw resources for 2019–2022 yielded R2 values of 0.84–0.86. The four-year mean straw yield was 9416 kg/ha, with precipitation as the main climatic factor shaping this pattern. In terms of utilization potential, the straw supply could meet the residue requirement of conservation tillage on 87.34% of the cultivated area, and the straw available for feed could meet approximately 70% of the straw demand of the existing cattle and sheep inventories. These results provide spatially explicit information for straw management and regional agricultural planning.

Abstract (英文): Infrared small targets occupy few pixels and often exhibit weak contrast, making them difficult to distinguish from bright edges, background clutter, and noise. Deep unfolding provides an interpretable framework for background–target decomposition, yet weak target responses may be absorbed into background estimates or suppressed during sparse updates. We propose a Target Confidence and Memory Network (TCMNet) that incorporates target confidence and cross-stage error memory into a multistage deep unfolding framework. A confidence-guided multiscale background modeling module combines smoothing priors at different scales with learned background representations. In later stages, target confidence derived from intermediate target estimates reduces the influence of potential targets on background estimation. A transform-domain sparse update module employs learnable transforms and channel-wise learnable thresholds to suppress clutter, together with local target protection to limit the attenuation of weak responses. To exploit information from preceding stages, a cross-stage error memory module integrates current target responses, inter-stage response changes, and reconstruction residuals into a recurrent state. This state produces bounded corrections to the target estimate before it is passed to the next stage. Target estimates are constrained to be nonnegative, while an auxiliary reconstruction loss penalizes discrepancies between reconstructed and observed images. A final prediction head combines target estimates, multiscale local contrast cues, and the reconstruction residual to produce a target probability map. Experiments on NUDT-SIRST and a local 1:1 partition of MDvsFA demonstrate the effectiveness of TCMNet, achieving mIoU scores of 91.71% and 90.31%, F1 scores of 95.68% and 94.91%, and detection probabilities of 98.41% and 96.84%, respectively. These results highlight the ability of the proposed framework to preserve weak target responses while suppressing background interference. By integrating explicit background–target decomposition with adaptive sparse updating and cross-stage error correction, TCMNet provides a structured approach to infrared small target detection in complex scenes.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Higher overall accuracy does not necessarily mean more complete landslide mapping at every location. Across eight rainfall-related events, we examine the benefits of earlier satellite imagery using U-TAE, a neural network that combines images acquired on different dates, as the main model. We compare overall accuracy with the fraction of mapped landslide area detected within local image patches. Mean average precision increased from 0.333 with a post-event image to 0.379 with a pre/post pair and 0.419 with a longer sequence. Adding earlier images to the pair improved mean average precision in seven events but slightly reduced it in Chimanimani. However, the average increase in the detected fraction across locations and regions was 1.1 percentage points (95% interval −1.3 to +3.3). This provides no clear evidence that earlier images increased the average fraction of mapped landslide area detected. The estimated gain in Italy decreased from 3.0 to 0.3 percentage points when the decision thresholds changed, indicating that the apparent improvement depended on how the predictions were converted into a landslide map. The benefit of earlier images was also greater in patches containing more mapped landslide area in some regions, but was not clearly related to the strength of surface change in patches with sufficient mapped area and usable optical observations. An auxiliary comparison with U-Net, a convolutional image-segmentation model, also showed local detection differences alongside higher overall accuracy. Local detection also varied when the same models were retrained using unchanged images. Checking local detection changes alongside conventional accuracy measures helps assess whether adding earlier imagery reduces missed landslide area across locations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Geospatial foundation models (GFMs) are proposed for operational Earth observation but benchmarked mostly on urban and agricultural data, so their value and limitations for fine-grained ecological monitoring, with fuzzy class boundaries and severe class imbalance, are largely untested. We benchmark five GFMs (Clay v1.0, Clay v1.5, Prithvi-EO-2.0, DOFA, TerraMind) against two supervised U-Net baselines, one with a transformer (MiT-B2) and one with a convolutional (ResNet-50) encoder, for post-classification habitat change detection in Gesäuse National Park, Austria. All models are trained with three seeds on 2013 expert-interpreted labels (23 habitat classes), using very high-resolution red, green, blue (RGB) and near-infrared aerial imagery and a normalised digital surface model (nDSM) derived from airborne light detection and ranging (LiDAR). Applied to 2019 imagery and 2020 LiDAR, their predictions are compared with the 2013 reference to yield change maps with eight transition classes plus no change. All five GFMs are robustly ahead of the convolutional U-Net, but so is the transformer U-Net, which only Clay v1.0 exceeds, by a small margin (multi-class change macro Intersection over Union (IoU) 0.233 vs. 0.209) whose paired 95% confidence interval (+0.001 to +0.045) nearly includes zero. Adding the nDSM helps every model, but mostly in the canopy-cover classes, whose reference labels are themselves LiDAR-derived. Rare or heterogeneous transitions remain poorly detected by every model. Rule-based post-processing defined with the Gesäuse National Park administration converts the predictions into polygon-structured, ecologically plausible change maps that meet the park’s data standards, with little effect on pixel accuracy (multi-class macro IoU −0.03 to +0.04) but lower recall for forest density loss and setback; the maps are therefore suited to flagging candidate changes for expert verification rather than to unsupervised use.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate detection of cotton seedlings is essential for monitoring crop establishment. However, manual annotation is time-consuming, and the quality of conventional automatic annotation methods is affected by illumination and background conditions. This study proposes a joint strategy combining green leaf index (GLI) automatic annotation with deep learning detection. Three annotation methods were considered: GLI, excess green minus excess red (ExG–ExR), and hue–saturation–value (HSV). Their nine combinations with three detectors (YOLOv10n, RT-DETR-L, and Mamba-YOLO-B) were evaluated using spatial data partitioning and independent cross-region testing. Under identical post-processing, GLI achieved the highest annotation quality, with an F1 score of 96.18% and a mean intersection over union (IoU) of 89.78% for matched boxes. For initial box drawing only, bounding boxes for 200 targets were generated in 0.399 s, compared with approximately 600 s for manual drawing. Detection accuracy was assessed using mean average precision (mAP) at IoU thresholds from 0.50 to 0.95 (mAP@0.5:0.95). Across three random seeds, Mamba-YOLO + GLI achieved the highest mean source-region mAP@0.5:0.95, at 0.8020. In cross-region testing, Mamba-YOLO + GLI achieved the best detection and counting performance, with an mAP@0.5:0.95 of 0.3446. However, its detection accuracy remained substantially lower than in source-region testing. These results demonstrate that GLI automatic annotation can provide effective training labels for cotton seedling detection. Among the evaluated combinations, Mamba-YOLO + GLI achieved the highest mean source-region mAP@0.5:0.95 and the highest cross-region mAP@0.5:0.95.

Abstract (英文): Adding optical imagery to radar can improve a flood map without improving the flood probabilities assigned to its pixels. We examine when these benefits agree, using a Sentinel-1 radar-only network as the reference for a Sentinel-1/Sentinel-2 fusion network. The same flood processes and reference pixels are compared as optical input is progressively masked. With no additional masking, fusion improves mean flood-area overlap by 0.0391/0.0373 and reduces mean flood-probability squared error by 0.0203/0.0211 on 18 internal and nine held-out flood episodes previously inspected during development. When only 25% of spatial blocks are retained, overlap gains remain slightly positive, but probability error increases by 0.0106/0.0091. Most of the original-optical probability benefit comes from avoiding false flood probabilities on reference permanent water; probability improvements on reference flood pixels are inconsistent. Controls and spatial examples expose trade-offs between missed floods and false floods. Additional single-event checks reveal adverse transfer. The practical contribution is evidence that flood-area accuracy and flood-probability quality must be assessed separately when optical support changes. This retrospective satellite-image assessment relates optical availability to flood-area accuracy and probability quality.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Regular convolution is widely used in spectral–spatial unmixing networks to aggregate neighborhood information, but its fixed sampling grid cannot readily follow irregular material boundaries or elongated structures and may introduce heterogeneous pixels whose compositions differ from that of the central pixel. This study presents DB-CASSE-Net, a dual-branch hyperspectral unmixing network that combines constrained adaptive spatial sampling with spectral feature enhancement. The spatial branch learns position-dependent offsets from configurable initial sampling geometries. A hyperbolic-tangent mapping bounds the displacement range, while magnitude and spatial-smoothness regularizers suppress excessive drift and abrupt local changes. In parallel, the spectral branch uses pointwise convolutions and an identity-initialized squeeze-and-excitation mechanism to recalibrate latent spectral channels adaptively. Features from the two branches are fused, and a spectral skip connection restores fine-grained information from the original spectral signatures that may be attenuated by successive nonlinear transformations. The resulting abundance responses satisfy nonnegativity and sum-to-one constraints, and a nonnegative linear decoder reconstructs the hyperspectral image, enabling unsupervised joint estimation of endmembers and abundances. Experiments on Jasper Ridge, Samson, and Urban show that DB-CASSE-Net achieves the lowest mean abundance RMSE and mean endmember SAD among the compared methods on Jasper Ridge and Urban, and the second-lowest mean abundance RMSE on Samson.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite-derived bathymetry (SDB) in inland waters remains challenging due to the strong spatial variability of optically active constituents and limited optical penetration depth. This study investigates the impact of a proposed Adaptive Spatial Filtering (ASF) approach and nonlinear regression models on bathymetric retrieval from PlanetScope imagery in two optically complex bays of Lake Dąbie, Poland. Single-band (SBA), multi-band (MBA), and band-ratio (BR) approaches were evaluated using linear regression (LR), generalized regression neural networks (GRNN), multilayer perceptron (MLP), and radial basis function neural networks (RBFNN). The results demonstrate that the proposed ASF method improves bathymetric reconstruction accuracy and surface quality. Within the threshold depth range, the average RMSE decreased from 0.195 m to 0.156 m, while coefficients of determination reached up to R2 = 0.914. Qualitative analyses further demonstrated that ASF improved the visual and geomorphological interpretability of SDB products and the overall quality of seabed reconstruction, enabling a more faithful representation of subtle morphological features and microtopographic structures. The SBA Red configuration provided the greatest effective mapping depth, reaching 2.76 m in Lubczyna Bay, whereas the MBA approach produced the most stable performance across varying depth ranges. GRNN achieved the highest accuracy within the threshold-depth range, while all neural-network models outperformed linear regression, except for the band-ratio configuration, which showed limited suitability for bathymetric retrieval in the study areas. The results indicate that local bio-optical conditions exert a stronger influence on SDB performance than depth alone and that no single spectral configuration is universally optimal for inland-water environments. The proposed methodology demonstrates the potential of combining adaptive filtering and nonlinear regression to enhance the applicability of satellite-derived bathymetry in optically complex inland waters for hydrographic surveys and environmental monitoring.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Leaks from above-ground landfill gas pipelines are hard to locate with point-based methods. This study tested whether unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) thermography can identify potential leak zones on the gas network in a site of central Italy. Two thermal surveys (November 2023 and 2024) were processed into 8 cm thermal orthomosaics, co-registered to a median offset of 22 cm and checked on 29 RTK GNSS points. The 147 thermal anomalies identified from the ground in 2023, only one of which had measurable gas, were compared with random, distance-matched and on-pipe controls, using a local thermal metric on all pixels and on soil only. Anomaly sites were warmer than random points (AUC 0.77) but not warmer than matched or on-pipe controls once spatial dependence was considered, and showed no excess soil heat in any of 176 parameter settings. Synthetic warm spots were detected in 80% of cases above 15 °C on the pipe and 10 °C on the adjacent soil, and became undetectable with position errors of 2 m. The study thus evaluates the thermal signature of ground-identified anomalies, not leak detection rates; UAV thermography can support screening only with matched controls, accurate co-registration and gas verification.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Urban thermal stress (UTS) is intensifying, but its evolution and relationships with the built environment may differ across China’s contrasting climate zones. We examined mean summer Universal Thermal Climate Index (UTCI) estimates for 1605 urban centres identified in the Global Human Settlement Layer Urban Centre Database (GHS-UCDB) across mainland China in 2005, 2010, 2015, and 2020. Random Forest models and interpretable machine-learning methods were used to compare national patterns with those in two humid zones. Mean UTCI increased from 29.61 °C to 30.43 °C, and 73.02% of urban centres recorded higher values in 2020 than in 2005. The increase was concentrated in humid China, reaching 1.08 °C in the tropical–subtropical humid (TSH) zone and 0.64 °C in the temperate humid and semi-humid (TH) zone. Built-environment associations also differed across the four assessment years. Nationally, the normalized difference vegetation index (NDVI) ranked first in 2005 and 2010, whereas surface albedo ranked first in 2015 and 2020. Regional models showed sharper contrasts. NDVI and impervious-surface proportion consistently ranked highest in TSH, whereas albedo, urban morphology, and landscape configuration were more prominent in TH. Nonlinear responses and leading interactions varied across scales, zones, and years. National models showed predominantly NDVI-related patterns in 2005 and 2010, whereas albedo-related patterns were more prominent in 2015 and 2020. NDVI–impervious cover was the leading TSH interaction in 2010, 2015, and 2020. In TH, NDVI–population density ranked first in 2005 and 2010, tree cover–built-up intensity in 2015, and albedo–tree cover in 2020. National associations therefore cannot be transferred uniformly across climate zones. These findings support climate-specific thermal-risk screening, but model-derived response ranges require local validation before application in local urban planning and design decisions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Rising fire frequency and intensity under global warming challenge ecological security, yet dynamic fuel-related variables remain insufficiently represented in forest fire prediction models. This study integrated LPJ-simulated fuel variables with five machine learning algorithms to assess their contribution to fire prediction across China. SHAP and partial dependence analyses examined predictor importance and responses in northeastern (NEF), southeastern (SEF), and southwestern (SWF) forests. Future fire occurrence (2041–2100) was projected with bias-corrected CMIP6 data under three SSP scenarios. The main findings were as follows: (1) Fuel inclusion improved model performance by approximately 2–4% under spatial cross-validation, with CatBoost showing favorable overall performance. (2) Predictor importance differed across regions and forest types. Aboveground litter carbon storage (LitterC) and vapor pressure deficit (VPD) were the leading predictors in northeastern coniferous forests; elevation (DEM) and soil moisture (SM) were important in the SWF; and SM ranked first in both SEF types. Partial dependence analyses revealed nonlinear responses, with prominent fuel-related patterns in the NEF and soil moisture-related patterns in the SWF and SEF. (3) Future predictions increased overall in the SWF and SEF, with larger absolute increases in the SEF. Increased predictions covered 66–69% of SWF forests and 74.80–94.01% of SEF forests. Although the NEF showed an overall decline in regional mean predictions, localized increases remained evident. These findings support integrating dynamic fuel information into fire prediction and identifying priority areas for future monitoring and management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Regional inventories and local condition assessment of waterway regulation structures require complementary observations from satellite and unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) imagery. A semantic-guided feature pyramid network (SGFPN) coordinates coarse semantics and fine detail, while the residual strip enhancement module (RSEM) provides gated local and axial context at high-resolution pyramid levels. On the satellite validation split, SGFAR-YOLO achieves 57.31% horizontal-bounding-box AP and 43.14% mask AP, exceeding the YOLO11 baseline by 3.11 and 2.80 percentage points. Ablation studies and qualitative comparisons examine the design across both tasks and illustrate boundary continuity, small-target recovery, and localization of surface damage conditions. The proposed method provides a practical framework for regional mapping of waterway regulation structures and local screening of their surface condition within the Jingjiang reach.

Abstract (英文): Frequency-division multi-polarimetric synthetic aperture radar (FDS-PolSAR) achieves simultaneous multi-polarimetric observation through a carrier frequency offset, which fundamentally eliminates temporal decorrelation in dynamic scenarios, but the four polarimetric channels do not share a common frequency basis, and the global scalar phase assumption of the classical Whitt general calibration technique (GCT) is structurally invalid, causing complete calibration failure for point-like targets. Conventional SAR ranging and differential Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS)/strapdown inertial navigation systems (INSs) only provide centimeter-level accuracy, which cannot satisfy the precision requirements for inter-frequency phase compensation. To solve this problem, this paper proposes an accurate slant range inversion (ASRI)-assisted polarimetric calibration scheme. The ASRI algorithm extracts the pure quadratic phase component of target echoes via a Deskew–Filtering–Recovery (DFR) chain and provides millimeter-level equivalent accuracy for polarimetric calibration by correlation matching with a digital twin phase library. The inter-frequency phase difference is pre-compensated using the inverted slant range, which restores the validity of the GCT framework. Calibration experiments validate that the proposed method improves the cross-polarization isolation to 40.31 dB and reduces co-polarization phase imbalance to 3.1°, and this residual phase imbalance corresponds to a slant range error equivalent of approximately 2.2 mm as a conservative indirect indicator of inversion performance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reported accuracy of unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)-based yield models depends on whether validation respects repeated plots and seasonal variation. For normalised difference vegetation index (NDVI), green normalised difference vegetation index (GNDVI), normalised difference red-edge (NDRE), optimised soil-adjusted vegetation index (OSAVI), and wide dynamic range vegetation index (WDRVI), the temporal mean, median, standard deviation, and normalised area under the curve gave 20 candidate predictors. Single-index and multi-index models, with predictors selected by Elastic Net stability selection or a genetic algorithm, were evaluated by nested plot-grouped cross-validation and leave-one-year-out (LOYO) validation against season-mean references. Pooled R2 reached 0.89 (RMSE = 433 kg ha−1; WDRVI–KNN), but a season-mean reference alone gave 0.83. Within 2024, the best models reduced root mean square error (RMSE) from 571 to about 290–380 kg ha−1; within 2025, none improved on the reference. In leave-one-year-out validation, the lowest RMSE (about 2.0 t ha−1) was only 20% below that of the training-season mean (2.5 t ha−1), and all R2 values were negative. Yield differences between control and manured plots reversed direction between seasons (−328 and +300 kg ha−1, both 95% confidence intervals included zero) and are confounded with plot position. The descriptors can rank plot conditions within a season, but two seasons do not support yield forecasting for unseen years.

Abstract (英文): The development of drainage systems is essential for sustaining oasis vitality and human habitation in arid and semi-arid regions. The Tang Wang Cheng Site (TWCS) is located in the desert-oasis ecotone at the northwestern edge of the Taklamakan Desert. Archaeological evidence from reclamation zones and irrigation channels at the site indicates a substantial historical reliance on water resources, in sharp contrast to the current arid conditions. However, the processes of paleo-drainage reorganization and associated environmental evolution around the TWCS remain poorly understood. In this study, paleo-channel traces were mapped through geomorphological interpretation of multi-source remote sensing data, supported by field investigations, geomorphological surveys, and archaeological excavations. Sedimentary stratigraphic analysis and optically stimulated luminescence (OSL) dating were used to investigate local changes in depositional conditions. The results reveal numerous paleo-channel traces west of and within the TWCS, indicating a high concentration of former channel features around the site. Five OSL ages from a natural profile approximately 11 km northwest of the TWCS constrain local depositional changes between approximately 1500 and 710 yr B.P., including a transition from gravel-dominated to sand-dominated sedimentation. Excavated channel deposits within the site provide evidence of fluvial activity during its occupation. These findings demonstrate the value of integrating historical satellite imagery and unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)-derived terrain data for mapping paleo-channel traces in arid archaeological landscapes and provide a basis for further investigation of hydrological change and settlement dynamics in southern Xinjiang.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vegetation restoration can increase vegetation cover while raising water demand in water-limited regions. Most drought-vegetation assessments focus on atmospheric and soil moisture droughts. The added value of groundwater information remains insufficiently quantified. This study developed a multilayer drought-vegetation loss framework for northern China during 2003–2022. It linked three single and four Copula-based compound drought types to leaf area index (LAI) negative-anomaly events. Compared with the atmosphere-soil framework, adding groundwater enabled additional drought matches for 16.3% of all assessable LAI loss events, including those unmatched by either framework. Most additions involved groundwater co-occurring with or connecting other water-stress layers. Compound drought dominated areas expanded from 20.7% to 58.6% of the valid area between 2003–2012 and 2013–2022. Groundwater-drought-dominated areas increased from 4.3% to 21.2%. Groundwater drought showed the most pronounced nonlinear association with cumulative relative vegetation loss (LOSStot) among single drought types. LOSStot peaked near an index of −2.16 and declined at more negative values. XGBoost-SHAP analysis identified event-period hydroclimatic anomalies as the largest contributor to relative predictive importance. These findings support ecological restoration planning tailored to local drought characteristics, water availability and vegetation vulnerability.

Abstract (英文): Improper disposal of solid waste is a major source of environmental pollution, making timely detection essential for protecting environmental and human health. Although several remote sensing datasets have recently been proposed for solid waste detection, most are limited in terms of spatial coverage, temporal diversity, annotation quality, and resolution. In this work, we introduce WastePNOA, a publicly available dataset based on Very-High-Resolution (VHR) aircraft imagery from the Spanish National Aerial Orthophotography Plan, also known as PNOA. The dataset comprises 24,884 images from 132 landfill areas across Spain between 2013 and 2023. The dataset provides pixel-level annotations across three semantic classes: waste, leachate pond, and background. We introduce two dataset distributions, WastePNOA-Spatial and WastePNOA-Temporal, to investigate the presence of waste across geographically unseen areas and the temporal evolution of previously observed areas, respectively, while also assessing the effect of background-only images. SegFormer consistently achieves the strongest overall performance, reaching 58.59% and 67.30% foreground-only mIoU for spatial and temporal generalization, respectively. Results also highlight the importance of background-only images for robust performance under realistic background-dominant scenes, while indicating that retaining all such images may be redundant. Overall, WastePNOA provides a challenging benchmark for solid waste disposal site detection and supports future research in remote-sensing-based environmental monitoring.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Loess landslides exhibit strong spatial heterogeneity and complex rainfall responses, making their evolution difficult to characterize from one-dimensional deformation observations alone. This study investigates the Li-Kan Highway landslide on the right bank of the Lijiaxia Reservoir, China, by integrating ascending- and descending-track time-series InSAR, aspect-parallel flow (APF) constrained three-dimensional deformation reconstruction, the Comprehensive Standardized Deformation Index (CSDI), wavelet-based rainfall-response analysis, and Massflow numerical simulation. The reconstructed deformation field shows that the landslide is dominated by northeastward horizontal motion, while local sectors exhibit distinct movement directions and deformation components, indicating multiple kinematic units. Along the approximately 2 km P–P′ profile, the north–south, east–west, and vertical deformation–velocity components exhibit spatially variable patterns, consistent with heterogeneous slope kinematics. Principal component analysis shows that the first two components explain 85.6% of the total variance, summarizing the spatial variability. CSDI analysis of representative points P1–P3 reveals asynchronous deformation activity: P1 is mainly active in autumn 2018, P2 shows enhanced activity in 2019 and spring–summer 2020, and P3 exhibits a weaker local increase around mid-2021. In particular, the 2020 response at P2 is interpreted as a local kinematic adjustment rather than synchronous acceleration of the entire slope. Because no overall failure occurred during the observation period, CSDI-defined Stage III is treated as enhanced deformation activity rather than a direct failure criterion. Continuous wavelet transform and time-lag cross-correlation indicate significant rainfall-related temporal responses, with deformation lagging rainfall by approximately 12, 108, and 36 days at P1, P2, and P3, respectively. Massflow simulation of a potential frontal failure scenario shows a rapid transition from source-area release to channelized high-speed runout and frontal deposition. The maximum simulated velocity reaches approximately 22.6 m/s in the main transport channel, while the frontal deposit exceeds 16 m in thickness and remains about 13.6 m at 200 s. The total simulated mass decreases by only about 0.38%, indicating good numerical mass conservation. These results demonstrate that the Li-Kan Highway landslide evolves through spatially heterogeneous and temporally asynchronous deformation, with rainfall responses varying among slope sectors. The integrated framework links long-term deformation monitoring, stage identification, rainfall-response analysis, and potential failure simulation, providing a practical basis for interpreting the evolution and hazard potential of large loess landslides.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Shipborne high-frequency surface wave radar (SHFSWR) plays a critical role in maritime surveillance but suffers from severe performance degradation in complex maritime scenarios characterized by sea clutter, dense target aggregation, and weak echo returns. To address these challenges, we propose Shipborne Surface Wave Radar You Only Look Once (SSWR-YOLO), a lightweight detection network built upon the YOLOv8 framework with specialized modules for robust detection under adverse conditions. Specifically, a receptive field spatial attention convolution is embedded into the backbone to dynamically enhance target responses relative to broadened sea clutter and highlight weak target features. Furthermore, a context aggregation network is integrated into the neck to enhance multi-scale feature fusion, effectively resolving target masking in dense vessel target scenarios. Additionally, a softened non-maximum suppression strategy is employed to prevent the erroneous removal of overlapped vessel proposals. Experimental results on both simulated and field SHFSWR data demonstrate that, on the 50 held-out test images, SSWR-YOLO achieves a 9.76 percentage point improvement in detection rate and a 3.11% reduction in overall false discovery rate over the baseline YOLOv8, while introducing only marginal parameter overhead. The proposed method offers an efficient and reliable solution for vessel detection under challenging maritime conditions, with a core network forward pass compatible with near-real-time processing on a desktop GPU and potential for embedded deployment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): RGB-thermal infrared (RGBT) target tracking integrates spatial features of both RGB and thermal infrared (TIR) modalities, providing a discriminative basis for target feature representation and state estimation. However, existing methods suffer from insufficient cross-modal feature interaction and inadequate spatio-temporal contextual correlation, which limits tracking stability and accuracy in challenging scenarios. To address these issues, we propose MSTrack, a novel tracker that integrates a multi-modal feature aggregation (MMFA) module and a spatio-temporal memory context encoding (STMCE) module. First, to capture both shared features and complementary details across modalities, we design the MMFA module, which employs parallel Swin Transformers and four cross-modal feature interaction (CMFI) components to achieve progressive fusion of RGB and TIR features. Specifically, CMFI integrates a cross-modal collaborative attention mechanism, a triplet attention mechanism, a bidirectional cross-modal weighted fusion mechanism, and a multi-stage information aggregation mechanism to achieve cross-modal interactions. Second, to fuse the key spatio-temporal features across multiple frames, we design the STMCE module. This STMCE module leverages a historical feature memory pool (HFMP) and a spatio-temporal context encoder (STCE) to model global dependencies among the template, current, and historical frames. Finally, the prediction head outputs the target bounding box. Experiments on four ground-based RGBT datasets demonstrate that the proposed tracker outperforms other state-of-the-art trackers. Furthermore, the results on the UAV-based VTUAV dataset validate its generalization potential. Specifically, MSTrack achieves PR/SR scores of 0.877/0.637 on RGBT234 and 0.669/0.530 on LasHeR. On the VTUAV dataset, it achieves MPR/MSR scores of 0.812/0.672 on the short-term subset and 0.595/0.501 on the long-term subset.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The three-dimensional architecture of the trunk and branch system of individual trees constitutes essential information for tree breeding trait evaluation, the construction of functional-structural plant models (FSPM), and precision forest management; its morphological traits are typically represented by diameter at breast height (DBH), tree height, branch order, and the 3D branching topology. In recent years, point-cloud-based tree structure parsing has advanced rapidly. However, constrained by field acquisition conditions and sensor principles, tree point clouds commonly exhibit missing data and noise, causing locally non-uniform density and introducing noise interference. Under these conditions, traditional QSM (Quantitative Structure Models) tend to produce cross-section distortion and inflated volume, degrading both modeling accuracy and the reliability of parameter extraction; moreover, their radius estimation usually relies on species-specific calibration, limiting cross-species application. To address these limitations, this study proposes a high-precision three-dimensional reconstruction method for the trunk and branch system to extract tree morphological phenotypes, which achieves robust reconstruction under noise and missing data through density-adaptive clustering and biologically mechanistic self-adaptive WBE radius simulation. Experimental results show that, compared with existing mainstream methods, the proposed method yields better modeling performance and stronger robustness on both artificial and real branch point clouds, reaching centimeter-level accuracy.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Roof plane segmentation from airborne light detection and ranging (LiDAR) point clouds provides planar patches for reconstructing 3D roof surfaces and their structural relationships. Existing methods still find it difficult to balance semantic awareness and geometric consistency, especially in the presence of ambiguous boundaries between adjacent roof planes, scattered outlier or non-roof points, and diverse roof structures. To address this problem, this paper proposes a semantic–geometric collaborative framework for roof plane segmentation that uses semantic segmentation results to guide geometric clustering. First, semantic segmentation identifies planar interiors and inter-plane boundaries, with an additional noise or non-roof class where applicable. Second, a k-nearest neighbor (KNN) local-priority clustering method is designed to generate the initial segmentation. It selects seeds from reliable planar interiors and uses a double-ended queue to guide roof plane clusters to grow from stable interior regions toward uncertain boundaries. Third, a multi-neighbor edge growth (MEG) strategy with adaptive residual constraints is introduced to refine the initial segmentation. For each initial roof plane, the plane model and residual threshold are estimated from its own geometric distribution, and the remaining unassigned non-noise points are assigned according to point-to-plane distances and neighboring plane relationships. Under the evaluation procedure used in this study, the proposed method achieves strong instance-level scores and competitive point-level scores on the WHU-Roof, RoofN3D, and Building3D datasets. On the WHU-Roof dataset, it achieves 86.8% coverage (Cov), 91.3% weighted coverage (WCov), and 97.4% F1-score. On the RoofN3D dataset, it achieves the highest Cov of 85.6%, with a WCov of 90.3%. On the Building3D dataset, it achieves 82.7% Cov, 88.4% WCov, and 95.8% F1-score. These results support combining semantic boundary information with geometric clustering to separate adjacent roof planes and refine their boundaries on the evaluated datasets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The accurate acquisition of meteorological variables, such as temperature and humidity, is critical for diverse societal and industrial applications. Meteorological retrieval, defined as the process of deriving atmospheric variables from satellite-observed radiances, remains a pivotal research domain. Existing meteorological retrieval generally relies on single-sensor or point-wise approaches. Single-sensor systems lack full vertical coverage due to spectral limits, and point-wise retrieval ignores the spatial context required to capture horizontal atmospheric flow. To overcome these dual limitations, we propose a framework that integrates multi-sensor fusion with direct 3D spatial retrieval. Our approach not only leverages the complementarity of infrared hyperspectral and microwave data to penetrate clouds and resolve vertical structures, but also utilizes 3D modeling to explicitly capture spatial correlations. This synergy is particularly effective for dynamic variables like humidity. By simultaneously expanding the spectral and spatial fields of view, our model successfully reconstructs a robust and continuous 3D atmospheric field. Quantitatively, under the ERA5-based evaluation, MS3D reduces the MAE of temperature and specific humidity by 18.9% and 23.9%, respectively, compared with the pixel-wise IReA baseline, and by 10.8% and 7.9%, respectively, compared with the 3D-field ViT baseline. Furthermore, experiments conducted under cloudy conditions validate that the 3D meteorological fields constructed via multi-sensor fusion possess the robustness to effectively handle complex meteorological scenarios.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Underground peat fires are difficult to detect using satellite observations because their thermal signatures can be weak and spatially mixed with vegetation, water, and cloud contamination, particularly in tropical peatland environments. This study proposes a cloud-aware multispectral framework for detecting potential underground peat-fire thermal anomalies from Moderate-Resolution Imaging Spectroradiometer (MODIS) observations over the Khuan Khreng Peat Swamp Forest, Thailand. The framework integrates a proposed First-Order Neighborhood Mean-Shift (FNM) segmentation algorithm, valid-land masking, multispectral feature representation, and Support Vector Machine (SVM) classification. FNM constrains feature updates within a bounded first-order neighborhood to preserve local spatial continuity while reducing computational complexity. On an annotated Landsat 8 segmentation benchmark, FNM achieved 92.00% accuracy, a 91.24% F1-score, and an 83.91% intersection over union (IoU), with an average runtime of 0.96 s per image. A masking-ablation experiment further demonstrated the contribution of FNM to downstream classification: FNM + SVM increased the F1-score from 57.75% without masking to 65.06% and reduced the false alarm rate from 7.82% to 5.88%. For the 2012–2015 Khuan Khreng evaluation dataset, the complete framework detected 95 of 118 reference fire observations and achieved an overall accuracy of 93.02%, recall of 80.51%, specificity of 94.12%, and F1-score of 65.07%. Feature-ablation experiments showed that thermal brightness-temperature variables provided the primary discriminatory information, while reflectance variables contributed complementary information, with the complete five-variable configuration achieving the highest classification performance. These findings demonstrate that the proposed FNM–SVM framework provides an effective integration of spatial segmentation, cloud-aware valid-land masking, and multispectral classification for mapping potential underground peat-fire thermal anomalies in cloud-prone tropical peatland environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral unmixing is a pivotal technique for analyzing mixed pixels in remote sensing imagery. However, the presence of spectral variability in crop hyperspectral images significantly compromises the accuracy of traditional unmixing methods. To address this challenge, this paper proposes a novel Endmember Adaptive Stacked Coding Network (EASCoN). The core of EASCoN is an innovative dual-branch architecture. The endmember encoding branch learns a pixel-dependent latent representation from the input spectrum and uses it to support adaptive endmember reconstruction, while the spatial–spectral branch extracts discriminative contextual features. A bidirectional cooperative optimization between the two branches is achieved through a spectral agent attention module and a weight-sharing mechanism, enabling mutual enhancement of spectrally adaptive features and spatial-contextual information. Furthermore, a comprehensive loss function incorporating multiple physical constraints is introduced to ensure the physical plausibility and spectral fidelity of the unmixing results. Extensive experiments conducted on the dedicated Wenchang and Wenjiao crop hyperspectral datasets, as well as the public Samson benchmark, demonstrate that EASCoN outperforms state-of-the-art methods across multiple evaluation metrics.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Frequent remote detection of deforestation is essential for rainforest conservation but remains challenging due to seasonal vegetation dynamics, biome heterogeneity, cloud cover, and anthropogenic disturbances. Traditional monitoring requires hundreds of expert hours monthly; deep learning reduces this workload but often lacks interpretability and expert-level reliability under strong class imbalance. We present a hybrid human–AI workflow integrating interpretable machine learning with expert validation to accelerate deforestation monitoring in the Brazilian Legal Amazon. The system was trained on 2.8 million manually labeled Sentinel-2 image pairs, each containing a candidate deforestation event preselected by automated change detection, plus vegetation-index time series and distance to the nearest water body. Our approach combines Convolutional Neural Networks for spatial features with a Recurrent Neural Network for temporal dynamics, fused through logistic regression to enable transparent estimation of feature-source contributions. During AI-assisted deployment, the model achieved F1 = 0.75 (precision 0.81, recall 0.70), ROC AUC 0.993, and PR AUC 0.781 against final analyst-validated labels. These AI predictions feed a graphical user interface that supports expert validation, cutting manual annotation time by 45 %. Deployed since October 2024 in Imazon’s Sistema de Alerta ao Desmatamento (SAD), these results demonstrate that interpretable model fusion with expert oversight enables scalable, accurate, and transparent tropical forest monitoring.
