Abstract (英文): Event-based UAV small-target segmentation requires recovering sparse and incomplete foreground events from structured background activity. Longer observation windows provide additional target evidence but also accumulate clutter, making temporal feature integration critical for accurate segmentation. We propose an Evolution-Aware Event Segmentation Network (EAES-Net) that models local transitions between adjacent feature states within a sparse encoder–decoder. The Temporal Evolution Encoder (TEE) jointly encodes neighboring states and their difference to construct transition representations. Local Evolution Consistency Aggregation (LECA) evaluates the cosine agreement of successive transitions at each spatial location and aggregates evolution evidence using spatially varying weights. Evolution-Guided Refinement (EGR) uses a learned gate to combine evolution-aware information with the original sparse features before decoding. A foreground consistency loss further regularizes adjacent target representations during training. Experiments on the EV-UAV benchmark demonstrate improvements in foreground-event segmentation, target detection, and false-alarm suppression over the evaluated reference methods. Component and design ablations quantify the contributions of the proposed modules, while temporal sensitivity experiments examine performance across different observation windows and temporal resolutions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Small deformations of large, steep rock slopes may be comparable in magnitude to cross-epoch point-cloud registration errors. Including deforming areas in registration can suppress change signals and introduce apparent changes in stable areas. This study proposes stable-area-constrained bridging registration (SACBR) and applies it to the Feishuiyan quarry slope at the Shuangjiangkou Hydropower Station. Field-observed cracks and surface monitoring monument data are used to select relatively stable reference areas. Cross-epoch rigid transformations are estimated using only stable overlapping areas, and a bridging scan transfers the reference frame to the remaining scans acquired within the same epoch. The two registration workflows are compared using representative cross-sections, surface distances calculated by multiscale model-to-model cloud comparison (M3C2), and statistics for monument neighborhoods. Compared with general whole-cloud registration, SACBR reduces systematic offsets in relatively stable areas and preserves clearer cross-epoch differences in deforming areas. In the selected relatively stable patch, the median signed M3C2 distance was −105.56 mm under general registration and −4.39 mm under SACBR. Because this patch was used to estimate the SACBR cross-epoch transformation, its statistics describe local surface agreement rather than providing an independent estimate of registration accuracy. For each selected monument, we calculated the absolute value of the mean signed M3C2 distance in its neighborhood. The deforming-to-stable-area ratio of the group means was 1.56 under general registration and 2.39 under SACBR. The higher ratio under SACBR describes a stronger contrast between the two sampled groups, not an improvement in registration accuracy. These findings support improved geometric consistency in stable areas and preservation of small-deformation responses under multi-station conditions. Independent check data are needed to determine whether SACBR improves registration accuracy outside the reference terrain.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Characterizing ecological-release (ER) disturbances and their short-term predictability in small-hydropower cascades requires integrating continuous monitoring with satellite observations. Using 677 days of ER records from 16 small hydropower plants (SHPs) in the Taohe River Basin and satellite-derived water-surface fraction (WSF) indicators from Sentinel-2, Landsat 8/9, and an experimental Sentinel-1 proxy across 32 fixed monitoring windows, we developed a capacity-weighted ecological-release disturbance index (ERDI), evaluated retrospective WSF–release associations, and assessed short-term ERDI predictability using topology-informed ridge regression. The results showed that daily ERDI rankings were robust to seven component-weight perturbations (Spearman’s ρ = 0.996–0.999), whereas plant-level contributions were sensitive to cascade aggregation. Multi-sensor WSF exhibited positive associations with contemporaneous ER, with variations across sensors and monitoring locations. The coefficients of determination (R2) ranged from 0.5702 to 0.6788 for optical sensors and from 0.6213 to 0.6635 for the experimental Sentinel-1 proxy. Incorporating contemporaneous WSF increased out-of-sample R2 from 0.141 to 0.392 on a common matched sample, suggesting additional explanatory information on ER. Comparative topology analysis selected a sparse three-neighbor upstream structure that retained 63.5% of the prescribed upstream connection weights using only 42 directed edges. Initial counterfactual comparisons indicated positive RMSE-skill differences relative to station-local, direction-reversed, and randomly rewired topologies at the 5- and 7-day horizons, whereas the advantages were inconsistent at the 1- and 3-day horizons. The combined forecasting configuration yielded reported out-of-sample R2 values of 0.866 and 0.499 at the 1- and 7-day horizons, respectively, with RMSE skill values relative to persistence of 52.63%, 42.56%, 45.82%, and 37.18% at the 1-, 3-, 5-, and 7-day horizons, respectively. However, the WSF-augmented configuration did not consistently outperform the baseline, with lower reported R2 values at the 5- and 7-day horizons. These findings support operational release disturbance identification, reach-specific monitoring, and short-term ER management in cascaded small-hydropower systems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Drought can promote fire anomalies through atmospheric drying, soil-water depletion and sustained climatic water-balance deficit, but these pathways are often correlated. We analyzed forest-grassland fire anomalies across China at 1 degree resolution using a three-dimensional drought-response framework that combines drought type, standardized intensity, and 1–8 month duration/timing windows. For each grid, VPD (Vapor Pressure Deficit), soil water and SPEI windows were screened with Spearman correlation, checked with AICc, and then entered full-subset AICc models with Shapley contribution decomposition. Among 362 classified grids, VPD-dominant, SPEI-dominant and soil-water-dominant responses accounted for 21.0%, 17.7% and 8.6%, respectively. Shared drought signals accounted for 20.2%, while no-clear and uncertain responses accounted for 21.5% and 6.9%. Most selected windows were supported by AICc, with delta AICc no larger than 2 in 77.9% of soil-water grids, 71.9% of SPEI grids and 65.9% of VPD grids. These results show that drought-related fire anomalies in China are spatially heterogeneous and cannot be represented by a single drought metric. These classifications represent relative model-supported statistical associations rather than causal dominance by individual drought processes. Regional fire-risk assessment should therefore combine atmospheric, soil-water and hydroclimatic drought information.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Solar radiation over mountainous terrain varies considerably with slope orientation and local topography, but this variability is not adequately represented when radiation measured on a horizontal surface is applied directly to hillslopes. We examined this issue using one year of simultaneous radiation observations from a valley site and slope-parallel measurements on sunny and shady slopes on the eastern Tibetan Plateau. The observations were combined with Digital Elevation Model‌ (DEM)-based catchment calculations and idealized south-facing slope calculations driven by observations from eight stations across China to assess hillslope radiation contrasts, the influence of DEM resolution, and site-specific responses to slope angle. The contrast between sunny and shady slopes was most pronounced in winter. Relative to the valley, downward shortwave radiation increased by nearly 100 W m−2 on the sunny slope but decreased by more than 100 W m−2 on the shady slope. When the diffuse-radiation fraction was low, the asymmetry index exceeded 0.7 in December and January. The winter daytime peak on the sunny slope reached 943.45 W m−2, or 1.43 times that measured in the valley. Net shortwave radiation differences were further decomposed into contributions from incident radiation and albedo, distinguishing differences in radiation receipt from those in surface absorption. Selecting terrain cells that more closely matched the observed slope reduced the monthly mean bias for the shady slope from 26.6 to 3.7 W m−2. At the catchment scale, the valley measurements were approximately 14% higher than the modeled mean in autumn and 19% higher in winter. Although coarsening the DEM had little effect on the seasonal cycle, it reduced the simulated spatial contrast: the winter difference between sunny and shady slopes declined from 107.8 W m−2 at 30 m resolution to 77.7 W m−2 at 90 m. Across the eight stations, slope responses varied with season and local radiation conditions. Exploratory clustering identified three response patterns within this sample: slope suppression, seasonal-background control, and winter amplification. These findings demonstrate the value of slope-parallel observations and terrain matching for evaluating hillslope radiation estimates, while highlighting the dependence of slope-related radiation gains on seasonal and local conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Chinese Mini SAR satellites, such as Dianjian-1, Shenqi-2, and the Huantian satellite (DP-15), are domestically developed high-resolution synthetic aperture radar (SAR) remote sensing satellites. With satellite masses of approximately 285–330 kg, these satellites are capable of rapid emergency observation and can acquire high-resolution surface information under cloudy and rainy conditions, providing critical data support for emergency flood monitoring and disaster assessment. Taking the Guangxi flood event on 6 July 2026, as a case study, this study utilizes high-resolution SAR imagery acquired by Shenqi-2 on 6 July 2026 (11 h after the dam failure) and Dianjian-1 on 7 July 2026 (25 h after the dam failure) to identify the flood extent and analyze its spatial characteristics. Sentinel-2 optical imagery and SAR imagery from the DP-15 (acquired on 7 July 2026, 24 h after the dam failure) are also used for cross-validation. The results show that the surfaces of the two reservoirs contracted rapidly on the day of the dam failure (6 July), while approximately 25.37 km2 of new inundated area emerged west of Jiajian–Futang Village in Yunbiao Town downstream. The floodwaters primarily propagated along river valleys, drainage channels, and relatively flat agricultural areas. The Sentinel-2 images obtained six days after the disaster still have about half of the spatial overlap. By 7 July, the combined area of the two reservoirs had decreased by a further 42.29%, while the downstream inundated area had receded to 11.46 km2. Building on these results, the flood-mapping capabilities of Dianjian-1, Shenqi-2, and Sentinel-1 were compared based on image-level land-cover details and backscatter profiles across representative land-cover types. The results indicate that Dianjian-1 and Shenqi-2 preserved water–land boundaries and small-scale water-body features more completely than Sentinel-1. The mean response ranges across representative water–land boundaries in the two high-resolution SAR datasets were approximately 17.5–20.5 m, whereas those of Sentinel-1 were approximately 3–4 times wider. For roads, the response ranges of Sentinel-1 were approximately 2–5 times wider than those of the high-resolution SAR datasets, indicating that high-resolution SAR satellites can capture spatial variations in land-surface backscatter within narrower spatial ranges. In densely built-up flood-affected areas, the X-band imagery exhibited more pronounced responses than the C-band imagery. Overall, small high-resolution SAR satellites demonstrate strong capabilities for rapid and fine-scale flood mapping, providing timely and detailed data support for emergency flood monitoring and disaster assessment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monitoring surface subsidence disasters induced by underground extraction is crucial for hazard mitigation. Distributed Scatterer Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar (DS-InSAR, DSI) enables the monitoring of deformation in overburden strata above active mining faces; however, its accuracy is often degraded by spatiotemporal decorrelation. While phase linking (PL) algorithms can recover these phases, their accuracy is constrained by severe sample coherence matrix (SCM) bias—due to underutilized spatial information—and data redundancy. To address these issues, this study proposes a DSI algorithm tailored to complex mining-induced deformation: (1) for SCM construction, a structure-aware distance metric introduces spatial-domain pixel homogeneity to compress the SCM; (2) to mitigate data redundancy, a signal-to-noise ratio constrained by the equivalent number of looks is adopted to construct a high-coherence interferometric baseline network; and (3) to resolve the non-positive-definite SCM issue, an eigen-decomposition-based strategy is introduced to determine suitable regularization parameters. Simulation and real-data experiments confirm effective phase extraction. Validation against field leveling shows that the proposed method achieves a high coefficient of determination (R2) of 0.9811 and minimizes the Root Mean Square Error (RMSE) to 8.24 mm, improving monitoring accuracy by up to 69.1% compared to traditional methods. These results establish its practical value for mining disaster monitoring.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Observations of nearshore processes face distinct temporal limitations: submarine groundwater discharge (SGD) plume surveys typically provide snapshots, while visible-band waterline monitoring is restricted to daylight hours. Because thermal infrared (TIR) cameras measure emitted longwave radiance, they can image surface thermal structure during both day and night. Here, we present a tower-based, oblique TIR imaging technique for continuous, process-scale monitoring of submarine groundwater discharge (SGD) and nearshore hydrodynamics. A 6-month field study on the shallow reef flat off Ofu Island, Territory of American Samoa, used an autonomous TIR camera system that collected one image every minute, together with in-situ meteorological and oceanographic observations. A processing workflow was developed to reduce the dominant geometric and radiometric biases associated with oblique coastal TIR imaging. After correction, the imagery resolved the surface expression of SGD through repeated tidal cycles and identified shoreline waterline position and wave-breaking patterns during both day and night. Tracked waterline elevations were strongly correlated with gauge-derived water levels paired at 1-min intervals during long-term assessment. Over constrained intervals, radiometrically corrected TIR imagery could also be converted to water temperature, indicating the potential for accurate surface-temperature estimates. These results demonstrate that tower-based TIR imaging can provide continuous, high-resolution observations of thermal heterogeneity and hydrodynamics in shallow nearshore environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reliable forecasting of floating algal accumulation is important for monitoring ecological conditions and supporting the management of drinking-water reservoirs, yet accurate prediction remains challenging because floating algal accumulation varies substantially across space and time under complex environmental forcing. Existing forecasting approaches are commonly limited by insufficient spatial resolution and poor interpretability. In this study, we developed an explainable pixel-wise temporal forecasting framework with spatially distributed inputs by integrating Harmonized Landsat and Sentinel-2 (HLS) observations (2013–2026) with ERA5-Land meteorological reanalysis to estimate floating algal accumulation across satellite observation gaps. The framework was constructed using 339 HLS observations retained after quality control, spatial completeness screening, and spatial reconstruction, together with continuous meteorological forcing over 111,947 stable water pixels. The Floating Algae Index (FAI) was used to characterize the intensity of floating algal accumulation, and representative machine learning and deep learning models were evaluated. Among all evaluated models, the Light Gradient Boosting Machine (LightGBM) achieved the highest mean R2 (0.437) for pixel-level FAI regression. Prediction performance was relatively more reliable for short satellite observation gaps (1–3 days), whereas date-level predictive skill became increasingly variable and generally deteriorated as the observation gap increased. SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP) analysis identified the historical FAI as the dominant predictor, whereas solar radiation and air temperature were the most influential meteorological predictors with pronounced nonlinear response patterns. The proposed framework integrates temporally discontinuous HLS observations with continuous ERA5-Land meteorological forcing, providing an interpretable approach for retrospective pixel-level FAI estimation across satellite observation gaps, with potential for future forecasting applications using operational meteorological predictions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Widespread, long-term outbreaks of the aspen leafminer (ALM) are posing a growing threat to North American boreal forests. Current monitoring relies heavily on annual Aerial Detection Surveys (ADS), which are costly and logistically constrained and result in spatial and temporal gaps in monitoring. To address this limitation, we developed a scalable, deep learning-based framework to automatically map ALM damage across Interior Alaska using a U-Net architecture with a ResNet-50 backbone. We evaluated two sensor-specific models, one trained on high-resolution (3 m) commercial PlanetScope imagery and the other on moderate-resolution (10 m) open-access Sentinel-2 imagery. At an independent test region, the PlanetScope workflow detected more of the reference damage (true positive rate 0.80 vs. 0.64) and achieved a higher F1 score (0.53 vs. 0.36) and ROC AUC (0.89 vs. 0.75). Both workflows mapped more damage than the manually digitized reference, which probably does not capture all affected stands, and a large share of their detections fell within deciduous and mixed forest. At spatially distant sites, the Sentinel-2 workflow mapped damage more extensively than the PlanetScope workflow, and both detected damage beyond ADS flight lines, including at a site not surveyed since 2020. These results show a trade-off between the two workflows and indicate that such models can complement aerial surveys by providing continuous, landscape-level maps of ALM damage under a changing climate.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large ancient landslides are widespread in tectonically active mountainous regions, and their reactivation poses a serious threat to communities in high-mountain gorge regions. However, reactivation mechanisms remain poorly understood for ancient landslides with locked segments and weak deformation responses to rainfall. This study investigated the deformation characteristics and reactivation mechanisms of the Bogexi ancient landslide in the Batang Fault Zone, eastern Tibetan Plateau, using high-resolution optical remote-sensing interpretation, field investigations, unmanned aerial vehicle photogrammetry, small baseline subset interferometric synthetic aperture radar (SBAS-InSAR) time-series deformation analysis, and numerical simulations. The results indicate that the landslide is approximately 2500 m long and 600–800 m wide, with an elevation difference of 1268 m and an estimated volume of 3.5–4.5 × 107 m3. It comprises two landslide bodies (I and II) separated by a central locked segment. SBAS-InSAR observations from 2017 to 2025 recorded the maximum cumulative displacement reached −2855 mm, and the maximum slope-parallel deformation rate was −362.5 mm/a. Deformation was concentrated in the central and frontal parts of landslide body I and throughout landslide body II. The central locked segment underwent creep at approximately 91.2 mm/a. These findings reveal a deformation pattern involving pushing from the rear section, resistance from the central locked segment, and traction at the toe. The landslide is dominated by long-term gravity-driven creep, and the locked segment controls the deformation. Numerical simulations indicated that seismic surface amplification may promote rupture propagation and coalescence within the locked segment. Under combined seismic and rainfall conditions, the maximum landslide displacement was predicted to reach 15 m, approximately 5.4 times that under natural conditions. The reactivation of the landslide can be divided into three stages: long-term creep, accelerated deformation driven by endogenic and exogenic geological processes, and instability following the failure of the locked segment. These findings would support identification and hazard assessment of such landslides in the alpine gorge regions of the Tibetan Plateau.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Continuous monthly surface-water records are essential for assessing hydrological variability in large river basins, but gaps and misclassifications in historical optical observations limit long-term analysis. We integrate label correction, precipitation-guided filling and temporal reconstruction to map monthly surface-water dynamics in the Yellow River Basin during 2000–2024. We first correct anomalous labels within persistent water bodies using annual classifications and long-term occurrence frequency from the Joint Research Centre (JRC) Global Surface Water record. Precipitation-guided filling resolves gaps using historical occurrence, while a Static-Guided Temporal U-Net (SGTU-Net) reconstructs residual gaps from monthly sequences, river-network and terrain information. The resulting 30 m Yellow River Basin Monthly Surface Water Dynamics dataset (YRB-MSWD) combines historical JRC reconstructions with recent Sentinel-derived maps. Monthly quality-control labels distinguish retained observations from corrected or reconstructed pixels. Validation at sampled variable-water and adjacent-land locations using Google Earth reference labels yielded an overall accuracy of 0.917 and a Kappa coefficient of 0.830. Mapped reservoir areas were positively associated with observed water levels, with mean r = 0.903 and mean R2 = 0.816. YRB-MSWD provides a continuous monthly record for analysing long-term surface-water dynamics across the basin.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Atmospheric correction of multispectral optical remote-sensing imagery aims to recover surface reflectance from top-of-atmosphere (TOA) observations and serves as a fundamental preprocessing step for quantitative remote-sensing applications. Existing deep-learning approaches generally formulate the conversion from TOA to bottom-of-atmosphere (BOA) reflectance as one-way supervised regression, without explicitly enforcing radiative-transfer consistency between the predicted BOA and original TOA observations. We propose the Physics-Constrained Deep Atmospheric Correction Network (PCACNet), which incorporates differentiable radiative-transfer closure into deep learning-based atmospheric correction. A large paired TOA-BOA simulation database with realistic spectral priors is first generated by driving the 6S radiative transfer model with real BOA spectra. A frozen but differentiable radiative transfer model emulator is subsequently incorporated into the training framework to establish a TOA–BOA–TOA differentiable radiative-transfer closure loop. PCACNet further employs separate encoders for spectral, atmospheric, and observation geometry, and an uncertainty-aware progressive learning strategy with reliability weighting based on TOA reconstruction residuals. On an independent simulated test set, PCACNet reduced BOA inversion RMSE and TOA reconstruction RMSE by 50.6% and 43.3%, respectively, relative to the best-performing comparison method, SAAC-Net. Evaluation on real Sentinel-2 imagery further supported the spatial and temporal transferability of PCACNet across the examined regions and acquisition periods, as demonstrated by consistent agreement with Sen2Cor-derived L2A products in spatial patterns, spectral characteristics, and remote-sensing indices. These findings support the effectiveness of the proposed physics-constrained framework under controlled simulation conditions and its applicability to real Sentinel-2 observations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Earlier heatwaves and spring precipitation deficits increasingly threaten the ‘drought escape’ strategy of winter wheat (Triticum aestivum L.) in Central–Eastern Europe. We compared canopy degradation dynamics across 30 commercial winter wheat parcels in Eastern Hungary on deep Calcic Chernozems (Hajdúböszörmény, Fields 1–15) and compaction-limited Meadow/Solonetzic soils (Nádudvar, Fields 16–30). Sentinel-2 time series (NDVI, NDRE, NDWI, and MSI) were combined with continuous soil moisture profiles (10–75 cm) and agrometeorological records for the 2025/2026 season. Topsoil moisture (10–20 cm) was weakly related to canopy status at both sites. Canopy–moisture correlations strengthened with depth on the Chernozem (r = 0.64 at 75 cm for NDVI) but peaked at 30–45 cm on the Solonetzic soil (r = 0.40–0.44); these gradients are descriptive, because none was significant after correcting for autocorrelation. Double-logistic fits against growing degree days (GDD) placed canopy senescence (NDVI = 0.60) 96.8 °C-days earlier at Nádudvar (95% CI 7.3–163.4; about 3 days in fitted dates), a direction that held across 36 sensitivity specifications and in per-field comparisons (p = 0.03). Heat-stress days (Tmax ≥ 25 °C) shifted per-day rates towards desiccation at both sites, with no significant between-site difference at that threshold. Piecewise regression identified soil moisture tipping points of 15.9% VWC (60 cm, Chernozem) and 9.3% VWC (30 cm, Solonetzic soil), which per-field estimates reproduced. These single-season results suggest that root-zone architecture influences the timing of canopy collapse and provide candidate thresholds for precision risk zoning.

Abstract (英文): Pumice rafts from submarine volcanic eruptions can drift over long distances, disrupting navigation, fisheries, and coastal ecosystems. Their changing distribution calls for detection methods that can be applied repeatedly with limited manual intervention. We developed a method combining spectral discrimination and one-class Gaussian mixture models (GMMs) using observations of the 2021 Fukutoku-Oka-no-Ba eruption from the Medium Resolution Spectral Imager II (MERSI-II) aboard Fengyun-3D (FY-3D). The pumice index (PI) and four complementary indices (I1–I4) were calculated from four visible and near-infrared bands and jointly thresholded across each input image. Initial index thresholds were set manually, and pumice regions of interest in the 16 August and 12 September results were manually confirmed and used to fit a separate GMM for each date. The fitted models were then applied to the independent 22 September acquisition without updating their parameters or acceptance thresholds. Evaluation against visually interpreted reference labels covered 669,653 pixels within manually delineated assessment ROIs near known pumice rafts across the three dates. The spectral-index method achieved precision of 94.35–97.00%. On the 269,658 assessment pixels for 22 September, the GMM union achieved 97.02% precision, 74.45% recall, and a Cohen’s kappa of 0.84. Relative to spectral-index detection, precision and recall increased by 2.66 and 14.91 percentage points, respectively, with a relative reduction of 36.86% in the omission rate. Additional detections occurred along fragmented bands, patch margins, and scattered accumulations. The GMM-based workflow recovered additional reference pumice pixels missed by the spectral-index workflow while retaining high precision within the assessment ROIs. Following this manual initialization, the fixed models were applied to the later acquisition without image-specific retuning. These results support the use of FY-3D imagery for repeated pumice detection within the studied eruption and identify a further source of observations for monitoring volcanic hazards at sea.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Deep learning-based bi-temporal remote sensing change detection methods identify changed regions mainly by learning spatial semantic differences between images acquired at different times. However, their performance can be affected by non-semantic appearance variations, including seasonal changes, illumination fluctuations, and background texture differences, and small targets and fine-grained details may also be insufficiently represented. To address these issues, this study explores local frequency-domain differences between genuine land-cover changes and non-semantic appearance variations. Unchanged regions may exhibit relatively stable frequency-domain patterns under certain conditions, whereas genuine changes can induce shifts and reorganizations in local spectral structures. Based on this observation, we propose FPG-Net, a frequency-domain prototype-guided change detection network with adaptive feature spectrum shift. First, stable structural anchors are selected through joint constraints of keypoint matching and unsupervised clustering, and an adaptive feature spectrum shift mechanism generates local frequency-domain embeddings to characterize spectral variations. Second, an unchanged frequency-domain prototype bank is constructed according to clustering groups and updated across batches using a momentum strategy to accumulate statistical features of different unchanged backgrounds. The prototype bank generates dense frequency-domain similarity maps that provide reference information for subsequent decoding. Finally, a prototype-guided decoder adaptively modulates multi-scale spatial difference features with these frequency-domain references and integrates high-level semantics with low-level details to produce binary change maps. Experiments on public datasets demonstrate favorable performance in building change detection and scenarios involving seasonal appearance variations, indicating the effectiveness of the proposed method across different change detection conditions.

</details>

A distinct MODIS pixel-level quality-screening comparison applying an 80% daily valid-area coverage threshold yielded a descriptive difference of −0.0278. In the matched 357-glacier August–September sample, trends were −0.00597 yr−1 for the original masks and −0.00615 yr−1 after 60 m inward buffering; both 95% confidence intervals included zero. Cross-sensor observations covaried strongly (r = 0.867), although the bias of −0.073 and RMSE of 0.086 indicated substantial disagreement in magnitude. The climate-adjusted MODIS post-2020 coefficient was non-significant (−0.0190; p = 0.287), while the Landsat coefficient changed sign with trend specification. Across six excavation intervals, the construction-activity proxy showed a non-significant association with albedo anomalies (Spearman ρ = −0.543; exact p = 0.297), with direction sensitive to the reference baseline. Seasonal wind alignment described regional directional relationships but did not establish particle transport or deposition. The observations document lower later-period albedo and a negative interior-mask trend tendency without isolating a construction contribution. These findings support continuous dust control during emission-generating activities and coordinated glacier, meteorological, and deposition monitoring.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reliable building damage inventories require accurate labels and individual building masks, but aggregate scores provide limited guidance for model development. We present a four-layer evaluation protocol for optical–synthetic aperture radar (SAR) building damage instance segmentation, linking observed errors, corrections to fixed detections, mask reconstruction, and intervention tests. We evaluate the protocol on the building damage assessment dataset using veRy-hIGH-resoluTion optical and SAR imagery (BRIGHT), with 697 validation tiles, 57,951 non-crowd annotations, three-seed experiments with two model families, and retrospective hazard holdouts. Mask correction yielded larger gains in mean average precision (mAP) overall (+0.218 versus +0.125) and in the smallest building tercile (+0.190 versus +0.081). The earthquake subgroup met the pre-specified replication criterion for larger class-correction gains. Other pooled hazard results were descriptive and could vary by event or model. Disaster-balanced sampling increased the average precision for the earthquake-destroyed class by 0.0091, below the 0.015 criterion. Three primary hazard holdouts met the relative-loss criterion, whereas the secondary class-correction criterion was not met. On 120 tiles with 11,636 annotations, fitting coefficients to ground-truth masks within their annotated boxes increased reconstruction intersection over union (IoU) from 0.862 to 0.950 across two seeds under the instance-normalized protocol. However, the tested prediction-time region-of-interest decoder reduced the mean mAP by 0.0039, with decreases in all three seeds. These findings support hazard-specific model-development hypotheses and distinguish reconstruction headroom from prediction improvement. Prospective evaluation on independent disasters is needed to establish the protocol’s wider usefulness.

Abstract (英文): Most existing studies on change detection (CD) focus on binary change detection, which identifies changed regions but does not distinguish different types of land-cover change. Unsupervised multiclass change detection provides this additional information without requiring manually annotated training samples. However, the number of change categories is often unknown, and spectral variability and overlapping change responses can lead to incorrect cluster assignments or redundant categories. Using these clustering results as pseudo-labels can propagate errors into subsequent network training. Moreover, pixel-wise spectral differences alone may be insufficient to separate change categories, making spatial context necessary for their discrimination. To address these issues, we propose an adaptive pseudo-label-guided cross Mamba framework (APC-Mamba) for unsupervised multiclass remote sensing CD. An adaptive pseudo-label generation and refinement module is developed to estimate potential change categories and provide reliable supervision by generating high-quality pseudo-labels. Meanwhile, a Wavelet Mamba Residual Block (WMRB) is introduced to model low-frequency spatial context, which is then used to guide the refinement of high-frequency detail features; this helps preserve the overall structure and local boundaries in regions of change, while simultaneously suppressing interference from high-frequency noise. Furthermore, a Spatial-Channel Cross Mamba module (SCCM) is designed to facilitate feature interaction and information fusion across spatial and channel dimensions, producing more discriminative bitemporal representations. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of APC-Mamba across hyperspectral and multispectral datasets, particularly in terms of overall accuracy and Kappa coefficient.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Infrared target segmentation models trained on one spectral band generalize poorly to other bands: short-wave infrared (SWIR), mid-wave infrared (MWIR), and long-wave infrared (LWIR) images are formed by different physical mechanisms, and labeled data are often limited to a few images of a single band. We study a strict source-only setting: a model is trained on a few labeled SWIR images, without target-band images, target-band labels, or auxiliary infrared data, and is tested on unseen bands. We propose KDConFormer, a SegFormer-based framework with two training-time mechanisms. Target mask-guided contrastive learning (TMGCL) uses the ground-truth mask to sample target-centered and background-centered patches and contrasts target patches against a momentum-encoded queue. Multiscale knowledge distillation (MKD) regularizes the feature hierarchy through intra-network self-distillation, cross-scale alignment, and adaptive fusion. The deployed model needs no mask and increases the parameter count by 9.4% and the FLOPs by 7.8% relative to SegFormer. With 25 labeled SWIR images, KDConFormer reaches 57.21±1.31% mIoU on LWIR images of the real-world SDU-IRSD ship dataset, 5.59 points above the strongest competitor, with a ship IoU of 16.5% against at most 5.5% for the competing methods; on the simulated MIRAT aircraft dataset, it improves on SegFormer by 4.74 points on MWIR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Assessing ecological quality and its spatial variation is important for balancing port development and coastal ecological conservation. However, regional assessments based on the Remote Sensing Ecological Index (RSEI) may overlook localized nearshore degradation and changes in landscape structure. This study developed a temporally comparable Coastal Remote Sensing Ecological Index (C-RSEI) by incorporating an additional reflectance-based spectral indicator (SI) into the conventional framework. The index was used to assess 52 cities across China’s five major coastal port clusters in 2000, 2010, 2019, and 2024. Coastal-buffer and landscape analyses characterized spatial differences, while geographical detector analysis and city-level regression examined associations with natural, socioeconomic, and port-related factors. The results showed that: (1) Mean C-RSEI increased by 26.7% from 2000 to 2024, despite a temporary decline during 2010–2019. Improvement was weaker within 2 km of the coastline than beyond 20 km, with 21.27% of nearshore pixels classified as degraded. (2) Landscape edge density increased in 34 of the 50 cities where C-RSEI improved, indicating that spectral improvement was not consistently accompanied by lower edge density. (3) Temperature, precipitation, and slope were significantly associated with between-city C-RSEI differences in all four study years. Associations with development-related factors varied across years and model specifications. These results provide data support for identifying degraded nearshore areas and developing spatially differentiated ecological management and restoration strategies for coastal port cities.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Wide sowing windows cause rice fields to develop asynchronously, leading to temporal misalignment of their spectral trajectories. This study evaluated the applicability of a fixed stage-based temporal representation for rice mapping under such conditions. Using 54 Sentinel-2 observations from August 2024 to July 2025 and 2457 reference samples in Xai-Xai, Mozambique, full-period spectral features, full-period and stage-based spectral index features, and normalized difference vegetation index (NDVI) texture features were constructed. Samples were spatially partitioned by 3 km × 3 km grids into training, validation, and test sets. With matched input dimensionality, the stage-based feature scheme (M1) achieved numerically higher validation performance than the full-period baseline (M0), with overall accuracy increasing from 97.60% to 98.20% and rice F1 from 97.24% to 97.92%. Shifting both stage boundaries by ±15 days resulted in limited changes in validation performance. On the same 739 spatially held-out test samples, M1 achieved 97.83% overall accuracy and 97.76% rice F1, compared with 97.02% and 96.88% for M0. Among 287 rice test samples with known sowing dates, M1 increased producer’s accuracy over M0 by 1.82, 0.66, and 5.00 percentage points for early-, middle-, and late-sown rice, respectively. Paired bootstrap and McNemar tests did not establish a statistically significant advantage over the full-period baseline. These results indicate that organizing spectral-index statistics within fixed temporal stages provides a feasible temporal representation for rice identification across the observed sowing-date groups.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Soil moisture is critical to agricultural water availability, yet its spatiotemporal variability is difficult to resolve from sparse ground measurements or coarse-resolution microwave products. We investigated whether optical and thermal remote sensing, combined with meteorological and ecological predictors, can estimate soil moisture across the root zone in agricultural Alberta, Canada. Using 33 predictors, we trained support vector regression (SVR), random forest (RF), extreme gradient boosting (XGBoost), and an ensemble to estimate soil moisture at 5, 20, 50, and 100 cm. We tested satellite precipitation’s added value and examined how predictor importance varied with depth. Model performance was broadly similar (R2 = 0.18–0.59), with the ensemble the most consistent across depths. SVR best reproduced the observed variability (highest KGE) but showed greater bias than the tree-based models. Estimates were more accurate at 5–50 cm and deteriorated substantially at 100 cm. Adding precipitation increased ensemble R2 by 6.7–29.9%, although its effect differed among individual models. Predictor importance also changed with depth; precipitation was most influential near the surface, whereas subregion and vegetation indices gained importance deeper in the profile. These results show both the potential and the depth-dependent limits of combining optical–thermal observations and satellite precipitation to estimate agricultural soil moisture where direct observations are sparse.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) carrying a visible and a thermal camera are used as remote sensing platforms, where visual object tracking is a key perception primitive. The two cameras are independent sensing pipelines rather than synchronised views of one scene: they are exposed for different durations, read out at different rates, with their outputs encoded at different resolutions and delivered over a shared link, so the observations reaching fusion are not equally old. We formalise this regime as heterogeneous sensor latency and show that it defeats fusion rules built for synchronised inputs: a stale observation is not a disagreeing one, so agreement-based cross-modal gating has nothing to correct. We therefore weight each modality’s output box by the reciprocal of its own extrapolation uncertainty, growing with that modality’s age and speed. The rule trains nothing: both trackers stay frozen, and only their boxes are combined, so it adds zero parameters and negligible latency. On the three aligned or near-aligned RGB-T (visible–thermal) benchmarks on which a box-level rule is defined, as the inter-modality delay grows from zero to six frames, equal-weight fusion degrades by 5.6–9.4 area-under-curve points. Over the same interval, the proposed rule loses at most 2.7 points, a loss that is not statistically resolved from zero. Ablations confirm that both the delay-aware weighting and the per-modality age model are necessary.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Dryland vegetation assessments need to consider both recovery-related dynamics and low vegetation activity under water stress. We assessed stable vegetation in Xinjiang using lag-1 temporal autocorrelation (AR1) of kernel normalized difference vegetation index (kNDVI) residuals, explainable machine learning, and projections from five climate models under three Shared Socioeconomic Pathway (SSP) scenarios. AR1 measures residual temporal persistence and serves as an indirect recovery-related proxy. Projected conditional risk used bias-corrected vapor pressure deficit (VPD), historical VPD–vegetation dependence, and risk-level Bayesian model averaging. Future AR1 used an equal-weight ensemble of gross primary productivity (GPP) corrected with additive quantile delta mapping (QDM). For the 2001–2023 historical study period, AR1 decreased in 57.68% of pixels with valid trends, whereas 15.89% of pixels with valid significance tests showed significant decreases. Elevation and precipitation variables dominated pooled model contributions, with non-monotonic responses. Conditional risk increased in 77.94–79.81% of grid cells across six scenario–period combinations for 2021–2060 and 2061–2100. Under the QDM ensemble workflow, increasing risk and increasing AR1 formed the largest joint class, covering 47.33–66.54% of analyzed area. Its later-period area fraction declined under SSP126 and increased under SSP370 and SSP585. However, historical kNDVI- and GPP-based trends were only partly concordant, and future AR1 directions and joint classes were sensitive to correction and model choice. GPP distribution correction did not improve out-of-period AR1 reconstruction. These conditional scenario assessments support monitoring priorities while retaining uncertainty in recovery interpretation and future local patterns.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Three-dimensional building models support urban planning, autonomous driving simulation, and smart city applications. Among the Levels of Detail (LoD), LoD2 offers a trade-off between geometric fidelity and computational cost. We present a structured review of traditional and deep learning-based methods for LoD2 building reconstruction, across the existing LoD2.0 to LoD2.3 sub-levels. The review covers methods built on active and passive sensing, from light detection and ranging (LiDAR) and imagery to multi-source data fusion. We introduce a comparative framework based on geometric accuracy, scalability, automation, robustness to noise and occlusions, and adaptability, with a scoring rubric for each criterion. Through a qualitative synthesis of reported results, we identify the strengths and limitations of existing methods, the open research gaps, and the role of deep learning and hybrid approaches in scalable urban reconstruction. We offer this review as a structured starting point for researchers and practitioners who choose LoD2 building modeling methods.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sub-pixel fire characterization is essential for quantifying wildfire energetics, combustion phase dynamics, and their atmospheric impacts from satellite observations. This study presents a series of Observing System Simulation Experiments (OSSEs) to systematically evaluate the influence of temperature phase complexity, channel selection, and intra-phase temperature heterogeneity on the performance of a Bayesian retrieval framework. Using synthetic observations derived from a multi-phase radiance model, we assess retrieval skill across key fire properties—including Fire Radiative Power (FRP), visible energy fraction (VEF), and flaming heat flux—under realistic sensor constraints. Results show that a bi-phasic fire model provides a strong balance between accuracy and observational feasibility, outperforming conventional approaches such as Wooster’s FRP regression, particularly in low-intensity fire scenarios. FRP is the retrieved quantity most resilient to model and observational uncertainties, while VEF and heat flux estimates are more sensitive to both fire phase partitioning and spectral coverage. Shortwave and mid-infrared bands, especially the day–night band (DNB) and 2.25 μ m channels, are shown to offer the greatest constraint on retrieval accuracy. We further analyze the impact of intra-phase temperature variability and propose practical filtering strategies for identifying low-confidence cases, such as smoldering-dominated fires. The theoretical framework is applicable to multi-channel fire observations more generally, while its companion Part II implementation focuses specifically on nighttime VIIRS observations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The imperative for sustainable agriculture has led to the promotion of conservation tillage practices, which are critical for maintaining soil health and reducing negative environmental impacts. However, the ability to verify the adoption of these practices at a regional scale and with minimal costs remains a challenge, particularly in regions with diverse crop systems. This study presents a satellite-imagery-based random forest classifier that integrates crop class information and a customized importance weighting technique for field-level, regional-scale mapping of tillage practices in crop-diverse regions. We use eastern Washington State, U.S., with multiple grains, legumes, and canola as a case study. Using 577 field-level ground-truth data points across two counties, the tillage classifier achieved a median overall accuracy of 84%, representing a 15 percentage point improvement over the baseline median overall accuracy of 69%, driven by the inclusion of crop class and the application of importance weighting. Specific crop classes, including grains, legumes, and canola, showed accuracy improvements of 6, 42, and 13 percentage points, respectively. While the model showed promising results, limitations such as overprediction of no-tillage in non-grain crop classes were identified, pointing to the need for further refinement with additional data. The model was applied to map tillage practices across dryland fields in eastern Washington State for the years 2012, 2017, and 2022, and the predictions were generally congruent with the USDA Census of Agriculture county-level statistics. This research offers a scalable tool for the verification of conservation tillage practice adoption, supporting both regional and global monitoring efforts for sustainable agricultural practices.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Coastal algal bloom monitoring requires frequent, spatially detailed, and globally consistent observations provided by Landsat-8/9 and Sentinel-2 A/B/C. Together, these missions provide more than a decade of high-resolution multispectral imagery with near-global coverage every 2–3 days, enabling detection of fragmented bloom structures not resolvable by coarse ocean-color sensors. However, their use in aquatic environments remains challenging due to limited spectral coverage and the lack of harmonized reflectance products. As an alternative to traditional bio-optical methods, deep learning-based image classification provides a data-driven approach that can overcome many of these limitations. This study presents implementation and systematic evaluation of vision transformer-based coastal algal bloom mapping using 30-m Landsat-Sentinel-2 images. A globally distributed algal bloom patch dataset (24,265 training and 12,255 validation patches, each 30 m 256 × 256 pixels) was generated in coastal algal bloom-prone hotspots worldwide. Four transformer-based architectures, Vanilla Vision Transformer, Swin Transformer, SegFormer, and Masked Autoencoders, were implemented and compared against a standard convolutional baseline model (ResUNet) for fine-scale bloom detection. The models were evaluated across diverse optical water types, atmospheric states, and surface environments. Under strict pixel-wise evaluation, F1-scores ranged from 0.50 (Vanilla ViT) to 0.58 (ResUNet), with the Swin Transformer following closely at 0.57. When accounting for a one-pixel error margin, performance improved significantly, ranging from 0.62 for MAE (Prithvi) to 0.73 for the Swin Transformer, highlighting architecture-specific strengths and limitations across scenarios. The models did not produce glint-induced false positives, which are common to fixed-threshold spectral indices under thin cloud cover and specular reflection, but algae obscured by severe glint frequently went undetected. Additionally, a comparison with MODIS-derived products highlighted the benefits and limitations of a higher spatial resolution algal bloom product in detecting fragmented and irregularly affected bloom areas. These results identify the conditions under which each architecture is suited for 30-m floating algal bloom mapping and provide a benchmark and reference dataset for future operational development.

</details>
